# TempestOS v0.26.0 — Owner test pack

|  |  |
|---|---|
| Tester | Steven Kreczman |
| Branch to test | `release/v0.26.0`, the final merge commit |
| Written against | `release/v0.26.0` at `08315a20` (2026-10-27). It holds the C-2 sheet standard, the 27 reference diagrams and the owner-decision builds of DC-21. |
| Version file | `0.26.0` |
| State | Ready for owner testing. Not released. Not tagged. Not merged to `main` (DA-01). |
| Platform | Windows 10 or 11, PowerShell |
| What it replaces | The pack for Addenda A and B. Its tests are all in this file. Appendix E maps every old section to its new place. |

## How to use this pack

This is the one pack for the whole v0.26.0 line. It covers the base release, Addendum A, Addendum B and the Addendum C features. It has eight parts. Work through them in this order.

| Part | What you do | Why it comes here |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Build and install | Nothing else works until the build runs on your machine. |
| 2 | Enter reference records from the Track P templates, in priority order | Some calculators block without records. Enter them before you test those calculators. |
| 3 | Walk through the demo project | The demo shows every chain working. You learn the screens before you test. |
| 4 | Test the highest-liability calculators | A wrong pass here can hurt a person. Test these while you are fresh. |
| 5 | Test the chains | A chain links calculators. Test the links after you have tested the calculators. |
| 6 | Test the remaining calculators | These have a lower liability. They still need a test. |
| 7 | Run the security spot checks | They go last, so they do not disturb the earlier results. |
| 8 | Fill in the sign-off sheet | It records your verdict for each part. |

**Results.** Each step has a Result box. Write `P` for pass, `F` for fail, `B` for blocked or `N` for not run. Add a short note. Blocked means you could not do the step. Say why. Record every fail before you move on.

**Two ways to do a step.** Every step works by hand. Most steps also work in the Guided test. By hand, you follow the step in the app and write the result in this file. In the Guided test, you open Engineering → Modules → Calculations → **Guided test** and start a run. You pick the step by its guided id. The panel opens the worksheet with the inputs and shows the expected outputs. You press **Compare with the form**, tick a result, add a note and press **Record result**. At the end you finish the run and save the results sheet. A guided run never changes a calculation or its Evidence.

**What the Guided test covers.** It has 110 steps. There are 87 worked examples. There are also 6 smoke steps and 4 linked chains. There are 4 import and record steps, 6 lifting steps and 3 legacy steps. The Guided column of each table names the guided id. A step with the word *none* in that column has no guided step. You do it by hand. The sign-off sheet in Part 8 counts the steps that have a guided id.

**Where the numbers are.** This pack does not copy the expected numbers of the worked examples. The file `v0.26.0-test-values.md` holds them. It is generated from the worked examples, so it follows them. A step here names the set to use. You read the numbers from the test values file. If a template changes, the test values file is generated again and follows it.

**The tolerance rule.** A number passes when it is close enough to the expected value. Close enough means a difference of no more than half a unit in the last digit. Use the last digit of the less precise of the two numbers. The form shows four or five significant figures. It shows a whole number exactly. The governing bar shows the utilisation to two decimals. A text, such as PASS or FAIL, must be the same. A different unit is for you to judge. `bar`, `bar a` and `bar g` are three different pressure bases.

**7 sets are for your review.** For 7 of the 87 worked examples the form cannot settle the printed value. Six print a hand-calculated or published value that differs from the form's digits. One prints a pressure in a bare `bar`, which names no basis. The test values file marks each one **For your review**. The Guided test leaves them to you and ticks nothing. Decide each one yourself. Do not log a fault when the difference is inside the example's own tolerance (1 % by default). The 7 are listed at the top of the test values file. Part 4 and Part 6 mark them in the Expect column.

**Long-form scripts.** Many calculators also have a long-form script from the earlier packs. Each has the id `T-xx`. Part 4 and Part 6 keep them under their calculator. They test the new-sheet reading and the variation steps. They were written before the Addendum C fixes. On 2026-10-27 each was run again on the release head, and every number, name and message in it now comes from that run. Appendix E shows where each old script went.

**Standards.** This pack cites standards by clause number and title only. It holds no standards text and no table values. Every table value you enter comes from your own copy.

**Other documents.** `v0.26.0-runbook.md` is the short route through the same tests. `v0.26.0-known-defects.md` lists the defects already found. Read its top section before you log a fault. `v0.26.0-test-values.md` holds the numbers. `docs/handover/v0.26.0-handover.md` holds the decisions that need you. **Track P** is the name of the parallel verification work. Its files are in `docs/verification/`.

## Part 1 — Build and install

Part 1 gets a clean build running on your machine. It takes about an hour, most of it unattended. The Guided test needs a signed-in person. Part 1 therefore includes your first sign-in. It ends with the start of a guided run.

### 1.1 What you need

- Windows 10 or 11 on x64, Git and the .NET SDK. `global.json` pins SDK `10.0.302` and allows any later 10.0.3xx feature band.
- No workload, Visual Studio, Node, Docker, database or licence file.
- The network, once, for `dotnet restore`.
- Python 3.10 or later, if you want the optional checks in section 1.5.
- A dedicated data folder. This pack uses `$HOME\TempestOS-Test-Data` so the test never touches other data.

### 1.2 Build from a clean clone

```powershell
# Step 1: check the SDK
dotnet --list-sdks                         # expect a 10.0.3xx line

# Step 2: clean clone of the release branch (keep it out of any other working tree)
cd $HOME
git clone --branch release/v0.26.0 https://github.com/kreczmans-creator/TempestOS-Core.git TempestOS-Core-0260
cd TempestOS-Core-0260
git log -1 --format="%h %s"                # expect the final merge on release/v0.26.0 (08315a20 or later)
Get-Content VERSION                        # expect 0.26.0

# Step 3: locked restore, as CI does (fails if a lock file does not match)
dotnet restore src/TempestOS.slnx --locked-mode

# Step 4: release build with warnings as errors, as CI does
dotnet build src/TempestOS.slnx --configuration Release --no-restore -p:TreatWarningsAsErrors=true
```

| Step | Action | Expect | Guided | Result |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1.2-1 | Run `dotnet --list-sdks`. | A line that starts with `10.0.3`. | none |  |
| 1.2-2 | Clone the branch. Read the last commit and the `VERSION` file. | The commit is `08315a20` or a later merge. `VERSION` reads `0.26.0`. | none |  |
| 1.2-3 | Restore in locked mode. | It succeeds. A lock-file mismatch is a fault. | none |  |
| 1.2-4 | Build in Release with warnings as errors. | `Build succeeded.` with 0 warnings and 0 errors. | none |  |

### 1.3 Run the automated tests

The Calc suites take a few minutes. The Desktop suite takes about 20 minutes with the timeout factor. Run them before you touch the app.

```powershell
foreach ($p in 'Engine','Sheets','Library','Calculators','FluidPower','Import') {
  dotnet test "tests/Tempest.Calc.Tests.$p" --configuration Release --no-build
}
dotnet test tests/Tempest.Core.Tests --configuration Release --no-build
$env:TEMPEST_TEST_TIMEOUT_FACTOR = '3'
dotnet test tests/Tempest.Desktop.Tests --configuration Release --no-build
```

The expected counts are in the table in section 2 of `v0.26.0-runbook.md`. They are kept in one place so they change in one place. A timing test can fail on a busy machine. Run it again alone. If it passes alone, note it and go on. Any other failure is a fault.

| Step | Action | Expect | Guided | Result |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1.3-1 | Run the six Calc suites. | Every suite passes. The Library suite has one skipped test (D-32). | none |  |
| 1.3-2 | Run the Core suite. | It passes. It has 15 skipped tests. | none |  |
| 1.3-3 | Run the Desktop suite with the timeout factor. | It passes. It has 1 skipped test. A single timing failure that passes alone is a note, not a fault. | none |  |

### 1.4 Start the app on a clean data folder

```powershell
# Close any running instance first (one instance per data folder)
Get-Process Tempest.Desktop, Tempest.Harness -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue | Stop-Process

# A dedicated data folder, so the test never touches other data
$root = "$HOME\TempestOS-Test-Data"
dotnet run --project src/Tempest.Desktop/Tempest.Desktop.csproj --configuration Release --no-build -- --persistence-root $root
```

- To start again from nothing, close the app and delete `$root`.
- The log is `$root\logs\tempest-<yyyyMMdd>.log`. The date in the name is UTC.
- No CI step launches the windowed app on Windows. Your launch is the first Windows run of this build. Report window problems apart from calculator results.
- Create or open a project (rail: **Projects**) before the linking, pack and record steps. Links, packs and the saved list work inside one project.

| Step | Action | Expect | Guided | Result |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1.4-1 | Start the app with the command above. | The window opens. The title bar reads `TempestOS 0.26.0 (<commit>)`. The commit is the one from step 1.2-2. | none |  |
| 1.4-2 | Open **Settings** and pick **Switch person…**. | It lists at least one person. Sign in as one. The Guided test and most governance steps need a signed-in person. If it lists nobody, mark every step that needs two people `B`. | none |  |

```powershell
python scripts/check-licences.py
python scripts/generate-guided-test-script.py --check
python scripts/generate-verification-snapshot.py --check
```

| Step | Action | Expect | Guided | Result |
|---|---|---|---|---|

The smoke test proves the app can open, run, save, check and issue one calculation. It uses the accumulator sheet. The Guided test has one step for each line.

| Step | Action | Expect | Guided | Result |

The sets of `fp.accumulator` in the test values show the expected `V0`. This is one of the 7 sets for your review. | `smoke-3` |  |
| 1.6-4 | Type `Smoke test` in **Calculation name**. Press **Save as calculation**. | The calculation appears in **Saved calculations**. | `smoke-4` |  |

If the app does not start, or the catalogue is empty, stop and record it.

|---|---|---|---|---|

### 1.8 What changed since the earlier scripts were written

The long-form scripts in Parts 4 to 6 come from the earlier packs. Three changes since then affect how you read them.

1. **The older Calculators surface is gone** (ADR-0167, Amendment 1). Worksheets replaced it. Every stored legacy run opens as a read-only archive worksheet. A step that opens Modules → Calculations → Calculators cannot run as written. For the B4 cross-check, compare B4's `U_1` with the `bs.plane-wall` worksheet. The automated `BoltShearReconciliationTests` cover BC1 and BC2.
2. **Two more worksheets sit behind the lifting flag.** `mech.lifting-lug` is gated like S8 (DI-04). With the flag off the catalogue lists 52 worksheets. With it on, 54.
3. **Templates moved up versions.** C-1 fixed defects in 35 templates. A script heading shows the version it was written for. The test values file shows the current version. A calculation saved on an older version opens as a new sheet on the current one.

### 1.9 The worksheet form in one place

- **Where the calculators are.** Rail: Engineering. Then Modules → Calculations → Worksheets. The same worksheets sit under Disciplines → (discipline) → Worksheets. Ctrl+K finds each by name.
- **Left column.** The catalogue, then **Saved calculations** and **Calculation packs**. Saved calculations lists the open project's calculations as `name · template · rev · status`. A calculation the preparer checked alone shows ` · SELF-CHECKED` after the status (DA-12). A stale one ends in an amber `STALE`. It has an **Open** button.
- **Calculation packs** makes a pack from a template. It lists the packs and their members. It has **Open pack member**, **Check pack**, **Reopen pack** and **Issue pack**.
- **Right column.** Each input is a row with a value, a unit picker, a source picker and a source reference. The *Recalculating…* marker shows while the sheet updates.
- Below the inputs is the governing bar. It reads PASS or FAIL with the utilisation, or `Blocked — …`. Then come the results, the checks, the notices and **Show working**.
- A stale calculation shows an orange `Stale — …` banner. A draft offers **Update linked inputs**. A checked one offers **Update to revision n**.
- **Buttons.** **Save as calculation** reads **Save changes** once saved. The others are **Record check**, **Issue**, **Revise**, **Export inputs (CSV)** and **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)**.
- A saved draft also shows **Link an input to another calculation**. If its template declares record kinds, it shows the **Reference records** panel.
- **Tables.** Table inputs have no row editor in this release (D-37). They arrive by **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)** from the clipboard. An import re-seeds the whole form. Import first, then type the single values.
- **Open over unsaved edits.** The first press of **Open** only says `The form has unsaved changes. Save them first, or press Open again to discard them and open '<name>'.` A second press for the same calculation discards them.
- Table cells carry a unit (`"Unit":"mm"`), a text (`"Text"`) or a flag (`"Flag"`). A blank cell is the same as no cell.

## Part 2 — Record entry from the Track P templates

Some calculators block until you give them reference records. Others run on a default and warn. No record that comes from a standard ships with the product. You enter each one from your own licensed copy. Track P made 82 header-only CSV templates in `docs\verification\record-templates`. They are ranked by liability. Track S added 8 more, 83 to 90, in `docs\calcs\record-templates` (DC-10). The C-3 records screen reads them. This part tells you which to enter first, what each one unlocks and how to import it.

Do this part before Parts 4 to 6. Enter as many records as your copies allow. A step that needs a record you do not hold is `B`. Say which record is missing.

### 2.1 The rules for records

- **Nothing ships.** The source, the seeds, the fixtures and the known-answer cases hold no standards table. The list below says what that covers.
  - BS 7671 and IET tables, BS EN 60204-1 tables, IEC 60364 tables, and CIBSE and MCS design data.
  - Eurocode National Annex tables.
  - SKF and Schaeffler diagrams, Peterson charts and Roark tables.
  - EN 10270, EN 13906, ISO 13788 and BS 5422 tables.
  - ASME B31.3, ASME BTH-1 and EN 13155 text and tables.
  - AS568 and ISO 3601 size tables, and Parker charts.
- **The only data that ships** is four generic seed materials for the material library (W3). They are marked *indicative*.
- **A record holds** its kind, title, revision and source title. It holds the edition and amendment, the table or clause, and the licence holder. It also holds who entered it, a licensed flag, its origin and its values.
- **A record is never edited.** A change saves the next revision. A calculation pins the revision it used. A record revised after use makes the calculation stale. You apply the record again to mend it.
- **Licensed source.** Tick it for values copied from a standard or guide that you hold under licence. The records screen ticks it for you. Untick it only when the owner of the source allows copies. Whether a manufacturer's datasheet needs it depends on the maker's terms. That is your call (Appendix C).
- **The export lock never changes.** A licensed value never leaves the program in an export. Section 2.8 tests it.

### 2.2 How to import a template on the records screen

The records screen is under Engineering → Modules → Calculations → **Reference records**. The left pane lists every record kind the calculators declare, with a count. A `!` marks a kind that a calculator cannot do without and that has no record yet. The right pane shows the records of the kind you pick. It never shows the values of a licensed record.

The steps below enter one template. Repeat them for each row of the table in section 2.3, from the top. The Guided test has one step for the whole screen: `import-13`. Use it to record your result for section 2.5.

| Step | Action | Expect | Guided | Result |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2.2-1 | Open **Reference records**. Pick the record type, then its **Record template**. The list shows the file number and name, for example `04 bs7671.ampacity (cable.ampacity)`. | The screen lists the type and the template. If two templates fill one kind (04 and 10), pick the one that matches your source. | `import-13` |  |
| 2.2-2 | Make your file. Copy the header-only file `docs\verification\record-templates\NN-name.csv`. Add one row for each line of your table. Keep the header and its units. Save as UTF-8. | The file has the template's header row and your rows. No other column is needed. | `import-13` |  |
| 2.2-3 | Fill the provenance. **Title, Source title, Edition, Table or clause and Licence holder** are required. The **Amendment** may be blank only if your copy has none. Or press **Load provenance file…** and pick Track P's `_provenance.csv`. | **Entered by** is you and cannot be typed. **Licensed source (never exported)** is ticked. **Origin** reads `UserEntered` unless the values are derived or a manufacturer's own example. | `import-13` |  |
| 2.2-4 | Press **Choose CSV file…**. Choose the **Number format** (point or comma) to match the file. It is never guessed. | The preview shows the file name and its SHA-256. It lists every column with where it goes and whether its unit is ok. It lists every row with its errors and warnings by line. | `import-13` |  |
| 2.2-5 | Fix every error. A header that is not in the template is an error until you press **Map column…** or leave it out. | The summary reads `Ready: n record(s), … table row(s)`. **Import file** is enabled only when no error is left. | `import-13` |  |
| 2.2-6 | Press **Import file**. | The status line reads `Saved '<title>' as a reference record (rev 1, licensed: its values never leave in an export).` The list shows the record as `LICENSED (never exported)` with its symbols and row counts, and no numbers. | `import-13` |  |
| 2.2-7 | Open a worksheet that reads the kind. Press **Apply record** on a saved draft. | The inputs take the values and cite the record. The missing-records panel clears without a refresh. | `import-13` |  |
| 2.2-8 | To revise a record, import a file with the same title. Tick **Save as the next revision when the title exists**. | It saves revision 2. A calculation that used revision 1 goes stale until you apply the record again. | `import-13` |  |

You can type a table instead of using a file. The grid has one column for each template column. A unit of another kind (volts for amps) shows `WRONG unit` at once. A unit of the same kind (mA for A) is kept as typed. Use **Add row**, **Remove last row**, **Clear grid**, then **Save typed rows**.

### 2.3 The templates in priority order

Track P ranked the templates by calculator liability, then by dependency. Electrical safety comes first (E4, E5). The Eurocode factors for S4, S5, S6 and S7 follow. Then come the accumulator, the fluid and the shaft records. The heating and condensation records come last. The file number is a fixed id, not a rank. Enter the rows from the top.

**Needed by** lists the calculators that read the kind. **Required** means the calculator is blocked, or cannot judge, without a record (C-3, `RecordRequirements`). **Conditional** means it is needed for some set-ups. **Optional** means an alternative or an extra. **Recommended** means the sheet runs on a default and warns until you apply a record.

| Order | File and kind | Needed by | What it unlocks | Import | Done |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 01 | `01` conductor.material | E5, E3, E7 | Removes the default warning on E5, E3, E7. Smallest record. Three built calculators need it. | yes |  |
| 02 | `02` device.characteristic | E5 (conditional) | Needed for a fuse or an MCCB; MCBs of type B, C and D need no curve. E5 needs it for every fuse and breaker that is not a standard miniature breaker. | yes |  |
| 03 | `47` device.ratings | **E4** required | E4 cannot pick a protective device without the preferred ratings of its range. | yes |  |
| 04 | `03` bs7671.max-zs | E5 (optional) | E5 checks Z_s against your tabulated maximum (the table `Z_s_max_table`), as well as against the derived Z_s,max it always checks. Built under DC-10. | yes |  |
| 05 | `04` bs7671.ampacity (`cable.ampacity`) | **E4** required, E3 | E4 cannot size a conductor without the current-carrying capacities for the installation method. | yes |  |
| 06 | `05` bs7671.voltage-drop | E3 (optional) | An alternative to typing the mV/A/m figure on the mV/A/m route (the table `mVAm_table`). Built under DC-10. | yes |  |
| 07 | `06` bs7671.factors-ambient (`cable.correction-factors`) | E4 | Removes the default warning on E4. A wrong factor can overrate a cable. E4 multiplies the capacity by it. | yes |  |
| 08 | `07` bs7671.factors-grouping (`cable.correction-factors`) | E4 | Removes the default warning on E4. Grouping is the factor most often missed. E4 only warns when C_g is 1 for a group. | yes |  |
| 09 | `08` bs7671.factors-insulation (`cable.correction-factors`) | E4 | Removes the default warning on E4. Insulated walls cut cable capacity sharply. E4 needs the factor for those routes. | yes |  |
| 10 | `09` bs7671.factors-protection | E4 | Do not fill. No calculator reads it. | not imported |  |
| 11 | `10` en60204.ampacity (`cable.ampacity`) | **E4** required | E4 cannot size a conductor without the current-carrying capacities for the installation method. | yes |  |
| 12 | `11` en60204.factors (`cable.correction-factors`) | E4 | Removes the default warning on E4. It completes the machine wiring set. E4 applies both factors as a product. | yes |  |
| 13 | `12` steel.strengths | S4, S6, S8 | Removes the default warning on S4, S6, S8. Three structural calculators read it. S7 takes f_y from a W3 material instead. Strength sets every utilisation. | yes |  |
| 14 | `13` ec3.partial-factors (`ec3-1-1.national-annex`) | S6, S8 | Removes the default warning on S6, S8. S6 divides the plate resistance by gamma_M0. A wrong factor shifts every result. | yes |  |
| 15 | `14` ec3.national-annex | S4, S6, S7, S8 | Removes the default warning on S4, S6, S7, S8. It governs bolt and weld resistance in three calculators. S6 reads gamma_M2. S7 reads gamma_M1. | yes |  |
| 16 | `15` en1990.factors | **S5** required, S8, S6 | S5 blocks without the psi factors; the partial factors come from the same record. | yes |  |
| 17 | `18` en1992.factors (`ec2.national-annex`) | S6 | Removes the default warning on S6. The base plate bears on concrete. These factors set the concrete design strength. | yes |  |

77 of Track P's 82 templates import. One fills a kind that stays proposed (48 surface resistances, DC-27). The screen refuses it and gives the reason in one sentence. Four are Track P's own *do not fill* files (09, 35, 36, 37). DC-10 built six of Track P's proposed kinds (03, 05, 19, 42, 43, 44). Track S added templates 83 to 90 in `docs\calcs\record-templates` for the eight kinds that had none: `motor.rating`, `cable.impedance`, `motor.start`, `starter.limits`, `transformer.data` and the three lifting kinds. So every kind a calculator declares now has a template. The three lifting templates are listed only while the Lifting flag is on. Templates 83 to 90 take the same steps as the others.

Templates 62 to 65 are the material library. Template 62 makes one material for each row. Templates 63, 64 and 65 add a table to a material. The material must already be in the library. The table is saved as its next revision. Section 2.9 tests the library.

Every other kind that a worksheet lists is **Recommended**. The sheet opens on a default for it and warns `a tabulated value not taken from a reference record`. Sections 2.10 and 2.11 give the full list by calculator.

### 2.5 Tests for the records screen and the CSV import

**What the checks are.** Units are checked per column. A value outside what **every** calculator reading the kind accepts is an error; one that only some calculators refuse is a warning naming them. Each template's notes add their own rules: required cells, positive values, duplicates, and tables that must run one way (an ambient factor falls as the temperature rises; a grouping factor does not rise as circuits increase; a time falls as the current rises; the rows of a size list rise). Warnings (an unusual value) never stop an import. The notes' wording is followed: "must fall" is strict, "must not rise" is not.

**Which templates import.** 77 of Track P's 82 do, and the 8 Track S templates 83 to 90 (the lifting three only while the flag is on). One (48 surface resistances) fills a kind that stays proposed: the screen refuses it and says why. Four (09, 35, 36, 37) are Track P's own "do not fill" files. Templates 62 to 65 are the material library: 62 makes one material per row; 63 (temperature tables), 64 (secant curve) and 65 (friction pairs) add a table to a material already in the library, as its next revision.

**The lock is unchanged.** Nothing on the screen exports a record. A record imported as licensed is refused by the record export, is withheld from a calculation's CSV export, and its rows are withheld from an issued sheet exactly as in section 2.7. The preview shows what you typed or imported before it is saved; after that the screen shows symbols and row counts, not values.

**Test it.** The numbers below are invented; do not use them for a design.

| # | Action | Expected | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| R20 | Open **Conductor sizing and overload coordination** (a new sheet). | A panel `2 required records are missing`, with `Cable current-carrying capacities` and `Preferred device ratings`, each with a **Fill** link, and `Cable correction factors` below as recommended. | |
| R21 | Press **Fill** on `Cable current-carrying capacities`. | The records screen opens on that type, template `04 bs7671.ampacity`, **Entered by** is you, **Licensed source** is ticked. | |

The summary says `Nothing will be saved: 1 error(s) must be fixed first` and the error says `The provenance needs its source title.` **Import file** is disabled. | |

| R24 | Change the third row to `4,,x` and choose the file again. | `Error · Line 4, I_t: … 'x' is not a number.` **Import file** is disabled. Nothing is in the list of records. | |

### 2.6 Enter a record from a worksheet

1. Open the worksheet, import any table inputs, type the single values, and **Save as calculation**. The records panel shows only for a saved draft whose template declares record kinds.
2. In the panel pick the record kind. Fill Title, Source title, Edition and amendment, Table or clause and Licence holder. All five are required. Tick **Licensed source (never exported)** where it applies.
3. Press **Save inputs as record**. The record takes the form's current values for that kind's symbols, and the inputs now cite it.
4. On another draft of a template that uses the same kind, pick the record under **Take values from** and press **Apply record**. The inputs take the values and cite the record as their source, with the edition as the revision.

The record form always saves a new record at revision 1. For a table of many rows, or the next revision of a record you already have, use the records screen (section 2.2): it takes a typed grid or a CSV and can save the next revision.

### 2.7 How the licence lock behaves

Grounded in `CalcInstanceService.Records.cs`, `CalcCsv.cs`, `CalcInputJson.cs`, `CalcSheetBuilder.cs` and their tests.

| Path | Behaviour |
|---|---|
| **Export inputs (CSV)** (worksheet) | Passes through `WithholdLicensedAsync`. An input whose source is a licensed record keeps its symbol and its record pin, loses its value, and reads `record:<id>@<rev> · licensed value withheld`. Unlicensed values export. A table is exported cell by cell with each cell's unit and source (so table-cell links round-trip); a table pinned to a licensed record is written as its own row only, with no rows. |
| **CSV or JSON re-import of a withheld value** | A withheld scalar is **blocked**: `<symbol>: this value was withheld on export (licensed record) and is not in the file; apply the record again, or enter the value from your own copy.` (CSV adds `Row <n>: ` in front.) Nothing is imported until you apply the record again or enter the value. A withheld table comes back empty with a warning, and the validator blocks the run. It never comes back as the template default. |
| `ExportRecordAsync` (the record itself) | Refuses a licensed record with: `Record '<title>' holds values from a licensed source (<source>, <edition>); its values are never exported.` Not reachable from the Desktop; tests only. An unlicensed record exports as CSV with its citation in one quoted cell. |
| Issued sheet, pack sheet, preview and PDF | Rows of a table pinned to a licensed record are withheld: the sheet prints the pin, the record's citation and `rows withheld (licensed record); the values used appear in the working`. A scalar from a licensed record is the value used and stays. That is the user's own calculation, so issuing is allowed. A pin to a revision that does not exist is judged by the record's latest revision. |
| Revision of a licensed record | Stays licensed. A revision cannot clear the flag. |
| A record saved from inputs that cite a licensed record | Becomes licensed, so licensed values cannot be laundered into an exportable record. |
| Calc-pack export, record attachments | No such export path exists today. Any new one must use the same guard (DA-06). |
| Known limit (DA-18) | The lock follows the **provenance**, not the numbers. If you retype a licensed value with another source, it exports. |

Test it with T-REC (section 2.8).

Section 2.8 tests the lock with T-REC. Part 7 repeats the main checks as security spot checks.

### 2.8 T-REC: reference records and the licence lock

Guided: `import-w3` covers T-REC and T-W3 together. The steps below are the original script, kept whole. R1 to R11 test the mechanism on M5. R12 to R17 set up E4 from your own copies.

Part 1 tests the mechanism on M5, because M5 does not demand an independent check, so you can check and issue alone. The numbers are the repository's test values, not the SKF figures; the lock does not care. Part 2 sets up the E4 records from your own copies.

**What the lock does (§A5, DA-23).** A value taken from a record marked *Licensed source* never leaves the program as a value:

- The CSV export (**Export inputs (CSV)**) writes the symbol and the record pin and no value: the reference reads `record:<32 hex digits>@<rev> · licensed value withheld`. A licensed table is written as its own row only, with no rows.
- A CSV or JSON re-import of a withheld scalar is **blocked** until the record is applied again. A withheld table comes back empty with a warning, and the sheet is blocked.
- An issued sheet, a pack sheet and the preview withhold the **rows** of a table pinned to a licensed record. They print the record pin, its citation and `rows withheld (licensed record); the values used appear in the working`. A scalar from a licensed record is the value used and stays on the sheet.
- The lock follows the provenance, not the numbers (DA-18): if you retype a licensed value with another source, it exports.
- The lock belongs to the record, not to one revision (SEC-B-02): an input pinned to a record is withheld if any revision of that record is licensed.

| # | Action | Expected | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| R1 | Open a project. Open **Rolling bearing life (ISO 281)**. Import the `skf-6206-pump` JSON (as in T-00 step 1). Name it `T-REC bearing`. **Save as calculation**. | `Imported 15 input(s).` then saved. A **Reference records** panel appears (it shows only for a saved draft whose template declares record kinds). | |
| R2 | Pick the kind `Bearing load ratings`. Fill Title `T-REC ratings`, Source title, **Edition and amendment**, **Table or clause**, **Licence holder** (any text; all five are required). Tick **Licensed source (never exported)**. Press **Save inputs as record**. | Status `Saved 'T-REC ratings' as a reference record (rev 1, licensed: its values never leave in an export) and applied it.` | |

### 2.9 T-W3: the material library

Guided: `import-w3`. The material library holds materials as records of kind `material.properties`. Template 62 fills it from a CSV file.

**What W3 is.** A material is a reference record of kind `material.properties`. It holds E, G, ν, ρ, α, λ, f_y, f_u, the R_m(d) curve coefficients, permissible strains, secant-modulus points and friction pairs, and temperature tables read linearly (never extrapolated). Each value carries its own source, edition, page or table, licence class, who entered it, when, and an evidence SHA-256. A calculation pins the material's revision. **W3 has no form panel of its own.** The existing **Reference records** panel applies a material to any template that maps an input to a material property. The library ships four **indicative** seed materials: carbon steel (generic), austenitic stainless steel (generic), aluminium alloy (generic) and copper (generic). Each carries a few rounded textbook figures for a class of material (E, G, ν, ρ, α, λ). They are not grades of a standard and not catalogue values. A design takes its values from the grade's own data, entered as a material of your own.

Nine Addendum B templates map 25 inputs to a material property: M7 (G, E, ρ and the two R_m(d) coefficients), M8 (the secant-modulus points, the two permissible strains and ν), S7 (E, G, f_y), S8 (f_y, f_u), S9 (E, ν, ρ, f_y), B8 (E, α), B9 (λ), F1 (E, f_y) and F3 (α, ν). This script uses M7, F1 and S7.

| # | Action | Expected | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Open a project. Open **Helical springs: compression, extension, torsion** (M7). Read the bar. Type `S_e` 400 MPa. Name the sheet `W3 spring`. Press **Save as calculation**. | A new sheet opens on the declared defaults only: `G` 80 GPa, `E` 206 GPa, `rho` 7850 kg/m³. Before you type, the bar reads `Blocked — S_e: enter 'Endurance strength for the stress kind (shear; bending in torsion) at N_c cycles, alternating (no default: from the wire maker or your fatigue reference)' in MPa; it has no default value.` Notices include, for `G`, `E` and `rho` alike, `G: a tabulated value not taken from a reference record (Material properties); enter it as one so its source, edition and licence travel with it.` After the save the **Reference records** panel appears. | |

They include: the seed materials are generic, unlicensed and flagged indicative; no standard or catalogue table is seeded; a material round-trips with each value's source stamp and evidence hash; a later revision stales every calculation pinned to the earlier one; a temperature table is read linearly and refused outside its ends; one licensed value makes the whole material licensed; and licensed values never leave through a material export or an issued sheet. | |

### 2.10 Records to enter for each calculator: base and Addendum A

A new sheet opens with every record-kind table empty and some record-kind values at the template default. The sheet warns `<symbol>: a tabulated value not taken from a reference record` until you apply a record.

| Calculator | Record kind | Fills | Enter from |
|---|---|---|---|
| **E4** | `cable.ampacity` | `ampacity` table (cross-section against I_t) | Your licensed table for the installation method and insulation. Machine wiring (default context) uses your BS EN 60204-1 copy; the installation context uses your BS 7671 copy. **Required**: E4 is blocked without it, and so is the motor-circuit pack. |
| **E4** | `cable.correction-factors` | `C_a`, `C_g`, `C_i` | Your licensed ambient, grouping and thermal-insulation factors for the same method. |
| **E4** | `device.ratings` | `ratings` table | The preferred ratings of the device range: manufacturer datasheet or product standard. **Required** as above. |
| **E5** | `conductor.material` | `rho_20`, `alpha`, `Q_c`, `beta`, `theta_i`, `theta_f` | Material constants (IEC 60228 / IEC 60949 values) and the insulation's initial and final temperatures. Form defaults give the copper KAT; check them against your source. |
| **E5** | `device.characteristic` | `curve`, `I2t_let`, `I_cn` | Manufacturer curve and datasheet for the exact device (fuse or MCCB). MCBs B, C and D need no curve. |
| **B4** | `climate.design-temperature` | `theta_e` | The location's external design temperature from your own licensed or published source, with its edition. |
| **B4** | `material.conductivity` | the whole `layers` table (λ column) | Manufacturer's declared value or your licensed reference. |
| **B4** | `thermal.bridging` | `y_tb` | Your own source or a bridging calculation. |
| E6 | `psu.derating` | `f_derate` | PSU manufacturer's derating curve at the ambient and altitude. |
| E6 | `battery.factors` | `DoD`, `eta_b`, `k_temp`, `k_age`, `k_p`, `H_rate` | Battery manufacturer's data. |
| M5 | `bearing.ratings`, `bearing.factors`, `bearing.aiso` | `C`, `C_0`; `e_lim`, `X_1`, `Y_1`, `X`, `Y`, `X_0`, `Y_0`; `a_iso`, `kappa` | Manufacturer catalogue and life tool for the exact designation. |
| M4 | `shaft.material`, `shaft.marin`, `shaft.limits` | `S_ut`, `S_y`, `E`; `k_a`; `theta_max`, `y_max` | Material certificate; your edition of the fatigue reference; bearing and gear data. |
| S4 | `ec3.national-annex`, `steel.strengths` | `gamma_M2`, `gamma_M0`, `k_e1`, `k_e2`, `k_p1`, `k_p2`; `f_y`, `f_u` | National Annex in use; product standard for the grade. |
| S5 | `en1990.factors` | `gamma_G_sup`, `gamma_G_inf`, `gamma_Q`, `xi`, `psi` | UK National Annex (NA.A1.2(A)–(C)), or the EN 1990 Annex A1 recommended set as a separate, labelled record. |
| S6 | `ec2.national-annex`, `ec3.national-annex`, `ec3-1-1.national-annex`, `joint.friction`, `steel.strengths` | `alpha_cc`, `gamma_c`; `gamma_M2`; `gamma_M0`; `C_fd`; `f_y` | National Annexes in use; grout data. |
| B5 | `fluid.properties` | `cp_h`, `cp_c` | A property table or B2 at each stream's mean temperature. |
| B6 | `emitter.rating`, `fluid.expansion` | `n`; `e_fluid` | Emitter manufacturer's catalogue (BS EN 442 rating); glycol supplier's data. |

Kinds are taken from the templates' `RecordKinds` in the code. B4's `surface.resistances` kind of the first draft no longer exists: the surface resistances are now conventional values chosen by the new `flow` column. M6 and the base calculators draw on no record kind.

### 2.11 Records to enter for each calculator: Addendum B

**Rule (§B5).** Nothing below ships in source, seeds, fixtures or KATs: EN 10270 R_m(d) tables, EN 13906 permissible-stress diagrams, IPC-2152 charts, IET cable and conduit factor tables, Eurocode National Annex values and C₁/C₂ tables beyond small factor sets, ISO 13788 climate data and annex examples, BS 5422 thickness tables, ASME B31.3 allowable stresses and K1, ASME BTH-1 and EN 13155 text and tables, AS568 and ISO 3601 size and gland tables, Parker extrusion charts, and the ISO/TS 13725 method. Enter each from your own copy as a record, with **Licensed source** ticked where the lock applies (section 2.7). The four generic seed materials of W3 are the only data that ship, and they are flagged *indicative*.

**What the "Sheet without it" column means.** *Blocked*: the input has no default and is required, so the bar reads `Blocked — …` until you enter it. *Default, warns*: the sheet opens on a starting value (a conventional or invented one, not a table value from your standards) and warns `a tabulated value not taken from a reference record` until you apply a record. *Optional*: the input has no default and the check that needs it is not run. A table input arrives by **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)** (D-37). Import first, then **Save inputs as record**. The kinds are read from the templates' `RecordKinds` at `8e5f1190`.

| Calculator | Data to enter | Record kind | Fills | Sheet without it | Enter from |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **M7** | Wire tensile strength R_m(d), by wire diameter | `spring.wire-strength` | `Rm` | Optional (the sheet needs `Rm` by one route or the other) | Your EN 10270 copy for the wire grade, or the wire maker's certificate. Licensed. |
| **M7** | R_m(d) as a curve R_m = A/d^m (alternative to `Rm`) | `material.properties` | `Rm_A`, `Rm_m` (with G, E, ρ) | Optional; used when `rm_source` is the curve | A fit you make to your own table, entered as a material of your own (T-W3). |

| **M7** | End-support (seating) coefficient | `spring.seating` | `nu_seat` | Default, warns | Your EN 13906-1 copy or the spring maker. |
| **M7** | Endurance strength at the required cycles | `spring.endurance` | `S_e` | **Blocked** | The wire maker or your fatigue reference. |

The tabulated-value warning is tied to record kinds, so a new sheet does not warn that these are not from a table | Your AS568 or ISO 3601 copy, or the seal maker's gland table. Licensed. Type them with the source *reference* and the table number. Never paste a table into a record that is not ticked *Licensed source*. |
| **F3** | Squeeze, fill and stretch bands | `oring.criteria` | `q_lo_s`, `q_hi_s`, `q_lo_d`, `q_hi_d`, `fill_lo`, `fill_hi`, `x_lo`, `x_hi`, `od_hi` | Default, warns | Your Parker ORD 5700 and PTD 5705 copies (U-24, U-25). Licensed. |
| **F3** | Extrusion limits | `oring.extrusion` | `gap_allow`, `ext` (table) | Optional | Your Parker extrusion chart, read into a gap or a table. Licensed. |
| **F3** | Ring material | `material.properties` | `alpha`, `nu` | Default, warns | W3 material of your own compound. |

**Notes.** The *Sheet without it* column comes from the template defaults at `8e5f1190`; the new-sheet reading in each T-xx script is the check. A default is a starting value so that a new sheet runs. It is not a value from a standard that you may rely on. The record kinds of the older calculators are in section 2.10.

## Part 3 — Demo project walkthrough

The demo is one invented project, **Demo — Hydraulic Press Cell**. It holds every linked chain this build ships, already built and linked. You can open a calculation, change a figure and watch the chain go stale. Then you update it and check it again. You need no project of your own.

The demo makes only what you ask for. It is never installed at start-up. It says DEMO on every sheet. You can remove it without touching anything else. The full description is `docs/worked-examples/demo/README.md`. Every figure is invented, apart from one published steel section that is marked indicative.

The Guided test has no step for the demo. Do every step here by hand. Mark the guided column *none*. The Guided test still helps: its `pack-*` steps cover the same chains on your own data in Part 5.

### 3.1 Install the demo

| Step | Action | Expect | Guided | Result |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 3.1-1 | Open **Settings**. Find the **Demo project** section. Read its text. | The status line reads `The demo project is not installed.` **Install demo project** is offered. **Remove demo project** is not. | none |  |
| 3.1-2 | Press **Install demo project**. In the confirmation window press Cancel. | The status line reads `Install demo project was cancelled.` Nothing is made. Look at **Projects** to confirm. | none |  |
| 3.1-3 | Press **Install demo project** again. In the confirmation window press **Install**. | The section shows what it is making. It takes under a minute. The status line then says how many calculations and packs it made: 60 calculations in 13 packs. | none |  |
| 3.1-4 | Open **Projects**. | The project is named `Demo — Hydraulic Press Cell` with the number `DEMO0-PRESS1`. It carries the tag `demo-project`. | none |  |
| 3.1-5 | Go back to **Settings** → **Demo project**. | The status line says the demo is installed. **Install demo project** is not offered while it is installed. | none |  |

| 3.2-2 | Read **Calculation packs**, then **Packs in this project**. | It lists 13 packs: the 12 pack templates and the scan-to-seal pack made for the demo. Each is a Draft. | none |  |
| 3.2-3 | Open any calculation. Read the watermark above the form. | The watermark reads DRAFT. The form is editable. A check records a second person's name, so the demo is not checked for you. | none |  |

### 3.4 The deliberate failure

Every sheet in the demo passes its checks except one. The failure is on purpose. It shows what a failed check looks like inside a chain that goes on to solve the problem.

| 3.4-1 | Open the pack **Control panel cooling**. Open the member **Enclosure heat**. Read the checks. | The check `Natural temperature rise against the allowed rise` reads FAIL. Its utilisation is about 15. The panel cannot lose its heat without cooling. | none |  |
| 3.4-2 | Open the next three members: the cooling-water air coil, the pipework and the pump. | All three pass. The coil, its pipework and the pump are the answer to the failed check. | none |  |
| 3.4-3 | Look through the other 12 packs for any other FAIL. | There is none. The tests name this one failing check and fail if any other appears. | none |  |

This is the main thing the demo teaches. Use the **Support stand ES-4** pack, or any pack with at least three linked members.

That includes members that take it through another member. | none |  |
| 3.5-2 | Open a stale member. | An orange banner reads `Stale — …` and names the reason. **Update linked inputs** is offered. | none |  |
| 3.5-3 | Press **Record check** on that stale member. | The check is refused. The message says the calculation is stale and to update it first. | none |  |
| 3.5-4 | Update the stale members in order, upstream first. | Each press reads `Linked inputs updated to the current revisions and recalculated.` The banners clear. A member that depended only on one you just updated may clear by itself. | none |  |

### 3.6 DEMO on the sheets

Every sheet made under the demo project carries the notice *DEMO — invented data for demonstration only; not for design, purchase or construction*. It is the second line of the sheet text. In the PDF it is in four places. They are the header band (`CALCULATION SHEET — DRAFT — DEMO`), a notice line, the footer of every page, and the file's title and subject. The mark follows the project's flag. No calculation holds the word, so the demo's sheets cannot lose it.

A draft has no PDF. To see the PDF you must issue a demo pack. An issued record is never deleted, so **Remove demo project** then refuses (section 7.4 tests that refusal). Do not issue the demo on your real data folder. The automated tests `DemoWatermarkTests` and `DemoWatermarkPdfTests` cover the mark in the text and the PDF.

| Step | Action | Expect | Guided | Result |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 3.6-1 | Run `dotnet test tests/Tempest.Calc.Tests.Sheets --configuration Release --no-build --filter "FullyQualifiedName~DemoWatermarkTests"` and the same filter on `tests/Tempest.Desktop.Tests` for `DemoWatermarkPdfTests`. | Both pass. | none |  |

### 3.7 Remove the demo

Removal is the only delete this feature makes. It keeps to the demo. It deletes the demo's calculation and pack rows and the two scan profiles the install wrote, in one transaction. It then marks the demo project and its objects deleted. The audit trail keeps one row for the install and one for the removal.

| Step | Action | Expect | Guided | Result |

| 3.7-3 | Close the demo project. Press **Remove demo project**. In the confirmation window press Cancel. | The status line reads `Remove demo project was cancelled.` Nothing is deleted. | none |  |
| 3.7-4 | Press **Remove demo project** again. Press **Remove**. | The status line says what was removed. The demo is gone from **Projects** and from the lists of calculations and packs. Your own projects, calculations and records are unchanged. Compare with step 3.7-1. | none |  |
| 3.7-5 | Press **Remove demo project** once more. | The status line reads `There is no demo project to remove.` Nothing else happens. | none |  |
| 3.7-6 | Install the demo again, then remove it again. | Both work. The project list shows no stale second `DEMO0-PRESS1` entry. | none |  |

## Part 4 — The highest-liability calculators

Part 4 tests the calculators where a wrong answer can hurt a person. Test them while you are fresh. Do Part 2 first, because several of them block without records.

### 4.1 What highest liability means

A calculator is in this part when a wrong answer can hurt a person. The answer shows as a pass, and nobody notices in time. That is a test of consequence, not of effort. It has three conditions.

1. A failure can injure or kill. The harm is direct: shock, fire, a falling load, a pressure release, a collapse or a broken part.
2. The sheet can show PASS while the part is unsafe. Track P found results on the unsafe side, so the risk is real.
3. Track P rated the calculator High, or you named it in Addendum C. You named E4, E5, S8, F2, S6, S7, M3 and M4.

| Harm | Calculators | Why they are on the list |
|---|---|---|
| Electric shock or fire | E4 conductor sizing, E5 fault protection | They size the cable and prove that a fault clears in time. An error leaves a live fault or a hot cable. |
| A lifted load falls | S8 lifting lugs and spreader beams, the lifting lug and pin joint | They hang a load over people. Both sit behind the Lifting flag, which is off by default. |
| Stored pressure energy is released | F2 accumulator, F1 cylinder circuit, F3 O-ring gland, B6 sealed heating system | They size a gas charge, a rod and a line, a seal and an expansion vessel. An error can burst, buckle or leak under pressure. |
| Steel fails | S6 base plate, S7 lateral buckling, S1 section properties, S4 connections, S3 member checks | They decide whether a column, beam or joint carries its load. S1 feeds the other four and had a Critical defect. |
| A rotating or bolted part fails | M4 shaft, M3a bolted joint, M3b bolt group, M6 weld group, M7 spring | They check parts that carry torque, preload or stored energy. M4 feeds the drive-train chain, which had a Critical defect. |

Track P rates nine more calculators High: E1, M2a, M2b, E6, E8, B1, B2, B7 and B9. A wrong result there harms through another calculator, or slowly, or by cost. They open Part 6, in Track P's order.

The order in Part 4 follows Track P's recommended order in `docs/verification/risk-triage.md`. That order puts the owner's list first: E4, E5, S8, F2, S6, S7, M3, M4. The other calculators follow it by the same test.

### 4.2 How to read a calculator block and a long-form script

Every calculator in Parts 4 and 6 has the same block. It has a facts table, a steps table, and its long-form script when it has one. The block names the sets, the guided ids and the Track P script. It copies no expected numbers.

1. **A set step.** By hand, open the worksheet and press **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)** with the set's `inputs.json`. In the Guided test, pick the step id and press **Open worksheet with these inputs**, then **Compare with the form**. Compare each output with the set in `v0.26.0-test-values.md`. Pass when every value matches in the digits shown and the governing check reads as stated.
2. **A deliberate FAIL.** A few sets are expected to FAIL. The Expect column says so. Record P when the sheet fails as stated.
3. **A set for your review.** The Expect column says so. Decide it yourself.
4. **A Track P script.** Open the file named in the facts table. Follow its steps. Copy the file first and fill in the copy. Track P wrote the scripts before Track S fixed the defects. A step that says *expect FAIL until fixed* now passes when the Defects row says the finding is fixed. It still fails if the finding waits for your decision.
5. **A long-form script.** It is brought to the current version of the template. Its heading says which. Some numbers in it moved when C-1 fixed defects. If a number differs: (a) read the list of fixes under the heading; (b) read the test values for the current figure; (c) if a fix explains the difference, record `P` with a note; (d) if nothing explains it, record `F`.
6. **Long-form rows and the Guided test.** The Guided test keeps one result for each guided id. A long-form row has no id of its own. Open the worksheet from the set step of the same calculator and do the row by hand. Record one result against the set step and name any failed row in the note.
7. **An owner-decision defect.** Do not use a calculator for a result that an owner-decision row touches. The Defects row names them.

Keep the guided run and this file in step. Record each result in both places, or in the Guided test alone and attach its results sheet to Part 8.

#### 4.2.1 How the long-form scripts work

This is the description of a long-form script from the earlier packs, kept whole. Where it says that an input is *manual* and a check is refused, the rule still holds.

Each per-calculator script follows one pattern.

1. Open the worksheet (`Engineering → Modules → Calculations → Worksheets`) and read the governing bar before you type: a new sheet opens on the declared defaults only, and the script says what the bar reads (`Blocked — …` with the next thing to enter, or the reading of the defaults).
2. Load any table inputs by import (PowerShell block, then **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)**). Do this first.
3. Type or pick the single values listed, then wait for *Recalculating…* to clear.
4. Compare the results, the governing bar and the checks with the expected column.
5. Run the variation steps. Each one changes an input and has an expected FAIL, a block, or a changed number.

**Inputs.** Every case is the first Confirmed known-answer case of the calculator (its `TestCases`). The expected values are that case's `CalcExpectedValue` list, with its relative tolerance. The *Form shows*, governing-bar, check and notice texts come from running the calculator on the case laid over a new sheet's defaults.

**Governing bar format.** `PASS — <check name>: utilisation <u>` or `FAIL — …`, with `u` to two decimals (a yes/no check prints no utilisation). A utilisation at or below 1 passes.

**Typical notices.** Tabulated inputs raise a warning of the kind "a tabulated value not taken from a reference record". That is expected until you apply a record (Part 2). It is a warning, not a failure. The sheet prints the repeated ones as one line listing the symbols; the form keeps one per symbol.

**Cell format.** A table cell with a number carries a unit (`"Unit":"mm"`). A text cell carries `"Text"`. A yes/no cell carries `"Flag"`. The JSON in the scripts is exactly the form the import reads. A blank cell is the same as no cell.

**Sources and Check.** A check or an issue is refused until every input has a source other than *manual*. Values you type in a per-calculator script are *manual*, so those scripts stop before Check. The worked examples carry sources on every input, so T-00, T-L1, T-IND and T-REC use them. The packs' example inputs carry the source `assumption: example input — replace with your own`.

**Addendum B scripts.** The scripts of 3.B and 3.D follow this pattern and add three things. Each opens with the **new-sheet reading**, which is what the governing bar shows before you type anything. Each ends with an **expected-output table** that gives the tolerance of the known-answer case and what the form shows. Where the tolerance is tighter than the form's rounding, the *Form shows* column is the test. Each has **variation steps** that change one input and give a FAIL, a block or a changed number, and then put the input back. Four template versions rose after the first draft of this pack (E5 to 1.2.0, B4 to 1.2.0, and E1 and E2 to 1.4.0, with M1, B1 and B2 also higher, Appendix D). Each rise added an output or a port and changed no number in the Addendum A scripts. The reference line of a sheet now reads the new version. The §B9 review fixes raised three more: `fp.accumulator` to 1.3.1, `elec.pcb-trace` to 1.0.2 and `bs.pipe-expansion` to 1.2.1 (Appendix D). Other fixes changed a warning, a label or a sheet line and no version. Every `<id>@<version>` in this pack was checked against the template in the code at `8e5f1190`, and every expected output against the worked example's `README.md`.

### 4.3 T-00: the common worksheet flow

Run this once, on M5. It tests the flow that every calculator shares: save, list, reopen, export, check, issue and revise. The Guided test has no step for this flow. Do it by hand.

The M5 example `skf-6206-pump` is tagged Unconfirmed in Appendix A. Here it tests the flow, not the book value.

| # | Action | Expected | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Open a project. Open **Rolling bearing life (ISO 281)**. In PowerShell from the repository root run the command in the block under this table, then press **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)**. | Status reads `Imported 15 input(s).` Governing bar reads `PASS — Modified rating life against required life: utilisation 0.659`. `L_nmh` is about 7590 h (7589.55 computed). | |
| 2 | Type `T00 bearing` in **Calculation name**. Press **Save as calculation**. | Status reads `Saved as 'T00 bearing' in <project name>; it is in the Calculation register and has its own Evidence.` The watermark reads DRAFT. The button now reads **Save changes**. **Record check** appears. | |
| 3 | Look at **Saved calculations** (left column). | One row: `T00 bearing · Rolling bearing life (ISO 281) · rev A · Draft`. No ` · STALE`. | |

| 6 | Press **Issue**. | Status reads `Issued as 'T00-bearing-A'. The sheet is attached to its Evidence and listed under Reports.` Watermark ISSUED. The form is read-only: `This calculation is Issued; it is read-only. Revise it to start a new revision.` | |
| 7 | Find the issued sheet. Look in Engineering → Modules → Calculations → Calculation register, then in the project's Evidence. Open the PDF. | The PDF opens. The Inputs section cites each source. The Verification footer shows the real template hash and the known-answer cases passed (5/5 for M5). `STATUS.md` records PDFs under 200 KB; note the size if you can see the file. Note where you found it (section C.3). | |
| 8 | Back in the form, press **Revise**. | Status reads `Revision B started as a draft; revision A is superseded.` The form is editable again. The Saved calculations list shows both: `… · rev A · Superseded` and `… · rev B · Draft`. | |
| 9 | Pick another worksheet in the catalogue. Pick `T00 bearing · … · rev A · Superseded` in **Saved calculations** and press **Open**. | The M5 form returns with the saved inputs. Status reads `Opened 'T00 bearing' · rev A · Superseded. It is read-only; revise it to start a new revision.` | |
| 10 | Pick the `rev B · Draft` row and press **Open**. Change `F_r` to 3 kN. Without saving, pick the `rev A` row and press **Open**. | Status reads `The form has unsaved changes. Save them first, or press Open again to discard them and open 'T00 bearing'.` The form still shows rev B with 3 kN. | |
| 11 | Press **Open** again on the same `rev A` row. | The edit is discarded and rev A opens, as in step 9. | |

Command for step 1 (from the repository root):


| Step | Action | Expect | Guided | Result |

The governing bar reads `PASS — Design current against the device rating: utilisation 0.83`. | `example:elec.conductor-sizing/conveyor-motor-circuit` |  |

#### 4.4.1 Long form T-E4: Conductor sizing and overload coordination

**Written for version 1.1.0; brought to version 1.5.0 on 2026-10-27.** The Addendum C fixes touched this template: `elec.conductor-sizing-01`, `elec.conductor-sizing-02`, `elec.conductor-sizing-03`, `elec.conductor-sizing-05`, `elec.conductor-sizing-R1`. Every number, name and message below is what version 1.5.0 shows.

**Inputs from** known-answer case `ka-three-phase-mcb-c` — Three-phase 7.5 kW (output) load on a type C MCB, θ_a 35 °C with C_a 0.91, two circuits with C_g 0.77. Status: Confirmed (analytic; see Appendix A for what is and is not checked against a primary source).

**Synthetic data.** The capacities and the factors C_a 0.91 and C_g 0.77 are invented for the repository's tests. They match no published table. Never use them for a design. The ratings are the typical preferred series. Replace them with your own records for UK practice (Part 2).

This calculator demands an independent check (DA-12). Whoever created the calculation, or set or changed any of its inputs, cannot check it. See the last step and T-IND. Where no second person is available, the preparer may check it alone by ticking **Self-check (no second person available)** and writing why they stand by it (DC-21): the statement is kept on the calculation, printed on the issued sheet as `Self-checked by the preparer: …` and recorded in the audit trail (T-IND, steps I6 to I8).

**Values to enter** (a new sheet opens on the declared defaults only; caption is `symbol — description`):

| Input | Value | Unit | On a new sheet |
|---|---|---|---|
| `supply` — Supply | three-phase |  | already set |
| `P` — Rated output (shaft) power; η converts it to input (DC: electrical power drawn, η not applied) | 7.5 | kW | already set |
| `U` — Supply voltage (line voltage for three-phase) | 400 | V | already set |
| `cos_phi` — Power factor (not used for DC) | 0.85 |  | already set |
| `eta` — Efficiency (not used for DC) | 0.9 |  | already set |
| `T_amb` — Ambient temperature | 35 | degC | type it |
| `N_g` — Grouped circuits | 2 |  | type it |
| `device` — Protective device | MCB type C |  | already set |
| `C_a` — Ambient correction factor (1 does not apply the ambient: enter the factor for T_amb from your licensed table) | 0.91 |  | type it |
| `C_g` — Grouping correction factor (at most 1) | 0.77 |  | type it |
| `C_i` — Thermal insulation correction factor (at most 1) | 1 |  | already set |

**Table inputs.** A new sheet opens with every table empty and the form has no row editor (D-37), so load these by import. Copy this block to the clipboard from PowerShell, then press **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)**:

```powershell
@'
```

#### 4.5.1 Long form T-E5: Fault protection (BS 7671)

**Written for version 1.2.0; brought to version 1.6.0 on 2026-10-27.** The Addendum C fixes touched this template: `elec.fault-protection-01`, `elec.fault-protection-03`, `elec.fault-protection-09`, `elec.fault-protection-05`, `elec.fault-protection-06`. Every number, name and message below is what version 1.6.0 shows.

**Inputs from** known-answer case `ka-mcb-b32` — 32 A type B MCB, 20 m of 2.5/1.5 mm² copper at 70 °C. Status: Confirmed (analytic; see Appendix A for what is and is not checked against a primary source).

A 32 A type B MCB on 20 m of 2.5/1.5 mm² copper at 70 °C, U_0 230 V. Every value but `k_R` equals the form default. The case uses `k_R` = 1, the pure resistivity; the form opens on 1.125. Disconnection is below 0.1 s and no let-through I²t is entered. Since C-2 that is a failing check, not a warning: `Conductors against let-through at the origin` fails until `I2t_let` is entered. So the verdict of this case is FAIL by design. The other checks pass.

This calculator demands an independent check (DA-12). Whoever created the calculation, or set or changed any of its inputs, cannot check it. See the last step and T-IND. Where no second person is available, the preparer may check it alone by ticking **Self-check (no second person available)** and writing why they stand by it (DC-21): the statement is kept on the calculation, printed on the issued sheet as `Self-checked by the preparer: …` and recorded in the audit trail (T-IND, steps I6 to I8).

**Values to enter** (a new sheet opens on the declared defaults only; caption is `symbol — description`):

| Input | Value | Unit | On a new sheet |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Z_e` — External earth fault loop impedance | 0.35 | ohm | already set |
| `L` — Circuit length | 20 | m | already set |
| `S_line` — Line conductor cross-section | 2.5 | mm^2 | already set |
| `S_cpc` — Protective conductor cross-section | 1.5 | mm^2 | already set |
| `k_R` — Conductor resistance factor on ρ₂₀ for R₁ + R₂ (1 = the resistivity itself; the default of 1.125 takes pure copper to the cable standard's maximum conductor resistances, so Z_s is not understated; k is not changed by it) | 1 |  | type it |
| `device` — Protective device | MCB type B (I_a = 5 I_n) |  | already set |
| `I_n` — Rated current | 32 | A | already set |

### 4.6 S8 — Lifting lugs and spreader beams (`struct.lifting`)

| Item | Where |

| Independent re-derivation | Not re-derived. |

**The flag steps.** S8 is built but withheld. A machine administrator must switch its feature on before anyone sees it. The flag is off by default (ADR-0173). The Guided test has six lifting steps. They cover the flag, the run, the four-person sign-off and the issued sheet. The two lifting worksheets open in the Guided test only while the flag is on. The steps below are in the order to do them. If you will also run chain 6 (section 5.20), do it before the last step.

#### 4.6.1 Long form T-S8: Lifting lugs and spreader beams (`struct.lifting@1.2.0`, behind the `lifting` feature flag)

**Written for version 1.1.0; brought to version 1.2.0 on 2026-10-27.** Every number, name and message below is what version 1.2.0 shows.

S8 designs the lugs and spreader beams that hang a load. A wrong PASS can drop a load on a person. S8 is built and tested, but a machine administrator must switch its feature on before anyone can see it. The flag is **off by default**. This script has four parts. Part A switches the flag on and off, and tries the ways that must not switch it on. Part B runs the calculator on its worked example. Part C takes one calculation from draft to issued with four different people. Part D lists what stays refused while the flag is off. **Do not switch the flag on for a machine that issues real calculations before section 4.6.2 is signed.** Use a test machine and a dedicated data folder. Switch the flag off again when you finish.

**What S8 does** (spec `docs/calcs/structural/struct.lifting.md`, ADR-0173).

- It checks a pin-loaded lifting lug and the lug loads of a spreader or lifting beam.
- It turns the rated load `WLL` into a sling force on each lug, from the sling angle `θ` (in the lug plane) and the out-of-plane angle `β`.
- It checks four sections: tension beyond the hole, single-plane fracture, shear-out on two planes and pin bearing. It adds a dishing ratio.
- BTH-1 mode follows clause 3-3.3 by number only. EN 13155 mode asks for no permanent set at 2× the rated load and no release at 3×, for at most 16,000 cycles. A third mode runs both.
- The `beam` switch adds the automatic 6° pitch and roll cases for a horizontal beam.
- Outputs: a capacity and a utilisation per mode, the governing mode, a proof-load value and a marking and certification checklist (text). Ports: `capacity` (`lug.capacity`), `governing_mode` (`lug.governing_mode`), `util` (`spreader.util`), and `F_s`, `F_ip`, `F_op` and `N_beam` for the linked checks.
- **Nothing from BTH-1 or EN 13155 ships.** The design factor `Nd`, the service factor `k_svc`, the divisors `n_y` and `n_u`, the bearing multiple `k_b`, the dishing limit `dish_max` and the proof-load factor `k_proof` are your records. None has a default. Every numeric input has a hard limit equal to its stated range: outside it the sheet does not run.

**Governance** (ADR-0173).

- The template names the `lifting` feature, a competent-person attestation and a second independent check.
- **Attest** records a competent person under the signed-in principal. The person must be none of the authors (the creator or anyone who set or changed an input). The name, qualification and statement they type are display text. A later change to any input voids the attestation.
- **Record check** is refused without the attestation. The attester cannot be the first checker.
- **Countersign** records a second checker with an organisation and a statement of at least 20 characters. That person must be none of the authors, not the attester and not the first checker.
- **Issue** is refused without an accepted second check.
- The Evidence record belongs to the worksheet. The generic Evidence Check, Issue and Revise acts refuse it, and its editor offers **Open worksheet**.
- **Signatures are not authenticated.** TempestOS asks for no password. It records and compares the signed-in names. It cannot know that a person is who they say, that the attester is competent or that the second checker is independent in fact. The sheet says so.

**Part A. The flag.**

The flag is read once, when the application starts. The Windows source is the machine policy value `Lifting` (a DWORD, set to exactly 1) under `HKLM\SOFTWARE\Policies\TempestOS\Features`. Linux and macOS read `"Lifting": true` from `/etc/tempestos/features.json`. That file counts only when it is a regular file, owned by root, in a directory owned by root, with no group or other write bit and no symbolic link. Nothing else is read: not `appsettings.json`, not the environment, not the command line, not the current directory and not the current user's registry hive.

| # | Action | Expected | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| A1 | **Flag off (the default).** Check that no policy value exists: `Get-ItemProperty 'HKLM:\SOFTWARE\Policies\TempestOS\Features' -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue`. Start the app. Open the worksheets. Look in the catalogue and under Disciplines → Structural. Press Ctrl+K and type `lifting lug`, then `spreader`. Open Engineering → Modules → Calculations → Calculators. | **Lifting lugs and spreader beams** is listed nowhere. The older module **Lifting lug and pin joint** is not offered in the Calculators list either. | |
| A2 | **Ways that must not switch it on.** Close every instance. Try each of the four below, one at a time, and restart the app after each. Undo each one before the next (the clean-up lines are in the block below). (a) A file `appsettings.json` beside the executable. (b) The environment variable `Calc__Features__Lifting`. (c) The switch `--Calc:Features:Lifting=true` on the `dotnet run` line. (d) The policy value under `HKCU` instead of `HKLM`. | S8 stays hidden after each one. Nothing but the machine policy counts. A flag that any of these can set is a finding: record it. | |
| A3 | **Values that do not count.** As administrator, set `Lifting` under the `HKLM` key to the string `1`, then to the DWORD `0`, then to the DWORD `2`. Restart after each. | S8 stays hidden after each. Only the DWORD 1 switches it on. | |
| A4 | **Switch it on.** As administrator, set the DWORD `Lifting` = 1 under the `HKLM` key (first block below). Restart the app. Open the worksheets. | **Lifting lugs and spreader beams** is listed under Structural, and Ctrl+K finds it by `lifting`. The older module **Lifting lug and pin joint** is offered in the Calculators list again. The application log holds the warning `Optional calculator feature 'lifting' is ENABLED by machine policy HKLM\SOFTWARE\Policies\TempestOS\Features. It has not been through the owner's review checklist unless the owner has recorded it.` Open the newest `tempest-<yyyyMMdd>.log` in `$root\logs\` (for this pack `$HOME\TempestOS-Test-Data\logs\`; the date in the name is UTC), with the command in the block under this table. If the line is not there, record `B`. | |
| A5 | **Optional, Linux or macOS.** Create the file with the commands in the last block below. Start the app. Then break each trust rule in turn and restart: make the file group-writable, make a user own it, replace it with a symbolic link, replace it with a named pipe. | The root-owned regular file switches S8 on. Each broken rule leaves it off, and the log says why (the `/etc/tempestos/features.json was ignored: …` warning). Record `N` if you have no such machine; the automated proof is `FeatureSourceTests`. | |

To read the newest application log for step A4 (in a normal PowerShell window):

```powershell
$root = "$HOME\TempestOS-Test-Data"
Get-ChildItem "$root\logs" | Sort-Object LastWriteTime | Select-Object -Last 1 | Get-Content | Select-String lifting
```

```powershell
# A2 (a): the file beside the executable (run from the repository root; the path is the Desktop build output folder)
'{ "Calc": { "Features": { "Lifting": true } }, "Lifting": true }' | Set-Content -Encoding utf8 "src\Tempest.Desktop\bin\Release\net10.0\appsettings.json"
# A2 (b): the environment variable (in the shell that starts the app)
$env:Calc__Features__Lifting = 'true'
# A2 (c): the full command line ($root as in section 1)
dotnet run --project src/Tempest.Desktop/Tempest.Desktop.csproj --configuration Release --no-build -- --persistence-root $root --Calc:Features:Lifting=true
# A2 (d): the current user's hive
New-Item -Path 'HKCU:\SOFTWARE\Policies\TempestOS\Features' -Force | Out-Null
New-ItemProperty -Path 'HKCU:\SOFTWARE\Policies\TempestOS\Features' -Name Lifting -PropertyType DWord -Value 1 -Force | Out-Null
# Clean-up after A2
Remove-Item "src\Tempest.Desktop\bin\Release\net10.0\appsettings.json"
Remove-Item Env:Calc__Features__Lifting
Remove-Item -Recurse 'HKCU:\SOFTWARE\Policies\TempestOS\Features'
```

Open an administrator PowerShell: Start, type PowerShell, right-click **Windows PowerShell**, **Run as administrator**. Run only the registry lines there. Start the app from your normal PowerShell window.

```powershell
# A3 and A4, in an administrator PowerShell
New-Item -Path 'HKLM:\SOFTWARE\Policies\TempestOS\Features' -Force | Out-Null
New-ItemProperty -Path 'HKLM:\SOFTWARE\Policies\TempestOS\Features' -Name Lifting -PropertyType DWord -Value 1 -Force | Out-Null
# A3 variants: -PropertyType String -Value '1'   then   -PropertyType DWord -Value 0   then   -PropertyType DWord -Value 2
# Switch it off again
Remove-ItemProperty -Path 'HKLM:\SOFTWARE\Policies\TempestOS\Features' -Name Lifting
```

```bash
# A5, Linux or macOS
sudo install -d -m 0755 -o root /etc/tempestos
echo '{ "Lifting": true }' | sudo tee /etc/tempestos/features.json
sudo chown root /etc/tempestos/features.json
sudo chmod 0644 /etc/tempestos/features.json
```

**Part B. The calculator on its worked example** (flag on).

**Inputs from** worked example `lifting-beam-lug-pair` (`docs\worked-examples\structural\struct.lifting\lifting-beam-lug-pair`) — Two lugs on a 150 kN horizontal lifting beam at 30° and 2° out of plane, both modes, with the automatic 6° tilt cases. Status: Confirmed (analytic; recomputed in python3 in the repository). The design factors in it are illustrative values, not values from any standard.

A 25 mm S355 lug plate, a 55 mm hole, a 50 mm pin, 75 mm end radius and 40 mm edge distance, on an 80 cm² beam. The 6° pitch case governs the sling force (101.87 kN against 86.66 kN untilted). Bearing governs both modes. The example leaves the attestation inputs blank, because an example is not an issued sheet.

The worked example carries a source on every input. Copy its inputs to the clipboard from PowerShell, run from the repository root, then press **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)** (step B3):

```powershell
Get-Content -Raw docs\worked-examples\structural\struct.lifting\lifting-beam-lug-pair\inputs.json | Set-Clipboard   # 22 inputs
```

| # | Action | Expected | Result |
|---|---|---|---|

**Part C. Draft to issued with four different people** (flag on). You need four people who can sign in: **P1** the author, **P2** the competent person who attests, **P3** the first checker and **P4** the second checker. Use **Settings → Switch person…** between steps, as in T-IND. If **Switch person…** lists fewer than four people, run the steps you can and record `B` on the rest. The automated proof is `WorksheetGovernanceTests`.

The sign-off controls sit in a panel named **Sign-off**. It shows only for a template that demands them. Attest is offered on a draft, Countersign on a checked calculation. Every name that the sheet prints comes from the signed-in person, so nobody can type another person into the checker line.
