#### 4.6.2 S8 review checklist: before the flag is ever enabled

**Why this section exists.** S8 designs the lugs and spreader beams that hang a load. A wrong PASS can drop a load on a person. S8 is in the tree, behind the `lifting` feature flag, which is off by default (T-S8, ADR-0173). The flag stays off on every machine that issues real calculations until this checklist is signed.

**Two conditions come before the flag is ever switched on.** Both are recorded in `docs/calcs/structural/struct.lifting.md`, ADR-0173 and the security review of S8.

1. **The owner review is recorded.** You compare the equations below with your own copy of the code, row by row, and write down the result (§B10 item 6). The three review rounds checked the arithmetic against the published course example and the equations against the clause forms. They did not check the sheet against BTH-1 or EN 13155 as you hold them. Only you can do that.
2. **Signatures are authenticated.** TempestOS asks for no password. A person who can use **Settings → Switch person…** can hold every role: author, attester, both checkers. The sheet says so. ADR-0173 keeps the flag off until this changes, or until your own procedure, outside the program, supplies the control (row E3).

**Who and what.** A competent person (the person LOLER regulation 9 has in mind for a thorough examination, or a chartered engineer with lifting-equipment experience) does the review. The reviewer holds their **own copies** of ASME BTH-1, *Design of Below-the-Hook Lifting Devices* (the 2023 edition or later; the addendum names 2023 or the 2026 edition), and EN 13155, in the edition they hold with its amendments. The addendum names "EN 13155+A1"; the repository states neither the edition nor the title of EN 13155. Record the exact edition and title from your copy. The reviewer also holds EN 13001-3-1 (*Cranes — General design — Part 3-1: Limit states and proof of competence of steel structures*), which Addendum B names as the calculation route of EN 13155 mode. The reviewer holds the Lifting Operations and Lifting Equipment Regulations 1998 (LOLER) and the Provision and Use of Work Equipment Regulations 1998 (PUWER).

**Ground rules.**

- **Cite clause numbers and titles only.** Never copy standards text, tables or figures into this file, into the repository or into a record that is not marked licensed. Write the reviewer's answer as a clause number plus *agrees*, *differs* or *not in my edition*.
- The only BTH-1 clause number the repository names is **3-3.3**. The sheet's own label for it is "pin-connected plates, design of lifting lugs"; confirm the clause title from your copy. The sheet names EN 13155 by title only ("clause to be confirmed") and EN 13001-3-1 as clause 5 (proof of static strength). Every other clause number is left for the reviewer to fill. Nobody looked any up. Write down the title next to each number you fill.
- Read the spec page of S8 (`docs/calcs/structural/struct.lifting.md`) and the sheet's **Show working** for each equation. Hand-calculate each one on the PDHonline S106 case (T-S8 step B16) and on one case of your own.
- The sheet's equations follow the failure modes of the published course example. Its constants (1.13 and 0.92 in the fracture area, 0.6 in the effective width, 0.275 in `C_r`, 55 in the shear angle) are closed-form coefficients from that example. They are not a table from a standard. Check each against your edition.
- A PASS in this checklist means the sheet's equations match your edition. It does not make the program's output a thorough examination. LOLER regulation 9 still needs a competent person's examination of the real equipment.
- Three review rounds found the arithmetic of the sheet consistent with the published S106 example (all seven published values reproduce) and conservative against the clause forms in the cases the reviewer checked. That is the programmer's evidence, not yours.

**A. BTH-1 mode: the equations.** Check each against clause 3-3.3 and its neighbours in your copy. Notation: `F_s` sling force, `F_a` allowable stress, `A_t`, `A_fr`, `A_v`, `A_b` the four areas, `D_h` hole diameter, `D_p` pin diameter, `t_p` thickness, `a` end distance, `side` the edge distance across the load line, limited to `R − D_h/2`.

| # | Check | What the sheet does | Reviewer's question | Clause in your copy | Result |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A1 | **Design factor and service factor.** | The design load is `D = N_d·k_svc·F_s`. `N_d` (1 to 5) comes from your record by Design Category. `k_svc` (1 to 3) is blank for 1.0. No default ships. BTH-1 mode refuses to run without `N_d`, `n_y`, `n_u` and `k_b`. | Does your edition put the design factor on the load, as here, or on the allowable stress? Is the service factor the same kind of factor? | | |
| A2 | **Allowable stress.** | One value for every section: `F_a = min(f_y/n_y, f_u/n_u)`. The divisors are your records (`n_y` 1 to 4, `n_u` 1 to 6). | Does your edition take **separate** allowables for yield-governed and fracture-governed sections? If it does, the sheet cannot reproduce it, and its single `F_a` can only be the lower. | | |
| A3 | **Tension through the hole.** | `P_t = C_r·F_a·A_t`, `A_t = 2·t·b_e,eff`, with `b_e,eff = min(side, 4t, 0.6·(f_u/f_y)·√(D_h/side)·side)`. The sheet warns when `b_e,eff` is below the edge distance. | Is the effective width limited by 4t and by the `0.6·(f_u/f_y)·√(D_h/b)` term, as here? Is `C_r` applied to this mode? | | |
| A4 | **Single-plane fracture beyond the hole.** | `P_sp = C_r·F_a·A_fr`, `A_fr = [1.13·a + 0.92·side/(1 + side/D_h)]·t`. It uses `side`, **not** the reduced `b_e,eff` of A3. | Are the 1.13 and 0.92 constants those of your edition? Should the second term use the reduced effective width `b_e,eff` instead of `side`? | | |
| A5 | **Shear-out on two planes.** | `P_v = F_a·A_v/√3`, `A_v = 2t·[a + (D_p/2)(1 − cos φ)]`, `φ = 55·D_p/D_h` degrees. The √3 is the distortion-energy shear ratio. | What strength and coefficient does your edition apply to double-plane shear-out? The sheet uses F_a/√3. Record the clause and whether the sheet is conservative against it. | | |
| A6 | **Bearing at the pin.** | `P_b = k_b·F_a·D_p·t`. `k_b` (0.5 to 3) is your record. | Which strength does your edition use for bearing (yield or ultimate), and what multiple? The sheet is conservative whenever `F_a` is below `f_y`. | | |
| A7 | **Pin clearance `C_r` and the ratio `D_p/D_h`.** | `C_r = 1 − 0.275·√(1 − D_p²/D_h²)`, applied for every `D_p/D_h < 1` with **no cut-off** as the ratio approaches 1. A pin larger than the hole blocks. A ratio below 0.9 warns. | Does your edition set `C_r` to 1 above a limit ratio (the reviewer recalled about 0.9 in the course example)? At what ratio? Omitting the cut-off is conservative. | | |
| A8 | **Dishing.** | The dishing ratio is `D_h/t`, compared with your record `dish_max` (blank: no check). A ratio above the limit is named in `governing_mode` and fails the dishing check. | Is the ratio defined on the hole diameter (as here), on the pin, or with another thickness? Is the limit a pass/fail check or a design-category rule? | | |
| A9 | **Geometry limits.** | Hard limits on every number: `t_p` 5 to 150 mm, `D_h` 10 to 500, `D_p` 8 to 500, `r_lug` 15 to 600, `b_e` 5 to 400. Blocks: pin larger than hole, `r_lug` not above the hole radius, `f_u` below `f_y`. | Does your edition set a minimum thickness, a hole position rule or an edge-radius rule that the sheet does not check? None is silently assumed. | | |
| A10 | **Strengths.** | `f_y` and `f_u` come from a W3 material (T-W3) of your grade. A seed material is indicative and raises its notice. | Do you use the plate strength for its thickness (T-S8's worked example does, from a mill certificate)? | | |
| A11 | **Units.** | The S106 case is in kip, inch and ksi. The form converts to kN and mm. | 1 kip = 4.4482 kN. 1 in² = 645.16 mm². Do the converted S106 values reproduce (T-S8 step B16)? | | |

**B. Load direction, tilt and sharing.**

| # | Check | What the sheet does | Reviewer's question | Clause in your copy | Result |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| B1 | **Sling force from the vertical load.** | `F_v = WLL/n_eff`. `F_s = F_v/(cos θ·cos β)`. `θ` is measured in the lug plane **from the vertical**; `β` is the out-of-plane angle. `F_ip = F_s·cos β`, `F_op = F_s·sin β`. | Are both angles measured as your edition measures them? If either is from the horizontal, the cosines swap for sines. | | |
| B2 | **Which force the lug is checked for.** | The lug is checked for the resultant `F_s` whatever `β` is (conservative). `F_ip`, `F_op` and the moment `M_op = F_op·h` are outputs for the weld and base checks on the linked sheets. | Does your edition check the out-of-plane component separately? | | |
| B3 | **The four-lug rule.** | `n_eff = min(n_lugs, 3)`: with four lugs, three are taken to share the load and a fourth is not relied on. | Is that the rule your edition and your lifting procedure intend for a statically indeterminate four-lug set, or does another share apply? | | |
| B4 | **Tilt: pitch.** | A horizontal beam (switch `beam`) adds a 6° pitch. The slings leave at `θ ± 6°`, meet at the hook and carry the load by statics. The sling at `θ − 6°` takes `F_s,pitch = 2·F_v·sin(θ + 6°)/sin 2θ` (divided by `cos β`). | Is the tilt 6°, and is it a pitch about a horizontal axis across the beam? | | |
| B5 | **Tilt: roll.** | The out-of-plane angle grows by 6°: `F_s,roll = F_v/(cos θ·cos(β + 6°))`. The larger of the nominal, pitch and roll cases governs. | Does your edition ask for pitch and roll **together**, not one at a time? Is 6° right for roll? | | |
| B6 | **Force along the beam (for S7).** | `N_beam` is measured along the tilted beam axis. In the pitch case each sling stays at `θ` to the beam's normal, so the more loaded lug sends `F_v·sin(θ + 6°)/cos θ` along the beam. The level case is `F_v·tan θ`. `N_beam` is the larger. | Do you agree with the geometry of the rigid hook–lug–lug triangle? The review rounds re-derived it from hook equilibrium. | | |
| B7 | **Small sling angles.** | For `θ ≤ 6°` with two or more lugs, one sling goes slack and the whole load can sit on one vertical sling. `F_s,pitch = WLL/cos β` and `N_beam = WLL·sin 6°`. The sheet warns. The value is continuous with the `θ > 6°` branch at 6°. | Is `WLL·sin 6°` the force you expect along the beam in that case? The reviewers judged it the upper bound. | | |
| B8 | **Angles at their limits.** | `θ` 0 to 60°, `β` 0 to 30° are hard limits. A sling angle plus tilt above 60° from the vertical warns. | Try `θ` 0, `θ` 60° with the beam on, and `β` 30°. The sheet blocks or warns. It never divides by a cosine near zero. | | |

**C. EN 13155 mode.**

| # | Check | What the sheet does | Reviewer's question | Clause in your copy | Result |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| C1 | **The two load cases.** | `P_EN = min(Y/2, U/3)` on the four sections. `Y` uses `f_y·A`, `U` uses `f_u·A`. Shear uses `f/√3` on `A_v`. The fracture area is taken **without** `C_r`. `u = F_s/P_EN`. The governing route is named: `2×WLL elastic` or `3×WLL no release`. | Do the 2× (no permanent set) and 3× (no release) cases and the stress bases match your edition with its A1 amendment? Is the fracture area right without `C_r`? | | |
| C2 | **Cycle limit.** | `n_cycles` has a hard maximum of 16 000. Above it the sheet does not run. | Is 16 000 the limit of the route in your edition? | | |
| C3 | **Calculation route.** | The sheet cites EN 13001-3-1 clause 5 and **does not implement** it. It is a nominal-section proof, with no notch-stress route. | Is a nominal-section proof enough for the attachments you design? Is clause 5 the right reference? | | |
| C4 | **Working load limit.** | `WLL` is the rated load of the appliance, carried by all its lugs (0.5 to 10 000 kN). | Is the sheet's WLL the lifted mass the attachment is rated for, in your edition? | | |
| C5 | **Proof load and marking.** | `proof_load = k_proof·WLL` (`k_proof` 1 to 3, your record; blank gives none). The checklist is text: marking (LOLER reg 7), thorough examination (reg 9), proof load, certificates, pin, shackle and sling ratings (PUWER reg 4), design basis, examination report. | Does `k_proof` equal the test load your edition sets? Does each marking item exist in your edition? The checklist claims no certification. | | |
| C6 | **Both modes at once.** | Every check of both modes is listed. `lug_util` is the larger utilisation, so `capacity` is the smaller of the two capacities. The governing mode is named. | Does the governing mode name match the route you would apply? | | |

**D. Records: nothing ships.**

| # | Check | Expected | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | Search the source, the seeds, the worked example and the test fixtures of S8 for any BTH-1 or EN 13155 table value or sentence. The §B5 grep in the third review round found none. | None. `N_d` by Design Category, `k_svc`, `n_y`, `n_u`, `k_b`, `dish_max` and `k_proof` are records you enter (kinds `lifting.design-factors`, `lifting.allowable-stress`, `lifting.limits`). The numbers in the worked example are labelled illustrative. | |
| D2 | Open the spec page and the sheet text. | Each cites clause numbers and titles only. No sentence is lifted from a standard. | |
| D3 | The worked example. | It says it is not an approval of any lug. Its design factors are labelled illustrative and not from any standard. | |
| D4 | The constants in A3, A4, A5, A7 (1.13, 0.92, 0.6, 0.275, 55). | They come from the published course example, not from a table in a standard. You decide whether they are acceptable as closed-form coefficients (§B5). | |

**E. Governance at Issue.**

| # | Check | Expected | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| E1 | The attestation (name, qualification, statement). | Required. Made through **Attest** by a signed-in person who is none of the authors. The statement has at least 20 characters. Any later input change voids it. A revision starts without one. | |
| E2 | The second Checked signature. | Two different checkers, neither an author, neither the attester. The second checker's statement has at least 20 characters. Issue is refused without an accepted second check. A rejection is recorded and blocks Issue. | |
| E3 | **What "signature" means here.** | The names are the signed-in names of the installation. TempestOS does not check who anyone is, whether the attester is competent, or whether the second checker is independent in fact. One person with **Settings → Switch person…** can hold every role (T-S8 step C16 shows a typed name accepted as typed). **Decide, in writing**, whether your procedure accepts that. If it does not, require a signed record outside the program and file it with the issue. Authenticated signatures are the second condition at the top of this section. | |
| E4 | The scope wording on the sheet. | It reads "design calculation, not a thorough examination" and carries the LOLER and PUWER scope notes. Check the notes against the regulations: regulation 7 (marking of lifting equipment), regulation 9 (thorough examination and inspection), and PUWER regulation 4 (suitability) and regulation 5 (maintenance). The regulation numbers are from memory in the sheet and in this pack. Check them. | |
| E5 | The issued sheet and its Evidence. | The PDF names the template version and hash, the mode, every record pin, the attestation and both checkers, and ends with `Optional feature: lifting feature enabled (…)` naming where the flag was switched on (T-S8 step C15). | |
| E6 | The Evidence record. | It belongs to the worksheet. The generic Evidence Check, Issue and Revise acts refuse it, so the second check cannot be bypassed from the Evidence editor. | |

**F. The flag.**

| # | Check | Expected | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| F1 | Where the flag lives. | Windows: `HKLM\SOFTWARE\Policies\TempestOS\Features`, DWORD `Lifting` = 1. Linux and macOS: `"Lifting": true` in `/etc/tempestos/features.json`, a regular file owned by root, in a directory owned by root, with no group or other write bit and no symbolic link. An environment variable, a command-line switch, the current directory, `appsettings.json` and `HKCU` do not enable it (T-S8 steps A2 and A3). | |
| F2 | Who may write that location on each machine. | A named person. A machine that issues real calculations never has the flag on before this checklist is signed. Enabling is logged at startup with its source. | |
| F3 | The residual risks the review rounds left open. | (a) The Unix check examines only the immediate parent directory, so a symbolic link higher up is accepted. For the fixed path the parents are `/etc` and `/`, which are root-only. (b) A user who patches the per-user application binaries can bypass any gate. The machine policy is the right control; code tampering is outside a configuration gate. Decide whether you accept both. | |
| F4 | With the flag off, what happens to an issued S8 sheet. | You know whether it stays readable (the PDF stays attached to its Evidence; the worksheet shows the refusal sentence; nothing can revise it). Nothing else can create, import or issue S8 (T-S8 part D). | |

| Rows that differ, with the clause number of each | |
| Condition 1: this review recorded | Yes / No |
| Condition 2: signatures authenticated, or an outside procedure accepted (row E3) | Yes / No |
| Decision | Enable the flag on: (machines) / Do not enable |

### 4.8 T-FPD: the Fluid Power discipline

F1, F2 and F3 belong to the fifth calculation discipline, Fluid Power (ADR-0170). Run this short script before F2. The Guided test has no step for it.

**What it is.** Fluid Power is the fifth calculation discipline and the sixth engineering domain. The Engineering tree and a project's Structure tab list it beside Structural, Electrical and Building Services. It holds three kinds of object, each with typed attributes: **Circuit** (circuit type, working pressure in bar, fluid, flow rate in L/min), **Actuator** (actuator type, bore, rod diameter and stroke in mm, working pressure in bar) and **Power unit** (pump type, displacement in cm³/rev, motor power in kW, reservoir volume in L, relief setting in bar). **The attributes are recorded as typed.** No rating, no safety factor and no design-code check applies to them. Pneumatics (ISO 6358) is out of scope and has no placeholder. The expected texts below come from `ADR-0170` and the Core test `DisciplineWorkspaceTests`. I did not run the Desktop windows, so the menu wording is to be confirmed.

| # | Action | Expected | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Open a project. Open the Engineering tree and the project's Structure tab. | **Fluid Power** is listed as a discipline, with three folders: **Circuits**, **Actuators** and **Power units**. All are empty in a new project. | |
| 2 | Create a Circuit named `Press circuit`. Set `Circuit type` `Open-centre, load sensing`, `Working pressure (bar)` `9999`, `Fluid` `ISO VG 46 mineral oil`. | Every value is accepted exactly as typed. 9999 bar raises no rating warning (by design). The inspector shows `Flow rate (L/min)` as `(not recorded)`. | |
| 3 | Try to set `Bore (mm)` on the circuit. | Refused. The message names the circuit's own attributes, including `Working pressure (bar)`. An attribute belongs to its own kind. | |
| 4 | Create an Actuator `Clamp cylinder` and a Power unit `Press pump`. Fill their attributes. Close the program and open it again on the same data folder. | The objects and their attributes survive the restart. | |
| 5 | Select `Press circuit` in the Structure tab. Press Ctrl+K, type `fluid-power`, and run **Request review**, then **Approve**, then **Release** (the commands of the Fluid Power discipline, one per step). If the Structure tab offers review buttons for a Structural member, use the same buttons here. | Each state change is accepted and shown on the circuit, as for a Structural member. To be confirmed: record the button or command names you used. | |
| 6 | Open Engineering → Modules → Calculations → Worksheets, and look under Disciplines. | A **Fluid Power** node lists three worksheets: **Cylinder and actuator circuit** (F1), **Accumulator sizing (gas law)** (F2) and **O-ring and seal gland** (F3). Ctrl+K finds each by name, in the category Fluid Power. | |
| 7 | Run `dotnet test tests/Tempest.Calc.Tests.FluidPower --configuration Release --no-build` and `dotnet test tests/Tempest.Core.Tests --configuration Release --no-build --filter "FullyQualifiedName~DisciplineWorkspaceTests"`. | Pass (FluidPower 97 cases, of which `FluidPowerDisciplineTests` 8). The Core filter includes the Fluid Power attribute and restart tests. | |

#### 4.9.1 Long form T-F2: Accumulator sizing (gas law)

**Written for version 1.3.1; brought to version 1.7.0 on 2026-10-27.** The Addendum C fixes touched this template: `fp.accumulator-R1`, `fp.accumulator-01`, `fp.accumulator-02`, `fp.accumulator-R2`, `fp.accumulator-03`. Every number, name and message below is what version 1.7.0 shows.

F2 sizes a gas-charged accumulator with the shared gas-law kernel (T-REF). It works in absolute pressure and prints `bar a` or `bar g` beside every pressure (the vessel pressure `PS` is `bar g`). On the Absolute basis it blocks a pressure below the atmosphere, which is probably a gauge value. A new input `process` says how the gas is charged. *By duty* (the default) sizes an *Emergency stroke* as a held accumulator, charged slowly (isothermally) to p2 and discharged at n, so `V0 = ΔV·(p2/p0)/[(p2/p1)^(1/n) − 1]`. The other duties use one exponent both ways. The sheet shows the bar·litre value and whether it is below the PSSR Schedule 1 250 bar·L exception; it is an indication only and never a pressure-vessel code check. The pre-charge rules and the size list are your records (section 2.11). F2 is a hard FAIL outside its valid range.

**Inputs from** worked example `clamp-circuit-bladder-accumulator` (`docs\worked-examples\fluid-power\fp.accumulator\clamp-circuit-bladder-accumulator`) — Bladder accumulator for a clamp circuit: 14 L delivered between 100 and 210 bar. Status: Confirmed (analytic; recomputed in python3 in the repository).

A 14 L stroke between 210 bar and 100 bar with a 90 bar pre-charge, n = 1, 30 to 50 °C, charged at 20 °C, in a 230 bar vessel, from a 10 / 20 / 32 / 50 L size list. The duty is *Energy storage* (one exponent both ways); with n = 1 the held-accumulator form gives the same volume. The pressures are absolute. The sheet warns that nitrogen departs from the ideal gas above 200 bar.

The worked example carries a source on every input. Copy its inputs to the clipboard from PowerShell, run from the repository root, then press **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)** (step 3):

```powershell
Get-Content -Raw docs\worked-examples\fluid-power\fp.accumulator\clamp-circuit-bladder-accumulator\inputs.json | Set-Clipboard   # 16 inputs
```

| # | Action | Expected | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Open **Engineering → Modules → Calculations → Worksheets**. Pick **Accumulator sizing (gas law)** in the catalogue (or press Ctrl+K and type its name). | The right pane heading reads **Accumulator sizing (gas law)**. The reference line starts `fp.accumulator@1.7.0  ·  hash`. | |
| 2 | Before you type or import anything, read the governing bar. | The bar reads `FAIL — Real-gas factor against the ideal gas above 100 bar absolute`. This reading comes from the template defaults alone; it is not a result to keep. | |
| 3 | Run the PowerShell line above and press **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)**. Do this first: an import re-seeds the whole form. | Status line reads `Imported 16 input(s).` Nothing is reported as skipped or blocked. | |
| 4 | Check each input against the *Inputs* table of the example's `README.md`. Change nothing. | Every value, unit and source matches. The *Recalculating…* marker clears. | |
| 5 | Compare the **Results** list with the table of expected outputs below. | Each result matches the *Form shows* column. Where the tolerance is tighter than the form's rounding (4 or 5 significant figures), the *Form shows* column is the test. | |
| 6 | Read the governing bar above the results. | The bar reads `PASS — Pre-charge against the p0/p1 rule: utilisation 1.00`. | |
| 7 | Read the checks list under the results (governing first). | PASS — Pre-charge against the p0/p1 rule: 90 bar a vs 90 bar a (u = 1.00); PASS — Pressure ratio against the p2/p0 rule: 2.4873 vs 4 (u = 0.62). | |
| 8 | Read the notices under the checks. | Warnings, not failures. One `Warning — <symbol>: a tabulated value not taken from a reference record (<record kind>); enter it as one so its source, edition and licence travel with it.` for each of `r_0`, `r_p` (Pre-charge rules); `sizes` (Accumulator size list). Also this warning: `Warning — p_2: the highest working pressure is 210 bar absolute, above 200 bar, where nitrogen departs from the ideal gas. The volume shown is too small, so enter f_rg (up to 3) from a gas-property source.`. Record any other notice you see. No *Block* line appears. | |
| 9 | Tick **Show working**. | Numbered steps appear, each as formula, substitution, result, with the clause tag (« …) where the step cites one. **Go to step** jumps to the governing step. | |
| 10 | Variation — Raise the pre-charge. Set `p_0` to 95 bar. Afterwards put `p_0` back to 90 bar. | Governing bar reads `FAIL — Pre-charge against the p0/p1 rule: utilisation 1.06`. `V0` shows 29.99 L, `u_r0` shows 1.06. | |

Afterwards put `p_2` back to 210 bar. | Governing bar reads `Blocked — PS: the vessel's maximum allowable pressure (230 bar gauge) is below the highest working pressure p_2 (248.99 bar gauge). Enter the nameplate or relief-valve setting.` The same text shows as a *Block* line. Nothing is calculated. This is the designed hard stop. | |
| 12 | Variation — Size it as a held accumulator. Set `mode` to `Emergency stroke`, `n_gas` (the gas exponent; the old symbol `n` is its alias) to 1.4 and `T_min` to 50 degC (so there is no temperature scaling). Afterwards put `mode` back to `Energy storage`, `n_gas` to 1 and `T_min` to 30 degC. | `V0` shows 46.74 L (case `ka-held-slow-charge-fast-discharge`, 46.74303967; Unconfirmed, section A.5). `process` reads `charged slowly to p_2 (isothermal, exponent 1), discharged at n_gas = 1.4`. `V_sel` shows 50 L and `u_V` 0.93. Governing bar reads `PASS — Pre-charge against the p0/p1 rule: utilisation 1.00`. Two more warnings appear: `n_gas: the exponent 1.4 is the ideal-gas value, and real nitrogen at 100 to 210 bar absolute is stiffer. An adiabatic discharge takes about 13 to 24 % more gas, so enter f_rg for the discharge path.` and `p_load: enter the lowest pressure the push load needs, or the stroke is not checked against p_1.` | |
| 13 | Variation — Size the same duty with one exponent both ways. With step 12's inputs, set `process` to `Same exponent both ways`. Afterwards put `process` back to `By duty` and the step 12 inputs back. | `V0` shows 36.69 L (case `ka-adiabatic`, 36.69278925) and `u_V` 0.73. A warning reads `process: an emergency stroke is held at p_2 and charged slowly. Ideal-gas volumes: 36.693 L with one exponent both ways, against 46.743 L for a slow charge.` This is the review's High finding G-AB-01 (DB-09): the one-exponent form undersizes a held accumulator. No emergency-stroke sheet was issued under 1.2.0 (DC-21), so none needs a re-run. | |

Afterwards put `p_0` back to 90 bar. | Governing bar reads `Blocked — p_0: 0.5 bar a is below the atmospheric pressure of 1.0132 bar a. That looks like a gauge value, so set p_basis to Gauge or enter the absolute pressure.` Nothing is calculated. Before version 1.3.1 a pre-charge of 0.5 bar absolute ran. | |

#### 4.10.1 Long form T-F1: Cylinder and actuator circuit

**Written for version 1.3.0; brought to version 1.5.0 on 2026-10-27.** The Addendum C fixes touched this template: `fp.cylinder-R1`. Every number, name and message below is what version 1.5.0 shows.

F1 is the first Fluid Power worksheet (the discipline is new: ADR-0170). It gives the cylinder areas and forces, speeds, cycle time, the pump flow and power, the line velocities against your limits, the recommended bore, the orifice flow and a rod-buckling screening. The sheet labels the buckling check "screening only; ISO/TS 13725 method not implemented". Line velocity limits, the hose size list, the mounting factor and the buckling safety factor are your records (section 2.11). F1 is one of four calculators with a hard FAIL outside its valid range (S7, S8, F1, F2).

**Inputs from** worked example `press-ram-125-90-pump-and-lines` (`docs\worked-examples\fluid-power\fp.cylinder\press-ram-125-90-pump-and-lines`) — Press ram: 125/90 cylinder at 180 bar on a 45 cm³/rev pump, lines checked, rod screened for buckling. Status: Confirmed (analytic; recomputed in python3 in the repository).

A 125 / 90 mm × 800 mm press ram at 180 bar, fed by a 45 cm³/rev pump at 1450 rpm (60 L/min circuit flow), pushing 150 kN and pulling 60 kN. The hose list is 16 to 63 mm. The pressure line is judged at the retract discharge, the larger stroke flow.

The worked example carries a source on every input. Copy its inputs to the clipboard from PowerShell, run from the repository root, then press **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)** (step 3):

```powershell
Get-Content -Raw docs\worked-examples\fluid-power\fp.cylinder\press-ram-125-90-pump-and-lines\inputs.json | Set-Clipboard   # 30 inputs
```

#### 4.11.1 Long form T-F3: O-ring and seal gland

**Written for version 1.2.0; brought to version 1.5.0 on 2026-10-27.** The Addendum C fixes touched this template: `fp.oring-gland-01`, `fp.oring-gland-02`, `fp.oring-gland-03`, `fp.oring-gland-R1`. Every number, name and message below is what version 1.5.0 shows.

F3 checks an O-ring gland: squeeze, volume-based fill, installed stretch over temperature, extrusion gap, each as worst case, RSS and a Monte Carlo (20 000 samples, seed 42). It uses the shared tolerance-stack engine (T-REF) and accepts measured groove distributions from W2 (T-W2, T-C7). The ring size and tolerance, the squeeze/fill/stretch bands and the permitted extrusion gap are your records (AS568 / ISO 3601 sizes, Parker limits; section 5.4). The sheet warns until you enter a permitted gap. No AS568, ISO 3601 or Parker chart data ships.

**Inputs from** worked example `locking-cylinder-piston-seal` (`docs\worked-examples\fluid-power\fp.oring-gland\locking-cylinder-piston-seal`) — Static piston O-ring seal of a 63 mm locking cylinder: squeeze, fill, stretch and extrusion over tolerance, temperature and swell. Status: Confirmed (analytic; recomputed in python3 in the repository).

A static piston seal: 63 mm bore, 57.5 mm groove root, 3.5 mm cross-section on a 56 mm ring, 4.7 mm groove width, −25 to 70 °C, 160 bar, 70 Shore A, normal distributions with k = 3.

The worked example carries a source on every input. Copy its inputs to the clipboard from PowerShell, run from the repository root, then press **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)** (step 3):

```powershell
Get-Content -Raw docs\worked-examples\fluid-power\fp.oring-gland\locking-cylinder-piston-seal\inputs.json | Set-Clipboard   # 39 inputs
```

#### 4.12.1 Long form T-S6: Column base plate (EN 1993-1-8)

**Written for version 1.1.1; brought to version 1.8.0 on 2026-10-27.** The Addendum C fixes touched this template: `struct.base-plate-01`, `struct.base-plate-02`, `struct.base-plate-03`, `struct.base-plate-E1`, `struct.base-plate-R1`. Every number, name and message below is what version 1.8.0 shows.

**Inputs from** known-answer case `ka-ih-moment-bolts` — The 260×256 I-section with N = 300 kN, M = 100 kN·m, V = 80 kN; two bolts a row, bolt rows at 165 mm from the axis, no prying. Status: Confirmed (analytic; see Appendix A for what is and is not checked against a primary source).

260×256 I-section on a 400×400×20 plate, S275, C25/30, N 300 kN, M 100 kN·m, V 80 kN, two bolts a row at 165 mm, no prying. Three values differ from a new sheet: `alpha` (the case uses 1.5, the form opens on 1), and `m` and `l_eff`, which have no default at all and must be typed (the sheet is blocked until you do). Check the rest against the list below.

This calculator demands an independent check (DA-12). Whoever created the calculation, or set or changed any of its inputs, cannot check it. See the last step and T-IND. Where no second person is available, the preparer may check it alone by ticking **Self-check (no second person available)** and writing why they stand by it (DC-21): the statement is kept on the calculation, printed on the issued sheet as `Self-checked by the preparer: …` and recorded in the audit trail (T-IND, steps I6 to I8).

| 9 | Variation — Overload the base. Set `M_Ed` to 150 kN*m. | Governing bar reads `FAIL — Tension at the bolt row against the plate T-stub resistance: utilisation 1.41`. Bolt tension FAIL (u = 1.41). Compression flange bearing FAIL (u = 1.16). Shear PASS (u = 0.37). Concrete bearing PASS (u = 0.21). `T_Ed` shows 396.34 kN. `t_req` shows the text `bolts govern (T_Ed exceeds n_t·F_t,Rd at the 100 mm end of the range, so no thickness helps)`. | |
| 10 | Put any changed value back to the case value. Type a name in **Calculation name**. Press **Save as calculation**, then **Record check** as the same signed-in person. | The check is refused: `'<name>' needs an independent check: sign in as someone other than its author (<your sign-in name>) to check it, or, where no second person is available, tick "Self-check (no second person available)" and say why you stand by it.` If nobody is signed in the text reads `… nobody is signed in, so there is no one to check it.` Either way the refusal comes before any other check gate. T-IND shows the second person succeeding. | |
| 11 | Press **Save as calculation**. The **Attach external report…** button appears for a saved draft. Press it and pick any small PDF (the picker offers PDF files only). Use a dummy file: this tests the mechanism, not an anchorage design. | Status reads `'<file>.pdf' is filed on the calculation's Evidence and cited by its input.` The `profis_ref` field now holds the file name with source *evidence*. The warning that the anchorage is designed outside this sheet no longer appears, and a check line `Anchorage designed externally (PROFIS report attached as Evidence)` reads PASS. Typing a made-up reference with source *evidence* does not do this: the Library refuses an evidence source that does not point at a file attached to the calculation's own Evidence. | |

### 4.13 T-IND: the independent check (E4, E5 and S6)

E4, E5 and S6 refuse a check by anyone who created the calculation or changed an input (DA-12). This script needs two signed-in people. The Guided test has no step for it. Do it by hand.

This script needs two signed-in people. **Settings → Switch person…** lists the released people the People directory knows a sign-in identity for, and asks you to confirm by name (there is no password). The v0.21.0 owner runbook (`docs/releases/v0.21.0/PO Test Runbook.md`, B2 and C13) adds a person under Engineering → Reference data → *Add a person*, then **Release**. I did not establish whether a person added that way carries a sign-in identity. If **Switch person…** lists nobody, record `B` on the steps that need it and rely on `IndependentCheckTests` and the `BlueRoundTests` cases named below.

E5 is used because its worked example carries a source on every input, so a second person's check is not stopped by the source gate. Person A is whoever is signed in now; person B is the second person.

| # | Action | Expected | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| I1 | As A, open **Fault protection (BS 7671)**. Copy `docs\worked-examples\electrical\elec.fault-protection\workshop-radial-c16\inputs.json` to the clipboard and press **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)**. Name it `IND check`. **Save as calculation**. Press **Record check**. | Status reads `Imported 19 input(s).` then the save line. The check is refused: `'IND check' needs an independent check: sign in as someone other than its author (<A>) to check it, or, where no second person is available, tick "Self-check (no second person available)" and say why you stand by it.` Governing bar before the save reads `FAIL — Fault current against the disconnection current` (README). Since `elec.fault-protection` 1.3.0 the conductor resistance carries `k_R` 1.125, so this example's loop impedance (1.4034 Ω) is above its 1.3656 Ω limit. A failed check does not stop a check; only a block, a stale link or a missing source does. | |
| I2 | Settings → **Switch person…** → B → confirm. Return to the worksheet form, pick `IND check` in **Saved calculations**, press **Open**, then **Record check**. | Status reads `Checked. Issue when ready.` B did not create or edit the inputs. | |
| I3 | As A again (switch back), import the same JSON, name the sheet `IND edit`, **Save as calculation**. Switch to B. Open `IND edit`. Change `Z_e` to 0.7 ohm and press **Save changes**. Press **Record check**. | Status reads `Saved.` The governing bar now reads `PASS — Loop impedance against the maximum: utilisation 0.95`. Then the check is refused: `'IND edit' needs an independent check: sign in as someone other than anyone who set up or changed its inputs (<A>, <B>) to check it, or, where no second person is available, tick "Self-check (no second person available)" and say why you stand by it.` B edited an input, so B is now an author. | |
| I4 | Switch to A and press **Record check** on `IND edit`. | Refused with the same text. The set of authors travels with each revision. A third person is needed. Record `B` if you cannot add one. | |
| I5 | Automated: run the command in the block under this table. | Pass. The cases include `R_A_05_Whoever_Changed_The_Inputs_Cannot_Check_Them_…`, `R_A_05_The_Pack_Check_Refuses_Whoever_Changed_A_Members_Inputs`, `R_A_11_Nobody_Signed_In_Is_Not_An_Independent_Checker` and the two `P_A_03` cases: a person who only presses **Update to revision n** (re-pins a link) is **not** made an author by it. | |
| I6 | As A, import the JSON again, name it `IND self` and **Save as calculation**. Under the Results panel find **Self-check**: the caption says the calculation needs an independent check and that you may check your own work where no second person is available. Press **Record check** with the box unticked. | Refused: `'IND self' needs an independent check: sign in as someone other than its author (<A>) to check it, or, where no second person is available, tick "Self-check (no second person available)" and say why you stand by it.` The Self-check panel is shown only for E4, E5 and S6 drafts; open M4 and it is absent. | |
| I7 | Tick **Self-check (no second person available)**, type `Looks fine.` in the statement box and press **Record check**. Then replace the statement with at least 20 characters saying what you checked (for example `No second engineer available; Zs re-derived by hand, agrees.`) and press **Record check** again. | First: `'IND self': the self-check statement needs at least 20 characters saying why the preparer stands by the calculation.` Second: `Checked by the preparer (self-check recorded). Issue when ready.` The Self-check panel disappears (the calculation is no longer a draft). The statement box takes no more than 500 characters. | |
| I8 | Press **Issue** (reference `IND-1`, client `Test`). Open the issued sheet. | Under the Checker line the sheet prints `Self-checked by the preparer: <your statement>`, in the text and in the PDF. The audit trail holds a `Calc.SelfChecked` row naming the calculation, you and the statement. A calculation checked by B (I2) prints no such line. The packs panel offers the same box (**Self-check (no second person available)** under the pack check fields) for a pack holding an E4, E5 or S6 calculation you prepared; the statement is kept on the pack, printed on the sheets of the members it is true of — those that demand an independent check and that you prepared; a member prepared by B, or whose template never asked for one, prints no line — and recorded as `Calc.PackSelfChecked` (naming those members). In **Saved calculations** the self-checked calculation reads `… · Checked · SELF-CHECKED` and the pack `… · Checked · SELF-CHECKED · n calculation(s)`, the way a stale one reads ` · STALE`. If B ticks the box on your calculation or your pack, B is refused: `'<name>': you are not its preparer; untick "Self-check (no second person available)" to record your check as an independent check.` (for a pack: `Pack '<title>': you are not the preparer of any calculation in it that needs an independent check; untick …`). S8 lifting offers no self-check (DB-13). | |

Command for step I5:

```powershell
dotnet test tests/Tempest.Calc.Tests.Library --configuration Release --no-build --filter "FullyQualifiedName~IndependentCheckTests|FullyQualifiedName~BlueRoundTests|FullyQualifiedName~SelfCheckTests"
```

#### 4.14.1 Long form T-S7: Lateral-torsional buckling and N+M interaction (EN 1993-1-1)

**Written for version 1.1.0; brought to version 1.3.2 on 2026-10-27.** The Addendum C fixes touched this template: `struct.ltb-interaction-01`, `struct.ltb-interaction-R2`, `struct.ltb-interaction-R1`, `struct.ltb-interaction-R3`. Every number, name and message below is what version 1.3.2 shows.

S7 checks lateral-torsional buckling, flexural buckling and the N+M interaction of a doubly-symmetric member between fork supports. The edition switch (2005+A1:2014 or 2022) changes the printed label only: the arithmetic is that of 2005+A1:2014, and the sheet says so. The Annex A and Annex B coefficients are typed from the published expressions and are **Unconfirmed** (section A.5, U-14 to U-17, and the worked example in section A.7): check them against your copy before you rely on the interaction factors. The imperfection factors, `C_1`, `C_2` and `k_c` are your Eurocode National Annex records (section 2.11); the example enters them as assumptions. The section properties are the S1 section sheet's for a UB 457×191×98; S1 and S2 can supply them by link in your own work.

**Inputs from** worked example `ub-457x191x98-beam-column` (`docs\worked-examples\structural\struct.ltb-interaction\ub-457x191x98-beam-column`) — Beam-column UB 457×191×98 S355, 5 m between restraints: LTB, flexural buckling and N+M interaction. Status: Unconfirmed (analytic; the Annex B coefficients are to be checked; see section A.5).

A beam-column between restraints 5 m apart carrying 400 kN compression with end moments of 340 and 170 kN·m in single curvature and a 6 kN·m minor-axis moment. Equation 6.62 of the interaction governs.

The worked example carries a source on every input. Copy its inputs to the clipboard from PowerShell, run from the repository root, then press **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)** (step 3):

```powershell
Get-Content -Raw docs\worked-examples\structural\struct.ltb-interaction\ub-457x191x98-beam-column\inputs.json | Set-Clipboard   # 46 inputs
```

| # | Action | Expected | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Open **Engineering → Modules → Calculations → Worksheets**. Pick **Lateral-torsional buckling and N+M interaction (EN 1993-1-1)** in the catalogue (or press Ctrl+K and type its name). | The right pane heading reads **Lateral-torsional buckling and N+M interaction (EN 1993-1-1)**. The reference line starts `struct.ltb-interaction@1.3.2  ·  hash`. | |
| 2 | Before you type or import anything, read the governing bar. | The bar reads `FAIL — Interaction equation 6.62 against its limit: utilisation 1.20`. This reading comes from the template defaults alone; it is not a result to keep. | |
| 3 | Run the PowerShell line above and press **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)**. Do this first: an import re-seeds the whole form. | Status line reads `Imported 46 input(s).` Nothing is reported as skipped or blocked. | |
| 4 | Check each input against the *Inputs* table of the example's `README.md`. Change nothing. | Every value, unit and source matches. The *Recalculating…* marker clears. | |
| 5 | Compare the **Results** list with the table of expected outputs below. | Each result matches the *Form shows* column. Where the tolerance is tighter than the form's rounding (4 or 5 significant figures), the *Form shows* column is the test. | |

One `Warning — <symbol>: a tabulated value not taken from a reference record (<record kind>); enter it as one so its source, edition and licence travel with it.` for each of `E`, `G`, `f_y` (Material properties); `gamma_M1` (EN 1993-1-1 National Annex factors); `alpha_a0`, `alpha_a`, `alpha_b`, `alpha_c`, `alpha_d`, `lambda_LT0`, `beta_LT` (Imperfection factors and 6.3.2.3 parameters); `C_2` (C₁, C₂ and k_c for the moment diagram). Record any other notice you see. No *Block* line appears. | |

Afterwards put `L` back to 5 m, `L_cr_y` back to 5 m, `L_cr_z` back to 5 m. | Governing bar reads `FAIL — Interaction equation 6.62 against its limit: utilisation 1.32`. `Mcr` shows 502.08 kN·m, `chi_LT` shows 0.52793, `Mb_Rd` shows 406.61 kN·m, `u_LTB` shows 0.84, `u_62` shows 1.32. Warning: `L_cr_z: non-dimensional slenderness 2.0864 exceeds 2.0.` | |
| 11 | Variation — Add axial load. Set `N_Ed` to -1500 kN. Afterwards put `N_Ed` back to -400 kN. | Governing bar reads `FAIL — Interaction equation 6.62 against its limit: utilisation 1.63`. `u_buckling` shows 1.01, `u_62` shows 1.63. The flexural buckling check also fails (1500 kN vs 1494.5 kN). | |
| 12 | Variation — Pick the 2022 edition. Set `edition` to 2022. Afterwards put `edition` back to 2005+A1:2014. | Governing bar reads `PASS — Interaction equation 6.62 against its limit: utilisation 0.93`. `edition_used` shows "EN 1993-1-1:2022 (arithmetic of 2005+A1:2014)", `u_62` shows 0.93. Warning: `edition: the 2022 choice changes only the printed label, not the arithmetic of EN 1993-1-1:2005+A1:2014. Check the 2022 clauses before relying on this sheet.` The numbers do not change. | |
| 13 | Variation — Type the end moments with opposite signs, as a frame program prints single curvature. Set `M_y_B` to -170 kN*m (it is 170). Afterwards put it back to 170. | A warning reads `M_y_B: opposite signs (340 and -170 kN·m) make this double curvature, so enter same-sign ordinates for single curvature. This uses C₁ = 2.7, C_my = 0.4.` A second warning reads `C_1: the ψ formula gives C₁ = 2.7 for ψ = -0.5, up to 10 % above the exact value. Check C₁ in an elastic buckling program and enter it.` Governing bar reads `PASS — Interaction equation 6.62 against its limit: utilisation 0.69` (0.93 before). It still passes: that is the silent slip the warning exists for. It is a warning only. | |

#### 4.17.1 Long form T-M4: Shaft design

**Written for version 1.1.0; brought to version 1.7.0 on 2026-10-27.** The Addendum C fixes touched this template: `mech.shaft-01`, `mech.shaft-02`, `mech.shaft-03`, `mech.shaft-04`, `mech.shaft-05`. Every number, name and message below is what version 1.7.0 shows.

**Inputs from** known-answer case `ka-simply-supported` — Uniform shaft, central load and mass: slope, deflection, critical speed. Status: Confirmed (analytic; see Appendix A for what is and is not checked against a primary source).

This is a deliberate FAIL: a uniform 30 mm shaft with a 2 kN load and the form's default limits (slope 0.001 rad, deflection 0.13 mm) breaks both limits. Fatigue and yield pass. The stated limits are the form defaults, not requirements.

**Values to enter** (a new sheet opens on the declared defaults only; caption is `symbol — description`):

| Input | Value | Unit | On a new sheet |
|---|---|---|---|
| `x_A` — Bearing A position | 0 | mm | already set |

#### 4.22.1 Long form T-M7: Helical springs

**Written for version 1.2.0; brought to version 1.6.0 on 2026-10-27.** The Addendum C fixes touched this template: `mech.spring-01`, `mech.spring-R1`. Every number, name and message below is what version 1.6.0 shows.

M7 sits in the Mechanical discipline. This script covers the compression mode, the only mode that has a worked example. The extension and torsion modes have known-answer cases (`ka-extension`, `ka-torsion`) in the automated suite (`SpringTests`, T-AUTO below). R_m(d), the permissible stress fractions, the endurance strength and the seating coefficient have no default: they are your records (section 2.11). The example enters them as assumptions.

**Inputs from** worked example `plunger-return-spring` (`docs\worked-examples\mechanical\mech.spring\plunger-return-spring`) — Plunger return spring: squared and ground compression spring, 60 to 180 N. Status: Confirmed (analytic; recomputed in python3 in the repository).

A 3 mm wire at 24 mm mean diameter with 9 active coils, squared and ground ends, working between 60 N and 180 N. The EN 13906-1 solid length (maximum wire diameter, minimum coil clearance) is selected, so the coil-clearance check governs.

The worked example carries a source on every input. Copy its inputs to the clipboard from PowerShell, run from the repository root, then press **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)** (step 3):

```powershell
Get-Content -Raw docs\worked-examples\mechanical\mech.spring\plunger-return-spring\inputs.json | Set-Clipboard   # 23 inputs
```

| # | Action | Expected | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Open **Engineering → Modules → Calculations → Worksheets**. Pick **Helical springs** in the catalogue (or press Ctrl+K and type its name). | The right pane heading reads **Helical springs**. The reference line starts `mech.spring@1.6.0  ·  hash`. | |
| 2 | Before you type or import anything, read the governing bar. | The bar reads `Blocked — S_e: enter a finite number.`. A blocked bar names the next thing to enter. This reading comes from the template defaults alone; it is not a result to keep. | |
| 3 | Run the PowerShell line above and press **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)**. Do this first: an import re-seeds the whole form. | Status line reads `Imported 23 input(s).` Nothing is reported as skipped or blocked. | |
| 4 | Check each input against the *Inputs* table of the example's `README.md`. Change nothing. | Every value, unit and source matches. The *Recalculating…* marker clears. | |
| 5 | Compare the **Results** list with the table of expected outputs below. | Each result matches the *Form shows* column. Where the tolerance is tighter than the form's rounding (4 or 5 significant figures), the *Form shows* column is the test. | |
| 6 | Read the governing bar above the results. | The bar reads `PASS — Working deflection against the travel to the minimum length, keeping the coil clearance at L₂: utilisation 0.88`. | |
| 7 | Read the checks list under the results (governing first). | PASS — Working deflection against the travel to the minimum length, keeping the coil clearance at L₂: 27.89 mm vs 31.71 mm (u = 0.88); PASS — Shear stress at solid length against the permissible stress: 633.7 MPa vs 742.5 MPa (u = 0.85); PASS — Goodman fatigue against the required safety factor: 1.2 vs 1.5579 (u = 0.77); PASS — Working deflection against the travel to solid: 27.89 mm vs 37 mm (u = 0.75); PASS — Working deflection against the buckling deflection: stable at any deflection vs λ_eff² < C₂′. | |
| 8 | Read the notices under the checks. | Warnings, not failures. One `Warning — <symbol>: a tabulated value not taken from a reference record (<record kind>); enter it as one so its source, edition and licence travel with it.` for each of `G`, `E`, `rho` (Material properties); `Rm` (Wire tensile strength R_m(d)); `f_tau`, `f_su` (Permissible and ultimate stress fractions); `nu_seat` (Seating (end-support coefficient)); `S_e` (Endurance strength). Record any other notice you see. No *Block* line appears. | |
| 9 | Tick **Show working**. | Numbered steps appear, each as formula, substitution, result, with the clause tag (« …) where the step cites one. **Go to step** jumps to the governing step. | |
| 10 | Variation — Load the spring past its coil clearance. Set `F_2` to 220 N. Afterwards put `F_2` back to 180 N. | Governing bar reads `FAIL — Working deflection against the travel to the minimum length, keeping the coil clearance at L₂: utilisation 1.08`. Checks: that one FAIL (u = 1.08); PASS — Goodman fatigue against the required safety factor (u = 0.97); PASS — Working deflection against the travel to solid (u = 0.92); PASS — Shear stress at solid length against the permissible stress (u = 0.85); PASS — Working deflection against the buckling deflection (a yes/no check). `s_2` shows 34.10 mm, `u_n` shows 1.08, `u_c` shows 0.85. | |
| 11 | Variation — Run the spring faster. Set `f_op` to 20 Hz. Afterwards put `f_op` back to 8 Hz. | Governing bar reads `PASS — Working deflection against the travel to the minimum length, keeping the coil clearance at L₂: utilisation 0.88`. `f_n` shows 207.00 Hz, `r_f` shows 10.349. Warning: `f_op: the surge frequency is 10.35 times the operating frequency, below 13, so the spring may resonate.` | |

**Torsion mode.** The worked example has no torsion spring. In torsion mode an expected working travel `s_work` is not used: the travel check applies to compression and extension only. The sheet now says so with the warning `s-work-unused` instead of ignoring the value. `SpringTests` covers it. The permissible stress fractions `f_tau`, `f_sigma` and `f_su` default to generic textbook values (0.45, 0.75 and 0.67); the sheet warns until you apply your EN 13906 record.

## Part 5 — The chains

A chain links calculators. One calculator's output becomes another's input. A change upstream marks everything downstream stale. A stale calculation cannot be checked or issued until you update it. Part 5 tests the links, the stale marker and the pack check and issue.

There are 12 built-in packs and the workflows for chains 2 and 3. The demo (Part 3) built all 12 packs for you to look at. In Part 5 you build them again, one in each new project, and test them.

### 5.1 How the chain scripts work

**How the chain scripts work.** Each script runs in the **Calculation packs** panel of the worksheet form. You need an open project. **Use a new project for each chain script**: calculation names are unique in a project, and several chains share step names (for example `Motor sizing` and `Enclosure heat`). A second pack in the same project is refused with `The project already holds a calculation called '<name>' (step '<key>'); … cannot be instantiated here twice, or beside a calculation of that name. Rename the existing one first.`

The pack templates are built in and carry example inputs. Every example input is recorded with the source `assumption: example input — replace with your own`, so the packs can be checked. The values are the repository's invented examples (see each pack's README under `docs\worked-examples\packs\`). They are not designs.

The generic steps are the same for all five. Each script lists the names, texts and numbers that differ.

- **Create.** Pick the pack template in **Pack template**, type the **Pack title**, press **Create pack from template**. Status reads `Pack '<title>' created from '<template>' with <n> linked calculation(s). Open any of them from the list, then check and issue the pack.` The pack is selected in **Packs in this project** as `<title> · rev A · Draft · <n> calculation(s)`. **Calculations in the selected pack** lists the members as `<name> · <template title> · rev A · Draft`. The suggested issue reference is `<title with spaces as hyphens>-A` and the client is the project name.
- **Look at a member.** Pick it in **Calculations in the selected pack** and press **Open pack member**. Status reads `Opened '<name>' · rev A · Draft.` A linked input shows source `link`. The *Link* panel and the banner are described in T-L1.
- **Break an upstream input.** Open the member named in the script, change the input, press **Save changes** (`Saved.`).
- **See STALE.** The Saved calculations list and the members list show ` · STALE` in amber on every step that depends on the change, directly or through another step. Open one: the orange `Stale — …` banner gives the reasons and **Update linked inputs** appears.
- **Check pack while stale.** Set **Checker's organisation** `Tempest Engineering` and **Check statement** `Checked in the worksheet form.` (the form suggests both), leave the outcome on *Accepted*, press **Check pack**. It is refused with the text in the script. **Issue pack** stays greyed out while the pack is a draft.
- **Update in order.** Open the first stale step, press **Update linked inputs** (`Linked inputs updated to the current revisions and recalculated.`). Press **Check pack** again. The refusal now names the next step that is still stale. A step that depended only on a step you just updated clears by itself and needs no press. Repeat until **Check pack** succeeds.
- **Check.** Status reads `Pack checked by <signed-in person>. Issue it when ready.` The pack shows `Checked`. **Reopen pack** becomes available (see T-C1).
- **Issue.** Check that **Issue reference** and **Client** are filled, press **Issue pack**. Status reads `Pack issued as '<reference>'. Its sheet and every calculation are attached to the pack's Evidence.` Open a member: the watermark reads ISSUED and the form is read-only.

#### 5.1.1 The ten Addendum B chains

Chains 1, 4, 5 and 7 to 10 are built-in pack templates (T-C6, T-C9, T-C10, T-C12 to T-C15). Chains 2 and 3 are workflows through the import panels (T-C7, T-C8). Chain 6 is run by hand with the S8 flag on (T-C11). The generic steps are those of section 5.1: create, open a member, break an upstream input, see STALE, check while stale, update in order, check, issue. **Use a new project for each chain script**, for the reason section 5.1 gives. T-C6 is different: the form refuses to create that pack until you supply your cable tables, as it does for the motor circuit (T-C2). The pack READMEs are under `docs\worked-examples\packs\`. Their values are invented and are not designs.

### 5.2 T-L1: result linking by hand

Do this first. It links two calculations yourself in the form, then walks the stale marker and the revision update. The guided test has no step for it. A chain does the same at pack scale. Part D lists the automated proofs. The numbers use the worked examples of E1 and M4. The test values hold their current outputs. The M4 template takes the steady torque `T` and a separate alternating torque `T_a` (C-1). The numbers below were run on the release head on 2026-10-27.

This script links two calculations yourself in the form, then walks the stale marker and the revision update. The five chains (T-C1 to T-C5) do the same at pack scale. The worked examples carry sources for every input, so the calculations can be checked and issued. Their inputs load by import. Run these from the repository root:

```powershell
Get-Content -Raw docs\worked-examples\electrical\elec.motor-sizing\ball-screw-pick-and-place\inputs.json | Set-Clipboard   # E1: 20 inputs
Get-Content -Raw docs\worked-examples\mechanical\mech.shaft\gearbox-input-shaft\inputs.json | Set-Clipboard                  # M4: 26 inputs
```

**Part A. Bind, dimension safety and cycle.**

| # | Action | Expected | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| A1 | In an open project open **Motor and actuator sizing**. Copy the E1 JSON (first line above) and press **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)**. Name it `L1 motor`. **Save as calculation**. | Status `Imported 20 input(s).` then `Saved as 'L1 motor' …`. Results include `T_rms` 1.440 N·m, `n_pk` 9000 rpm, `T_pk` 2.579 N·m and `P_rms` 0.7841 kW. Governing bar `PASS — RMS torque with the safety factor against the rated torque: utilisation 0.90`. | |
| A2 | Open **Shaft design**. Copy the M4 JSON (second line) and import it. Name it `L1 shaft`. **Save as calculation**. | Status `Imported 26 input(s).` then `Saved as 'L1 shaft' …`. `R_A` 3.195 kN and `n_f_min` 1.6908. Governing bar `PASS — Slope at the bearings against limit: utilisation 0.90`. | |

Press **Link input**. | Status `Linked T to T_rms; it is pinned to that calculation's current revision.` Field `T` shows about 0.0014402 kN·m (the form converts N·m to the input's unit). Its source shows `link`. | |
| A4 | Link `n` to `L1 motor` output `n_pk`. | Status `Linked n to n_pk; …`. `n` shows 9000 rpm. `n_f_min` rises to 2.1017; the fatigue check reads u = 0.71. | |
| A5 | Pick input `n`, calculation `L1 motor`, output `T_rms`. Press **Link input**. | Refused: `'L1 motor'.T_rms is in N·m (m²·kg/s²); 'n' needs rpm (1/s). The dimensions differ, so the link is refused.` The link on `n` is unchanged. | |

Press **Link input**. | Refused: `Linking 'F_p' to 'L1 shaft' would close a cycle: 'L1 shaft' already takes a value, directly or through others, from 'L1 motor' (or another revision of it).` | |

**Part D. Automated proofs of the §A4 requirements.**

```powershell
dotnet test tests/Tempest.Calc.Tests.Library --configuration Release --no-build --filter "FullyQualifiedName~LinkingTests|FullyQualifiedName~CellLinkTests|FullyQualifiedName~Chains|FullyQualifiedName~CalcInstanceServiceTests|FullyQualifiedName~BlueRoundTests"
dotnet test tests/Tempest.Desktop.Tests --configuration Release --no-build --filter "FullyQualifiedName~WorksheetLinkingTests|FullyQualifiedName~WorksheetSavedAndPacksTests"
```

| D2 | Dimension mismatch refused; self-link refused; cycle refused | `LinkingTests.A_Binding_Across_Dimensions_Or_To_Itself_Or_Round_A_Cycle_Is_Refused` | Pass | |
| D3 | Chain depth capped at 20 | `LinkingTests.A_Chain_Longer_Than_Twenty_Links_Is_Refused` | Pass | |
| D4 | Stale propagates through the chain; stale blocks Check until updated | `LinkingTests.A_Changed_Upstream_Makes_Everything_Downstream_Stale_And_Stale_Blocks_Check_Until_Updated` | Pass | |
| D5 | Link survives revise and supersede; stale blocks Issue; "update to revision" gives a new revision | `LinkingTests.A_Revised_Upstream_Stales_Its_Downstream_Whose_Link_Survives_And_Updating_Gives_A_New_Revision` | Pass | |
| D6 | Pack issues as one immutable bundle | `LinkingTests.A_Pack_Template_Builds_A_Linked_Pack_Checked_Once_And_Issued_As_One_Immutable_Bundle` | Pass | |
| D7 | Crash mid-issue (single calculation and pack) | `CalcInstanceServiceTests.Crash_Mid_Issue_Leaves_Instance_Checked_And_Evidence_Checked` and `.Pack_Crash_Mid_Issue_Leaves_Every_Member_Unissued` | Pass | |
| D8 | Table-cell links; a checked calculation gone stale is revised, not stranded; a checked pack gone stale is reopened | `CellLinkTests` (19), `BlueRoundTests.R_A_04_A_Checked_Calculation_Gone_Stale_Is_Revised_Not_Stranded`, `.R_A_04_A_Checked_Pack_Gone_Stale_Is_Reopened_Corrected_And_Issued` | Pass | |
| D9 | No check or issue on a draft upstream unless both go in one pack | `BlueRoundTests.R_A_02_*` (2 tests) | Pass | |
| D10 | Each chain runs end to end; revising an upstream stales every step below it | `DriveTrainChainTests`, `WetHeatingChainTests`, `CoolingChainTests`, `SteelSupportChainTests`, `MotorCircuitChainTests` | Pass | |
| D11 | The form shows the stale banner and **Update linked inputs** | Desktop `WorksheetLinkingTests.An_Input_Linked_In_The_Form_Goes_Stale_When_Its_Upstream_Changes_And_Updates_Back` | Pass | |
| D12 | The list shows STALE; packs are created, checked, reopened and issued from the form | Desktop `WorksheetSavedAndPacksTests` (15 tests) | Pass | |

| T-C7 | Scan-to-seal (chain 2) | workflow: W2 → M1 → F3 | none | `import-w2` | none |

| T-C11 | Lifting beam (chain 6) | worksheets only; flag on | none | `lifting-3` | none |

The bracket-assembly pack (M2, M3, S1) is a test-only example of the pack path. It is not a pack template.

Use one new project for each chain. Do the drive train first. It covers every pack button. Track P puts the chain files after the single-calculator files. Do T-C9 early among the Addendum B chains. It covers the pack buttons with a blocked step.

#### 5.4.1 Long form T-C1

**Written before C-1; brought to the release head on 2026-10-27.** The Addendum C fixes touched this chain: `chain.drive-train-01`, `chain.drive-train-02`, `chain.drive-train-05`, `chain.drive-train-D1`, `chain.drive-train-03`, `chain.drive-train-C1`, `chain.drive-train-06`, `chain.drive-train-07`. Every number, name and message below is what the release head shows.

**Chain.** E1 motor sizing → M4 shaft → M5 bearing life → M2 seat fit; E1 → M3 coupling flange bolts. Pack template **Drive train** (`pack.drive-train`). README: `docs\worked-examples\packs\drive-train\README.md`.

| Member | Template | Checks when created |
|---|---|---|
| Motor sizing | Motor and actuator sizing | 5 PASS |
| Shaft | Shaft design | 5 PASS |
| Bearing A life | Rolling bearing life (ISO 281) | 2 PASS |
| Bearing A seat fit | Limits and fits (ISO 286) | 1 PASS |
| Coupling flange bolts | Bolt group (elastic method) | 4 PASS |

**Values that flow down each link.** The *Before* column is what the pack shows when created. The *After* column is what it shows once you have broken the upstream input and updated every step.

| Link | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| Motor sizing `T_load` → Shaft `T` | 0.151579 kN·m | 0.155789 kN·m |
| Motor sizing `T_alt` → Shaft `T_a` | 0 kN·m | 0 kN·m |
| Motor sizing `n_pk` → Shaft `n` | 1475 rpm | 1475 rpm |
| Shaft `R_A` → Bearing A life `F_r` | 1.34604 kN | 1.34604 kN |
| Motor sizing `n_mean` → Bearing A life `n` | 1475 rpm | 1475 rpm |
| Motor sizing `F_ax_pk` → Bearing A life `F_a` | 0 kN | 0 kN |
| Shaft `d_A` → Bearing A seat fit `D` | 35 mm | 35 mm |
| Motor sizing `T_pk` → Shaft `T_pk` | 0.159248 kN·m | 0.163458 kN·m |
| Motor sizing `T_design` → Coupling flange bolts `M_z` | 0.159248 kN·m | 0.163458 kN·m |

**Coupling design torque (chain.drive-train-D1).** The coupling flange bolts carry the motor step's `T_design`, on the basis you choose in **Motor sizing** (`T_basis`): *Load peak (T_pk)* (the default, so the values above), *Motor peak (T_peak)* (the typed peak torque of the candidate motor), *Service factor × rated (SF_c·T_rated)* (type `SF_c`) or *Protective limit (T_lim)* (type `T_lim`). Choose *Motor peak* and update the stale members: the flange bolts' `M_z` (the old symbol `T_z` is its alias) becomes the typed `T_peak`, the shaft's `T_pk` stays the load peak. Choosing the service-factor or limit basis without `SF_c` or `T_lim` blocks the motor step with a message naming the missing input.

#### 5.5.1 Long form T-C6

**Written before C-1; brought to the release head on 2026-10-27.** The Addendum C fixes touched this chain: `chain.hydraulic-power-unit-01`, `chain.hydraulic-power-unit-02`, `chain.hydraulic-power-unit-03`. Every number, name and message below is what the release head shows.

**Chain.** Cylinder and pump circuit (`fp.cylinder`) → Emergency stroke accumulator, Pressure line, Pump drive motor; Pump drive motor (`elec.motor-sizing`) → Motor supply cable, Pump motor start; Motor supply cable (`elec.conductor-sizing`) → Motor supply voltage drop, Motor supply fault protection, Pump motor start; Motor supply fault protection (`elec.fault-protection`) → Pump motor start. Pack template **Hydraulic power unit** (`pack.hydraulic-power-unit`), 8 steps and 24 links. README: `docs\worked-examples\packs\hydraulic-power-unit\README.md`. Section 5.5.2 turns this script into a run on one of your own jobs.

**Why this one needs your tables.** Like the motor circuit (T-C2), this pack holds a cable step (E4) that needs a cable-capacity (`ampacity`) table and a preferred device-rating (`ratings`) table. None ships (§A5). Three later steps take a value from the cable step (the voltage drop, the fault protection and the motor start), so **Create pack from template** is refused at the cable step and creates nothing. This differs from T-C9, where nothing takes from the cable step and the pack is created with that step blocked. The form's Create passes no inputs of its own, so you cannot give the pack your tables through it. Part A shows the refusal. Part B names the automated proofs. Part C builds the chain by hand, calculation by calculation, which is how you run it on a real job.

**The example is invented.** A 100/70 × 500 mm cylinder at 160 bar, a 28 cm³/rev pump at 1450 rpm, a 12 m line, a candidate motor of 120 N·m at 1460 rpm (an invented nameplate), a 400 kVA, 4 % transformer (invented) and a 30 m cable. The README warns that the cable tables of the example are synthetic test values and that the example must not size a real cable.

**Part A. The refusal (form).**

| # | Action | Expected | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Open a new project. In **Calculation packs** pick **Hydraulic power unit**, type the **Pack title** `Press HPU`, press **Create pack from template**. | Status reads `Step 'e4' is blocked, so 'e3' cannot take 'I_b' from it: ampacity: enter the current-carrying capacities for the installation method from your own licensed table (record kind cable.ampacity). The cable step needs your own cable-capacity (ampacity) and preferred device-rating (ratings) tables: none ship with the program. Enter them from your copy of the wiring regulations and the device manufacturer's data, then create the pack again.` The first step that takes from the cable step is the voltage drop, so the text names `e3`. | |
| 2 | Look at **Saved calculations** and **Packs in this project**. | Both stay empty. The chain runs in memory first, so a refusal leaves no half-made calculations (`PackDryRunTests`). | |

**Part B. Automated proofs.**

```powershell
dotnet test tests/Tempest.Calc.Tests.Library --configuration Release --no-build --filter "FullyQualifiedName~HydraulicPowerUnitChainTests|FullyQualifiedName~PackExampleInputsTests|FullyQualifiedName~ChainPackWorkedExampleTests"
```

| # | Action | Expected | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| 3 | Run the command. | Pass (12 + 13 + 12 tests). The list under this table says what they cover. | |

The tests of step 3 cover:

- The values and units on every link.
- That each binding is a real link from the output it names.
- A bigger cylinder and pump staling every step below, refusing the check and the issue. The update in order carries the values through.
- A re-bored cylinder line staling the pressure line. The update carries the bore into its segment table (the one table-cell link of the chain).
- The stale refusal naming the stale step.
- Check and issue as one bundle once the cable step has an independent check.
- The refusal at the cable step without your tables.
- The refusal naming `n_rated` when the motor's rated speed is missing (the cable is sized on the rated power).
- The chain created when only the cable step is given its tables.
- A smaller supply transformer staling only the motor start.
- A different voltage factor on the fault protection reaching the motor start without a spurious warning.
- The issue staleness gate when the cylinder pressure moves on.

**Part C. Build the chain yourself (owner-led).** The form cannot make a pack from saved calculations, so this part stops at linked, saved calculations and the stale marker. It makes **23 of the 24 links**. The twenty-fourth, the cylinder's line bore `d_prs` into the first row of the pressure line's segment table, is a table-cell link, and the *Link* row of the form makes no cell link. Type that bore (20 mm) into the segment table instead. The automated test in Part B covers the cell link.

Copy each step's input list out of the issued record. The record is one JSON object keyed by step (`f1`, `f2`, `b1`, `e1`, `e4`, `e3`, `e5`, `e9`). In PowerShell, from the repository root:

```powershell
$hpu = Get-Content -Raw docs\worked-examples\packs\hydraulic-power-unit\inputs.json | ConvertFrom-Json
$hpu.f1 | ConvertTo-Json -Depth 10 | Set-Clipboard    # F1: 30 inputs. Change f1 to f2 (16), b1 (15), e1 (28), e4 (21), e3 (15), e5 (23) or e9 (30).
```

If `ConvertTo-Json` flattens a table in your PowerShell version, open `inputs.json` in Notepad and copy the step's array by hand. The links in the record name the original calculations and do not resolve in your project. Make them again with the *Link* row, upstream first. **The cable step's list holds the repository's synthetic `ampacity` and `ratings` tables** (invented values, labelled as such). Use them only to reproduce the numbers below. For a real job, replace both tables with your own (T-REC part 2) before you trust the cable.

#### 5.5.2 Your own hydraulic job through chain 1

This is the most useful test in the pack. It runs one of your own jobs through the hydraulic power unit chain. The Guided test has no step for it.

**Why.** Every Addendum B number so far comes from an invented example or from a hand check. Chain 1 (`pack.hydraulic-power-unit`) is the one chain that runs from a hydraulic circuit all the way to the electrical supply. A job you built and commissioned is the first test where a real unit can tell you the program is wrong. This section asks you to run one such job through the chain and to record what differs.

**Which job.** Steven, pick a past power-unit or cylinder job of your own. The best one has:

- a hydraulic circuit diagram and the cylinder, pump and motor datasheets;
- the cable run, protective device and supply details as built;
- a commissioning record with measured values: working pressure, cycle time, motor current, oil temperature, and any voltage dip, nuisance trip or noisy start;
- an accumulator on the emergency stroke, so that F2 has something to size. With a cylinder-only job, run F1 and B1 and leave F2 out.

If you have two jobs, use one that worked and one that gave trouble. The trouble job is the better test of the checks.

**How.** The form cannot create this pack without your cable tables (T-C6 part A). Build the eight calculations by hand, upstream first, as T-C6 part C does, **but import nothing from the invented example**. Type your own job data. Give every input a source other than *manual* (drawing, spec, evidence or your measurement) so that a single calculation can be checked. Enter your own `ampacity` and `ratings` tables for E4 as licensed records (T-REC part 2). Do not use the repository's synthetic tables for a real cable.

**Before you run it.** Write your expectation of each key output in the table below, from the as-built job. Do this first. It stops you tuning the inputs to match.

**Where each input comes from.**

| Step | Inputs from your job |
|---|---|
| F1 Cylinder and pump circuit | Bore, rod, stroke, supply pressure, push and pull loads, required speed or cycle time, pump displacement and speed, efficiencies, hose and pipe sizes and your velocity limits |
| F2 Accumulator | Pre-charge, lowest and highest working pressure, temperatures, the size list of the maker, gauge or absolute basis, the gas process (by duty: an emergency stroke is charged slowly and discharged at n), the swept volume (it arrives by link from F1) |
| B1 Pressure line | Line length, bore, fittings, oil density and viscosity at the working temperature (the flow, the density and the bore arrive by link, the bore as a typed value in the form) |
| E1 Pump drive motor | Nameplate rated speed (`n_rated`) and torque, rotor inertia, duty. The pump torque and speed arrive by link |
| E4 Motor supply cable | Your cable and device tables, installation method, length, grouping and ambient. The motor's rated power arrives by link |
| E3 Voltage drop | Supply voltage and your drop limit. The current, length and cross-section arrive by link |
| E5 Fault protection | Transformer rating and impedance, measured external loop impedance Z_e, the device curve, the required disconnection time. The cable data arrive by link |
| E9 Motor start | Locked-rotor ratio and starting power factor from the motor datasheet, the starter type (direct, star-delta, soft starter or drive), the dip limit. The fault current, cable and rated power arrive by link |

**Record sheet.** Fill the first columns before the run and the others after. Class each difference as: *program wrong*, *my data wrong*, *model limit* (section C.6) or *expected*.

| Step | Output | My expectation | Program | As built or measured | Source of that figure | Difference | Class |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| F1 | Pump delivery against circuit flow (`u_pump`) | | | | | | |
| F1 | Extend and retract force against the loads | | | | | | |
| F1 | Cycle time `t_cycle` | | | | | | |
| F1 | Line velocities (suction, return, pressure) | | | | | | |
| F1 | Rod buckling utilisation | | | | | | |
| F1 | Shaft torque `T_shaft` and power `P_pump` | | | | | | |
| F2 | Gas volume `V0`, selected size, pre-charge `p0` | | | | | | |
| B1 | Pressure drop in the line and its velocity (note the Reynolds number and any warning) | | | | | | |
| E1 | RMS torque utilisation and `P_rated` | | | | | | |
| E4 | Cross-section, device rating, conductor capacity `I_z` | | | | | | |
| E3 | Voltage drop (volts and per cent) | | | | | | |
| E5 | Loop impedance against its maximum; `I_k3`; disconnection time | | | | | | |
| E9 | Dip at the motor terminals and at the point of common coupling; kvar to correct | | | | | | |

**Run the three what-ifs of T-C6 on your job**, with your own numbers: raise the pressure by 10 %, lengthen the stroke by 20 %, and halve the transformer. Write down which steps go stale and which clear by themselves (the first break stales every step except the pressure line; the second only the accumulator; the third only the motor start). The point is that you can predict the stale list before you look.

**Also record.**

- Which inputs you had to guess or take from a catalogue because no drawing or record held them.
- Every notice, warning and block, with its text. Say which ones told you something you did not know.
- Any check that FAILs where the real unit works, or PASSes where the real unit gave trouble. This is the finding that matters most.
- The links the form made, the links it refused and the stale banners you saw.
- The time the run took, and where it was slow.
- Whether the answer would have changed a decision on the job.

**Keep in mind.** F2 sizes a held accumulator with a slow charge (F2 1.3.1); a sheet issued under 1.2.0 should be re-run. F1 is steady-state: no acceleration, cushioning or compressibility. B1's fittings loss is understated below Reynolds number 4000. E9's dip is a lower bound. F1's rod buckling is a screening (ISO/TS 13725 is not implemented). E4 sizes the cable on the motor's nameplate power, not on the pump's load. The pump is assumed to start unloaded. Use the run to test the program, not as the design basis of the job.
