#### 5.6.1 Long form T-C2

**Written before C-1; brought to the release head on 2026-10-27.** The Addendum C fixes touched this chain: `chain.motor-circuit-02`, `chain.motor-circuit-06`, `chain.motor-circuit-05`, `chain.motor-circuit-04`, `chain.motor-circuit-07`, `chain.motor-circuit-R1`. Every number, name and message below is what the release head shows.

**Chain.** E1 motor sizing → E6 load budget → E4 cable and device → E3 voltage drop and E5 fault protection → E2 enclosure heat. Pack template **Motor circuit** (`pack.motor-circuit`). README: `docs\worked-examples\packs\motor-circuit\README.md`.

**Why this one is different.** The E4 step needs a cable-capacity (`ampacity`) table and a preferred device-rating (`ratings`) table. None ships with the program (§A5). The pack template carries example inputs for every step except those two tables, so **Create pack from template** is refused at the cable step and says so. The form's Create passes no inputs of its own, so you cannot supply the tables through it. Parts B and C show what you can do instead.

**Part A. The refusal (form).**

| # | Action | Expected | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Open a new project. In **Calculation packs** pick **Motor circuit**, type the **Pack title** `Servo axis motor circuit`, press **Create pack from template**. | Status reads `Step 'e4' is blocked, so 'e3' cannot take 'I_b' from it: ampacity: enter the current-carrying capacities for the installation method from your own licensed table (record kind cable.ampacity). The cable step needs your own cable-capacity (ampacity) and preferred device-rating (ratings) tables: none ship with the program. Enter them from your copy of the wiring regulations and the device manufacturer's data, then create the pack again.` | |
| 2 | Look at **Saved calculations** and **Packs in this project**. | Both stay empty. The chain is run in memory first, so a refusal leaves no half-made calculations (`PackDryRunTests`). | |

**Part B. Automated proofs.**

```powershell
dotnet test tests/Tempest.Calc.Tests.Library --configuration Release --no-build --filter "FullyQualifiedName~MotorCircuitChainTests|FullyQualifiedName~PackExampleInputsTests"
```

| # | Action | Expected | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| 3 | Run the command. | Pass (7 + 8 tests). They cover: the demand reaching cable, voltage drop, fault check and enclosure; the fault check **blocked** when its device differs from the one the cable step sized (`device_code`); the protective conductor following the cable (`S_cpc`) and the cable sized on a load's running current, not its duty-weighted one; a bigger motor staling the chain and the update carrying the current through; the refusal at the cable step when the tables are missing; the chain created when only the cable step is given its tables; and the issued pack never exporting the licensed ampacity table. | |

**Part C. Build the chain yourself with your own tables (owner-led).** The form cannot make a pack from saved calculations, so this part stops at linked, saved calculations and the stale marker. Create the six calculations by hand, taking the inputs of each step from `docs\worked-examples\packs\motor-circuit\inputs.json` (one JSON object keyed by step; copy one step's list out of it and import it. Its links name the original calculations and do not resolve here, so re-make the links with the *Link* row). Give E4 your own `ampacity` and `ratings` tables and your own `C_a`, `C_g`, `C_i`. Use the **Save inputs as record** panel as in T-REC part 2 if you want the tables licensed.

| # | Action | Expected | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| 4 | Make the 16 links, upstream first, with the *Link* row: E1 `P_rms` → E6 `P_add`; E1 `P_rated` → E6 `P_rated_add`; E6 `I_3ph` → E4 `I_b_in`; E4 `I_b` → E3 `I`; E4 `L` → E3 `L`; E4 `CSA` → E3 `A_c`; E6 `U_L` → E3 `V_n`; E6 `pf_link` → E3 `cos_phi`; E4 `T_op` → E3 `T_c`; E4 `CSA` → E5 `S_line`; E4 `CSA` → E5 `S_cpc`; E4 `L` → E5 `L`; E4 `I_n` → E5 `I_n`; E4 `device_code` → E5 `device_code`; E4 `T_op` → E5 `T_op`; E6 `Phi` → E2 `P_add`. | Each press reads `Linked <input> to <output>; it is pinned to that calculation's current revision.` A refusal for a dimension mismatch reads as in T-L1 step A5. The shipped example carries E1 `P_rms` 0.784142 kW and `P_rated` 0.628319 kW into E6, and E6 `Phi` 0.153333 kW into E2. Your E4 results depend on your tables, so compare them with your own hand calculation (Appendix B). | |
| 5 | In E5 pick a protective device that is not the one E4 sized (for example type B when E4 chose type C). | E5’s governing bar reads `Blocked — device: the cable step sized the circuit for MCB type C (I_a = 10 I_n), but the device chosen here is MCB type B (I_a = 5 I_n); choose MCB type C (I_a = 10 I_n) (or change the cable step) so the fault check uses the device that was sized.` Put it back to the sized device and the block clears. The wording of the two device names follows the device list; the structure of the text is the one in the code. | |
| 6 | Open E1, change the moved mass `m`, **Save changes**. | E6 shows ` · STALE`. E4, E3, E5 and E2 follow (E4 takes `I_b_in` from E6; E3 takes from E4 and E6; E5 from E4; E2 from E6). Updating in order (E6, then E4, then E3, E5 and E2) clears them. | |
| 7 | Record anything that does not behave like the other four chains. | Check and issue of each step is by the single-calculation buttons. E4 and E5 demand an independent check (T-IND). A draft upstream blocks a downstream check until the upstream is checked and issued. | |

#### 5.10.1 Long form T-C9

**Written before C-1; brought to the release head on 2026-10-27.** Every number, name and message below is what the release head shows.

**Chain.** Tamper cam (`mech.mechanism`) → Follower return spring, Cam shaft, Cam shaft bearing A, Cam drive motor; Cam shaft (`mech.shaft`) → Cam shaft bearing A; Cam drive motor (`elec.motor-sizing`) → Cam drive supply cable. Pack template **Mechanism review** (`pack.mechanism-review`). README: `docs\worked-examples\packs\mechanism-review\README.md`.

**Why this one needs your tables.** The cable step (E4) needs a cable-capacity (`ampacity`) table and a preferred device-rating (`ratings`) table. None ships (§A5). Nothing takes from the cable step, so unlike the motor circuit the pack **is created**, and the cable step opens blocked. The pack cannot be checked until you give E4 your own tables (the rows after the last update). The cable step's figures in this script come from the repository's run with its synthetic test tables; compare yours with a hand calculation (Appendix B). The cam's peak input torque enters the shaft as its alternating torque `T_a` (and its peak torque `T_pk`) with a steady torque of 0, because the cam's torque is fully alternating about a zero mean; the README explains it. The spring's rate and preload are typed in the cam step and are not linked back (that would be a cycle); the spring step warns `travel-mismatch` if its own working travel departs from the cam's lift by more than 1 %.

#### 5.11.1 Long form T-C10

**Written before C-1; brought to the release head on 2026-10-27.** Every number, name and message below is what the release head shows.

**Chain.** Ejector slider-crank (`mech.mechanism`) → Replacement cylinder; Replacement cylinder (`fp.cylinder`) → Emergency stroke accumulator. Pack template **Actuated linkage** (`pack.actuated-linkage`). README: `docs\worked-examples\packs\actuated-linkage\README.md`.

**The link into the cylinder is a kinematic proxy** (DB-04). A cylinder-actuated linkage runs at about constant cylinder speed, not at constant crank speed, so the crank's peak slide speed and peak force are what the cylinder must reach and carry, not what it would reproduce through the stroke. The cylinder's flow field is blank: its required extend speed `v_req` (linked) gives the flow. The stroke is typed (200 mm, the slide's stroke).

#### 5.12.1 Long form T-C12

**Written before C-1; brought to the release head on 2026-10-27.** Every number, name and message below is what the release head shows.

**Chain.** Room heat loss (`bs.room-heat-loss`) → Wall condensation; Room air state (`bs.psychrometrics`) → Wall condensation. Pack template **Envelope moisture** (`pack.envelope-moisture`). README: `docs\worked-examples\packs\envelope-moisture\README.md`.

**What is linked.** B4's U-value of the first element (`U_1`) is the Glaser step's `U_ref`. B2's dry-bulb (`T_1_out`) and relative humidity (`phi_1`) are the Glaser step's internal temperature (`T_int`) and humidity (`RH_int`). The wall's layers are **typed in both** the heat-loss and the Glaser steps; no per-layer link exists. The Glaser step warns (`u-ref-mismatch`) when its own U-value departs from B4's by more than 2 %. That guard compares the thermal resistance only: it cannot see a swapped layer order or a wrong vapour resistance factor. The monthly climate, the surface resistances, the critical humidity and the condensate limit are your records. **The example's climate is invented.** B4's layer table has no row editor in the form (D-37), so this script breaks the chain at B2; the automated test revises a B4 layer instead (`EnvelopeMoistureChainTests`).

|---|---|---|
| Room heat loss | U-values and room heat loss | 1 PASS |
| Room air state | Psychrometrics | no check |
| Wall condensation | Glaser condensation (interstitial and surface) | 3 PASS |

| Room air state `T_1_out` → Wall condensation `T_int` | 21 degC | 21 degC |
| Room air state `phi_1` → Wall condensation `RH_int` | 50 % | 60 % |

#### 5.14.1 Long form T-C14

**Written before C-1; brought to the release head on 2026-10-27.** Every number, name and message below is what the release head shows.

**Chain.** Pipe expansion and anchor force (`bs.pipe-expansion`) → Anchor post frame; Anchor post frame (`struct.frame-2d`) → Anchor post base plate. Pack template **Pipework supports** (`pack.pipework-supports`). README: `docs\worked-examples\packs\pipework-supports\README.md`.

**What is linked.** B8's `anchor_force` is the first load row (a table cell) of the S2 post frame; S2's base reactions are S6's actions, as in the steel-support pack. B8's moment is **not** carried to the post (it is the guided-cantilever root moment, not an applied one). The links depend on S2's numbering: node 1 the column base (fixed), node 2 the anchor, member 1 the post, the first load row the thermal force. The holding-down bolt data are typed (no bolt-check step). B8's allowable range and screening limit are your records; the example's are invented. **The anchorage (concrete and anchor design) is done in Hilti PROFIS: the file attached to the base-plate step in the shipped example is a placeholder.** S6 demands an independent check (DA-12).

| Anchor post frame | Beam and 2D frame analysis | no check |

#### 5.15.1 Long form T-C15

**Written before C-1; brought to the release head on 2026-10-27.** Every number, name and message below is what the release head shows.

**Chain.** Power trace (`elec.pcb-trace`) → Board load budget; Board load budget (`elec.load-budget`) → Enclosure temperature; Enclosure temperature (`elec.enclosure-heat`) → Cover latches, Cover plate. Pack template **Electronics enclosure** (`pack.electronics-enclosure`). README: `docs\worked-examples\packs\electronics-enclosure\README.md`.

**What is linked.** E7's trace loss (`trace_Ploss`) is a cell of E6's load table (a table-cell link); E6's heat (`Phi`) is E2's additional load; E2's internal temperature is M8's temperature of use; E2's breather-limited pressure is S9's cover pressure `q`, and, times the cover area, M8's required latch force `F_req`. The enclosure is sealed with a pressure-compensation breather, so the pressure difference is the smaller of the gas-law value and the breather's cracking pressure; with the breather governing, a revised trace does not move the pressure or the force. The latches' and cover's material values are **typed for 60 °C**, the enclosure's limit; they do not follow the links. The trace's board ambient (60 °C), the supply's loss row and the latch count (six) are typed.

| Member | Template | Checks when created |
|---|---|---|
| Power trace | PCB trace width (IPC-2221) | 1 PASS |
| Board load budget | Load budget, PSU, transformer and battery | no check |
| Enclosure temperature | Enclosure heat dissipation and fan sizing | 1 PASS |
| Cover latches | Snap-fits | 3 PASS |
| Cover plate | Plate bending | 2 PASS |

**Values that flow down each link.** The *Before* column is what the pack shows when created. The *After* column is what it shows once you have broken the upstream input and updated every step.

| Link | Before | After |

### 5.16 T-W1: CAD parameter import

Guided: `import-w1`. W1 reads CAD parameter files (NX, Creo, SolidWorks, Onshape and generic CSV or JSON). It never evaluates an expression. Chain 3 (T-C8) needs this script first. Part 7 repeats its hostile-file checks.

W1 reads parameters out of a CAD file into the inputs of a draft calculation. It is a workflow with no template and no sheet of its own: its marks on a sheet are the **source of each imported input** and the **import record** on the calculation. Files are untrusted: the reader caps a file at 5 MB, refuses control characters and macros, and **never evaluates an expression** (a right-hand side with a reference, a function or an operator stays read-only text). This script uses an invented NX expression file and a mapping profile into M1 (`mech.tolerance-stack`); the same panel reads the Creo, SolidWorks, Onshape and generic formats (golden files for each are under `tests/Tempest.Calc.Tests.Import/Samples/`). The format layouts are **Unconfirmed**: the samples are invented from each format's documented shape (section A.8). The panel needs a **saved, unedited draft**. Chain 3 (T-C8) adds a linked M2 fit.

Create the files. The profile text is what the product writes for the profile in `CadDrivenToleranceChainTests`:

```powershell
$w = "$HOME\TempestOS-Test-Data\w1"; New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force $w | Out-Null
@'
// Expression file created by NX 2306 (invented)
// Part: STACK-1
// Revision: A
[mm]plate_len=30
[mm]spacer_len=10
[mm]pin_len=5
[mm]gap_min=24.5
[mm]gap_max=25.5
[mm]derived=plate_len-spacer_len
[mm]flange=40 // not in the profile
'@ | Set-Content -Encoding utf8 "$w\stack-1.exp"
@'
{"id":"nx-stack","version":1,"title":"NX stack lengths to M1","entries":[{"cadName":"plate_len","target":"contributors[0].nominal","required":false},{"cadName":"spacer_len","target":"contributors[1].nominal","required":false},{"cadName":"pin_len","target":"contributors[2].nominal","required":false},{"cadName":"gap_min","target":"minimum","required":false},{"cadName":"gap_max","target":"maximum","required":false}],"system":"nx","templateId":"mech.tolerance-stack","ignoreCase":false}
'@ | Set-Content -Encoding utf8 "$w\nx-stack.json"
```

The M1 draft the import fills (a three-part pin stack that the file overwrites):

```powershell
[{"Symbol":"contributors","Rows":[{"Cells":{"name":{"Text":"Plate"},"direction":{"Text":"adds"},"nominal":{"Number":29,"Unit":"mm"},"upper":{"Number":0.1,"Unit":"mm"},"lower":{"Number":-0.1,"Unit":"mm"}}},{"Cells":{"name":{"Text":"Spacer"},"direction":{"Text":"subtracts"},"nominal":{"Number":9,"Unit":"mm"},"upper":{"Number":0.05,"Unit":"mm"},"lower":{"Number":-0.05,"Unit":"mm"}}},{"Cells":{"name":{"Text":"Pin"},"direction":{"Text":"adds"},"nominal":{"Number":5,"Unit":"mm"},"upper":{"Number":0.02,"Unit":"mm"},"lower":{"Number":-0.02,"Unit":"mm"}}}]},{"Symbol":"minimum","Number":20,"Unit":"mm"},{"Symbol":"maximum","Number":30,"Unit":"mm"},{"Symbol":"k","Number":3},{"Symbol":"f_rss","Number":1.5},{"Symbol":"cpk_target","Number":1.33},{"Symbol":"n_mc","Number":2000},{"Symbol":"seed","Number":1},{"Symbol":"notation","Text":"BS 8888"}]
'@ | Set-Clipboard
```

Open the newest `tempest-<yyyyMMdd>.log` in `$root\logs\` (the date in the name is UTC), with the last command in the block under this table. If the lines are not there, record `B`. The automated proof is `WorksheetImportLoggingTests` (4 tests). | |

Commands for T-W1 steps 10, 14 and 15 (`$w` is the folder of the first block of this script; `$root` is as in Part 1):

```powershell
# Step 10: change plate_len from 30 to 31 in the file
(Get-Content -Raw "$w\stack-1.exp") -replace 'plate_len=30','plate_len=31' | Set-Content -Encoding utf8 "$w\stack-1.exp"
# Step 14: a copy with a decimal comma (the file reads plate_len=31 after step 10)
(Get-Content -Raw "$w\stack-1.exp") -replace 'plate_len=31','plate_len=30,5' | Set-Content -Encoding utf8 "$w\stack-1-comma.exp"
# Step 15: read the newest application log for the import lines
Get-ChildItem "$root\logs" | Sort-Object LastWriteTime | Select-Object -Last 1 | Get-Content | Select-String import
```

**Automated proofs.** `dotnet test tests/Tempest.Calc.Tests.Import --configuration Release --no-build` (625 cases: golden files per CAD system, the bounded reader, locale-safe numbers, path refusal, the workbook reader with zip-bomb and macro refusal, mapping, provenance, CsCheck fuzz), `dotnet test tests/Tempest.Calc.Tests.Library --configuration Release --no-build --filter "FullyQualifiedName~CadDrivenToleranceChainTests|FullyQualifiedName~CadImportSecurityTests"` and `dotnet test tests/Tempest.Desktop.Tests --configuration Release --no-build --filter "FullyQualifiedName~WorksheetCadImport"`.

**Number formats.** A generic CSV whose profile declares a decimal comma and whose delimiter is a comma is ambiguous: `12,5` splits into 12 and 5. The reader therefore refuses **every unquoted number** in the value or expression column under that shape, whole numbers too. The text says to put the numbers in quotes (`"12,5"`) or to save the file with a semicolon delimiter (SEC-B-01, P-B-01; ADR-0171). Under a decimal point, a digit string that a thousands separator may have split is refused or blocked. Both rules are loud and never read a wrong value (RR-B-01 and RR-B-05 in section C.7). If a real export meets one of them, record the text. A workbook cell or JSON number that underflows to zero is refused too (SEC-B-04, P-B-02). The automated proofs are `AddendumBSecurityRegressionTests` in the Import project.

### 5.17 T-W2: scan deviation import

Guided: `import-w2`. W2 reads scan deviation tables through your own column profile. It reports statistics and pushes a fitted normal into M1, F3 or M2. Chain 2 (T-C7) needs this script first. Part 7 repeats its hostile-file checks.

W2 reads an inspection table (ZEISS or GOM Inspect, multi-stage, PolyWorks or Geomagic text) through **your own column profile**, computes the mean, σ, Cp, Cpk, Pp and Ppk of each feature over N parts with an Anderson–Darling normality check, and pushes a fitted **normal** into M1 (a stack contributor), F3 (a measured-dimension row) or a single input. It is a workflow with no template. The statistics are never trimmed silently; outliers are reported and kept unless you tick the box. Fewer than 2 parts: not computable. Fewer than 8: normality is not assessed. Fewer than 30 (adjustable): a capability warning. **The PDF table path is not built**: export the table as CSV or `.xlsx`. The header spellings of real exports are **Unconfirmed** (section A.8); the profile is where you say what your export calls each column.

The table is invented: ten parts of a groove root diameter (nominal 51.5 mm, ±0.05) and one bore row that the profile does not map. The profile JSON is what the product writes.

Cp and Pp are equal and Cpk and Ppk are equal because the subgroup size is 1. Below it a line says the table has **fewer than 30 parts**. Hand check: s = √(Σ(x − x̄)²/9) = 0.0062361 mm; Cp = 0.1/(6s) = 2.6726; Cpk = 0.035/(3s) = 1.8708. | |

| 10 | Provenance is a claim that is checked. Type 0.02 over the `upper` cell of contributor 1 (re-import the table JSON from step 1 and edit it) and press **Save changes**. | The save says the edited value is now a **manual entry** (a scan-import source stands only while a record holds that exact value at that place). Record the text. If the form cannot edit a table cell (D-37), record `B`: `ScanToSealChainTests` covers it (`Scan_Provenance_Is_A_Claim_That_Is_Checked_…`). | |
| 11 | The within σ is c4-corrected. In the panel set *Subgroup size* to 5 (the ten parts then make two consecutive subgroups of five) and read the statistics line again. Afterwards put the size back to 1. | The line reads `Groove root (no stage): n = 10, mean 51.485 mm, σ 0.0062361 mm, Cp 2.478 Cpk 1.735 (σ within 0.006726 mm, pooled over the subgroups and divided by c4 = 0.96931 (unbiased)) Pp 2.673 Ppk 1.871, normality …` (hand-computed with python3, not run in the form: record what the form shows). The pooled within σ is 0.0065192 mm on 8 degrees of freedom. It is divided by c4(9) = 0.96931 because the square root of an unbiased variance is not an unbiased σ. Without the division Cp would read 2.557 and Cpk 1.790, about 3 % optimistic at ten parts (DB-11). With the size left at 1 nothing is pooled and nothing is corrected, so Cp = Pp and Cpk = Ppk. | |

**Automated proofs.** `dotnet test tests/Tempest.Calc.Tests.Import --configuration Release --no-build` (statistics against python3 and a ten-value hand calculation: mean 10.003, s 0.0188856, Cp = Pp 0.8825, Cpk = Ppk 0.8296; subgroups of five: pooled σ 0.02 on 8 degrees of freedom, c4(9) = 0.96931, σ within 0.020633, Cp 0.8078, Cpk 0.7593 (the uncorrected 0.02 gave 0.8333 and 0.7833); the N = 30 reference tables; GOM, PolyWorks and Geomagic layouts; blank, NaN, `1e999`, markup and link cells; caps; fuzz), `dotnet test tests/Tempest.Calc.Tests.Library --configuration Release --no-build --filter "FullyQualifiedName~ScanToSealChainTests"` and the Desktop filter `FullyQualifiedName~WorksheetScanImport`.

#### 5.20.1 Long form T-C11

**Chain.** Lug and spreader beam S8 (`struct.lifting`) → beam member check S7 (`struct.ltb-interaction`) and section S1 (`struct.section-properties`); lug weld M6 (`mech.weld-group`); lug bolts S4 (`struct.connection-ec3`) or bolt group M3 (`mech.bolt-group`). This chain is **not a pack template** and has no chain test. §B7 runs it with the flag on in the test host only, and ADR-0173 leaves the wiring to the integrator. No pack names S8, and the form cannot make a pack from calculations you saved (DA-14), so you run this chain by hand with the *Link* row.

**What S8 offers.** S8 declares the ports `capacity`, `governing_mode` and `util` (`lug.capacity`, `lug.governing_mode`, `spreader.util`). For the linked checks it also offers `F_s`, `F_ip` and `F_op` (for the weld group M6, the bolts S4 and the bolt group M3) and `N_beam` (for the member check S7 and the section S1). `N_beam` is the force along the beam axis at the more loaded lug. It is the larger of the level case `F_v·tan θ` and the pitched case `F_v·sin(θ + 6°)/cos θ`. Which input of M6, S4, M3, S7 or S1 takes each port is the integrator's choice.

**Check the sign before you link `N_beam`.** S7 takes compression as negative and refuses a positive `N_Ed` (its hard maximum is 0). `N_beam` is a magnitude. A link therefore cannot carry it into `N_Ed` unchanged, and the *Link* row cannot negate a value. Record what the form does.

| # | Action | Expected | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Switch the flag on (T-S8 step A4). In a new project, bring S8 to a saved draft with the worked-example inputs (T-S8 step B3) and name it `Lug and beam`. | Saved. The bar reads `PASS — Design load against the bearing resistance (BTH-1): utilisation 0.57`. | |
| 2 | Open S7 and S1 and save a draft of each (S7 from the worked example `ub-457x191x98-beam-column`, T-S7 step 3). In the *Link* row of S7 pick the input `N_Ed`, calculation `Lug and beam`, output `N_beam`. | Record the result: either the link is refused (the text), or it is made and S7 then blocks because `N_Ed` is above its hard maximum of 0. Either way S8 and S7 do not agree on the sign. Do not issue the result. | |
| 3 | Link `F_ip` and `F_op` from `Lug and beam` into the inputs you choose on M6, S4 or M3 (a force input of the right dimension). | Each press reads `Linked <input> to <output>; it is pinned to that calculation's current revision.` A dimension mismatch is refused as in T-L1 step A5. Record which inputs you linked. | |
| 4 | Open `Lug and beam`. Change `WLL` from 150 to 160 kN. **Save changes**. | Every calculation you linked to it carries ` · STALE` in amber. Open one: the banner names the S8 output (for example `Stale — 'Lug and beam' rev A now gives a different 'F_ip' than the value '…' took.`). **Update linked inputs** clears it. Update in order (T-L1 part B). Put `WLL` back to 150 kN afterwards. | |
| 5 | Check the governance carries through. Try **Record check** on `Lug and beam` as its author, then as a second person with no attestation. | Refused as in T-S8 steps C2 and C3. A linked calculation that takes a value from `Lug and beam` cannot be checked or issued while `Lug and beam` is a draft (T-L1 step B5). | |
| 6 | Switch the flag off (T-S8 part D) and open one of the linked calculations. | The linked calculation stays readable. Updating or issuing it is refused while its upstream S8 is switched off (the link is refused with the switched-off sentence). Record the text. | |

## Part 6 — The remaining calculators

Part 6 tests the other 36 worksheet calculators. Their liability is lower than Part 4. They still need a test, because the chains and your own work depend on them.

The order follows Track P. The nine calculators that Track P rates High come first. The Medium and Low ones follow. The calculators that Track P wrote no script for come last. Those are M9, E9 and ten replacements for the older calculators. Read section 4.2 first. It says how to read a block.

Ten replacements for the older calculators have no long-form script. They are bearing life basic, fatigue damage, shaft stress and thermal expansion. They also include thin vessel, material margin, column buckling, fillet weld, plane wall and thermal chain. Test them with their sets and the shared checks in the automated suites.

### 6.1 T-REF: the shared kernels

Two pieces of logic moved into the Engine without changing a number. One is the gas-law kernel (B6 and F2). The other is the tolerance-stack engine (M1, F3 and W2). This script re-runs one worked example of each. The Guided test has no step for it. Do it by hand. Use the test values for the numbers.

Phase B-0 moved two pieces of logic into the Engine without changing a number. The B6 sealed-system vessel now calls a shared polytropic gas-law kernel (`GasLaw`, which F2 also uses), and M1 now calls a pure tolerance-stack engine (`ToleranceStackEngine`, which F3 and W2 also use). The B6 and M1 sheets, their goldens and their known-answer cases are unchanged. This script re-runs one worked example of each and compares it with the values in the example folder, which are the same values the Addendum A and base scripts use. The automated proofs are in T-AUTO below (Engine `GasLawTests` and `ToleranceStackEngineGuardTests`; Calculators `ToleranceStackEngineTests`, `SealedSystemTests` and `ToleranceStackTests`).

**Part A. B6 expansion vessel**

**Inputs from** worked example `heat-pump-retrofit-55-45` (`docs\worked-examples\building-services\bs.sealed-system\heat-pump-retrofit-55-45`) — Heat pump retrofit at 55/45 °C: existing radiators and expansion vessel. Status: Confirmed (analytic; recomputed in python3 in the repository).

A five-room heat-pump retrofit at 55/45 °C flow and return with a 12 L vessel. The example FAILS by design: the emitters are too small for the room heat at that mean temperature difference.

**Part B. M1 tolerance stack**

**Inputs from** worked example `bearing-housing-gap` (`docs\worked-examples\mechanical\mech.tolerance-stack\bearing-housing-gap`) — Bearing housing axial clearance (analytic). Status: Confirmed (analytic; recomputed in python3 in the repository).

#### 6.6.1 Long form T-E8: Containment fill, bend radius and cable pulling

**Written for version 1.1.0; brought to version 1.3.0 on 2026-10-27.** The Addendum C fixes touched this template: `elec.containment-01`. Every number, name and message below is what version 1.3.0 shows.

E8 takes the space factor, the bend-radius multiple, the friction coefficient, the pulling limits and the weight correction from your records. No IET table ships (section 2.11). The example enters them as assumptions.

**Inputs from** worked example `servo-cable-pull-three-bends` (`docs\worked-examples\electrical\elec.containment\servo-cable-pull-three-bends`) — Servo power and encoder cables pulled through 40 mm conduit with three bends. Status: Confirmed (analytic; recomputed in python3 in the repository).

Two servo power cables (14 mm) and four encoder cables (9 mm) pulled through a 40 mm conduit with three bends (90° at R 500 mm, 90° at R 400 mm, 45° at R 600 mm) and 29 m of straight run. The sheet recommends the pull direction by the lower tension.

The worked example carries a source on every input. Copy its inputs to the clipboard from PowerShell, run from the repository root, then press **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)** (step 3):

```powershell
Get-Content -Raw docs\worked-examples\electrical\elec.containment\servo-cable-pull-three-bends\inputs.json | Set-Clipboard   # 9 inputs
```

|---|---|---|---|

The reference line starts `elec.containment@1.3.0  ·  hash`. | |
| 2 | Before you type or import anything, read the governing bar. | The bar reads `Blocked — cables: enter at least one row of 'Cables in the containment and pulled together' (the table takes up to 40 rows): fill in od (Overall diameter, mm), count (Number of cables), mass (Mass per metre, each, kg/m).`. A blocked bar names the next thing to enter. This reading comes from the template defaults alone; it is not a result to keep. | |

#### 6.9.1 Long form T-B7: Glaser condensation (interstitial and surface)

**Written for version 1.2.0; brought to version 1.4.0 on 2026-10-27.** The Addendum C fixes touched this template: `bs.glaser-01`. Every number, name and message below is what version 1.4.0 shows.

B7 is the Glaser condensation method over twelve months. The monthly climate, the surface resistances, the critical humidity and the condensate limit are your records (section 2.11). **The climate in both examples is invented** (a maritime year); it is not ISO 13788 data. The sheet warns that EN 15026 is the better tool for timber and breathable construction.

**Part A. Internal insulation without a vapour control layer**

**Inputs from** worked example `internal-insulation-no-vcl` (`docs\worked-examples\building-services\bs.glaser\internal-insulation-no-vcl`) — Solid brick wall with internal mineral wool and no vapour control layer: interstitial condensation over a year. Status: Confirmed (analytic; recomputed in python3 in the repository).

A 12.5 mm plasterboard, 50 mm mineral wool and 215 mm brick wall at 20 °C and 50 % inside. Condensation starts in month 11 and peaks in month 3.

The worked example carries a source on every input. Copy its inputs to the clipboard from PowerShell, run from the repository root, then press **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)** (step 3):

```powershell
Get-Content -Raw docs\worked-examples\building-services\bs.glaser\internal-insulation-no-vcl\inputs.json | Set-Clipboard   # 11 inputs
```

| # | Action | Expected | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Open **Engineering → Modules → Calculations → Worksheets**. Pick **Glaser condensation (interstitial and surface)** in the catalogue (or press Ctrl+K and type its name). | The right pane heading reads **Glaser condensation (interstitial and surface)**. The reference line starts `bs.glaser@1.4.0  ·  hash`. | |
| 2 | Before you type or import anything, read the governing bar. | The bar reads `Blocked — layers: enter at least one row of 'Wall layers from the inside outwards (thickness d, conductivity λ and vapour resistance factor μ)' (the table takes up to 30 rows): fill in d (Thickness, mm), lambda (Thermal conductivity λ, W/(m*K)), mu (Vapour resistance factor μ).`. A blocked bar names the next thing to enter. This reading comes from the template defaults alone; it is not a result to keep. | |
| 3 | Run the PowerShell line above and press **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)**. Do this first: an import re-seeds the whole form. | Status line reads `Imported 11 input(s).` Nothing is reported as skipped or blocked. | |

| 5 | Compare the **Results** list with the table of expected outputs below. | Each result matches the *Form shows* column. Where the tolerance is tighter than the form's rounding (4 or 5 significant figures), the *Form shows* column is the test. | |
| 6 | Read the governing bar above the results. | The bar reads `FAIL — Peak condensate on an interface against its limit: utilisation 6.05`. | |
| 7 | Read the checks list under the results (governing first). | FAIL — Peak condensate on an interface against its limit: 3022.7 g/m² vs 500 g/m² (u = 6.05); PASS — Minimum surface temperature factor against the factor of the wall at 80 % critical humidity: 0.59026 vs 0.87729 (u = 0.67); PASS — Condensate left after 12 months against zero: 0 g/m² as entered, 0 g/m² in 20 sub-layers per layer vs 0 g/m² (the wall dries out within the year). | |

#### 6.10.1 Long form T-B9: Insulation thickness (ISO 12241)

**Written for version 1.1.0; brought to version 1.2.0 on 2026-10-27.** The Addendum C fixes touched this template: `bs.insulation-01`. Every number, name and message below is what version 1.2.0 shows.

B9 is the ISO 12241 formula route: the thickness that meets a heat-loss limit, a surface-temperature limit and an anti-condensation margin, solved for the outer diameter, and the heat flow and surface temperature at the thickness you choose. The heat-flow targets and the dew-point margin are your records (BS 5422 targets, section 2.11). The surface coefficient is either typed or taken from a convection and radiation correlation. Run both examples.

**Part A. Chilled-water pipe, anti-condensation**

**Inputs from** worked example `chilled-water-pipe-condensation` (`docs\worked-examples\building-services\bs.insulation\chilled-water-pipe-condensation`) — Chilled-water pipe in a plant room: heat-gain limit and anti-condensation, 25 mm chosen. Status: Confirmed (analytic; recomputed in python3 in the repository).

A 90 mm pipe carrying 6 °C water in 28 °C, 70 % air, with 25 mm of λ 0.035 insulation and a 12 W/m heat-gain limit.

The worked example carries a source on every input. Copy its inputs to the clipboard from PowerShell, run from the repository root, then press **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)** (step 3):


| 1 | Open **Engineering → Modules → Calculations → Worksheets**. Pick **Insulation thickness (ISO 12241)** in the catalogue (or press Ctrl+K and type its name). | The right pane heading reads **Insulation thickness (ISO 12241)**. The reference line starts `bs.insulation@1.2.0  ·  hash`. | |
| 2 | Before you type or import anything, read the governing bar. | The bar reads `PASS — Chosen thickness against the heat-loss limit: utilisation 0.95`. This reading comes from the template defaults alone; it is not a result to keep. | |

| 4 | Check each input against the *Inputs* table of the example's `README.md`. Change nothing. | Every value, unit and source matches. The *Recalculating…* marker clears. | |
| 5 | Compare the **Results** list with the table of expected outputs below. | Each result matches the *Form shows* column. Where the tolerance is tighter than the form's rounding (4 or 5 significant figures), the *Form shows* column is the test. | |
| 6 | Read the governing bar above the results. | The bar reads `PASS — Chosen thickness against the heat-gain limit: utilisation 0.80`. | |
| 7 | Read the checks list under the results (governing first). | PASS — Chosen thickness against the heat-gain limit: 9.593 W/m vs 12 W/m (u = 0.80); PASS — Chosen thickness against the anti-condensation allowance: 2.726 K vs 4.988 K (u = 0.55). | |
| 8 | Read the notices under the checks. | Warnings, not failures. One `Warning — <symbol>: a tabulated value not taken from a reference record (<record kind>); enter it as one so its source, edition and licence travel with it.` for each of `lambda` (Material properties); `h_se` (Outer surface coefficient); `eps` (Surface emissivity); `q_max_m`, `dew_margin` (Insulation criteria). Record any other notice you see. No *Block* line appears. | |

Afterwards put `t_chosen` back to 25 mm. | Governing bar reads `FAIL — Chosen thickness against the heat-gain limit: utilisation 1.12`. Checks: FAIL — Chosen thickness against the heat-gain limit (u = 1.12); PASS — Chosen thickness against the anti-condensation allowance (u = 0.89). `q_per_m` shows 13.42 W/m, `T_se` shows 23.55 °C, `u_q` shows 1.12, `u_dew` shows 0.89. | |
| 11 | Variation — Use bright cladding. Set `eps` to 0.1. Afterwards put `eps` back to 0.9. | `h_se` is given (8 W/(m²·K)), so `eps` only judges that value against condensation. At ε = 0.9 the sheet does not warn. At ε = 0.1 a warning reads `h_se: the anti-condensation thickness is 13.162 mm as given, but 25.183 mm by the correlation at ε = 0.1. A fixed h_se above the real value under-sizes it.` The governing check stays PASS on the heat-gain limit, now at u = 0.80. Enter the emissivity of the real jacket before you rely on the anti-condensation result. | |

**Expected verdict:** PASS (governing: Chosen thickness against the heat-gain limit).

#### 6.11.1 Long form T-M5: Rolling bearing life (ISO 281)

**Written for version 1.1.0; brought to version 1.3.0 on 2026-10-27.** Every number, name and message below is what version 1.3.0 shows.

**Inputs from** known-answer case `ka-ball-95` — Ball bearing, radial load, 95 % reliability. Status: Confirmed (analytic; see Appendix A for what is and is not checked against a primary source).

Only `R_rel` (the reliability; the old symbol `R` is its alias) and `L_req` differ from the form defaults. The sheet states the edition: ISO 281:2007 Weibull form for a_1 (0.64 at 95 %).

**Values to enter** (a new sheet opens on the declared defaults only; caption is `symbol — description`):

| Input | Value | Unit | On a new sheet |
|---|---|---|---|
| `type` — Bearing type | ball (p = 3) |  | already set |
| `F_r` — Radial load | 2 | kN | already set |
| `F_a` — Axial load | 0 | kN | already set |
| `n` — Speed | 1000 | rpm | already set |
| `C` — Basic dynamic load rating | 20 | kN | already set |
| `C_0` — Basic static load rating | 11 | kN | already set |
| `R_rel` — Reliability | 95 | % | type it |
| `a_iso` — Life modification factor a_ISO (from the manufacturer's tool) | 1 |  | already set |
| `L_req` — Required life | 10000 | h | type it |

| # | Action | Expected | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Open **Engineering → Modules → Calculations → Worksheets**. Pick **Rolling bearing life (ISO 281)** in the catalogue (or press Ctrl+K and type its name). | The right pane heading reads **Rolling bearing life (ISO 281)**. The reference line starts `mech.bearing-life@1.3.0  ·  hash`. | |
| 2 | Before you type or import anything, read the governing bar. | The bar reads `FAIL — Required life against modified life: utilisation 1.20`. This reading comes from the template defaults alone; it is not a result to keep. | |
| 3 | Enter the values marked *type it* or *pick it* in the table above. Leave every unit picker on the unit shown. | No red or orange problem text appears under the inputs you entered. The *Recalculating…* marker clears. | |
| 4 | Compare the **Results** list with the table of expected outputs below. | Each result matches the *Form shows* column. Where the KAT tolerance is tighter than the form's rounding (4 or 5 significant figures), the *Form shows* column is the test. | |
| 5 | Read the governing bar above the results. | The bar reads `PASS — Required life against modified life: utilisation 0.94`. | |
| 6 | Read the checks list under the results (governing first). | PASS — Required life against modified life: 10000 h vs 10632 h (u = 0.94); PASS — Required static safety factor against the static safety factor: 1 vs 5.5 (u = 0.18). | |
| 7 | Read the notices under the checks. | Warnings, not failures. One `Warning — <symbol>: a tabulated value not taken from a reference record (<record kind>); enter it as one so its source, edition and licence travel with it.` for each of `C`, `C_0` (Bearing load ratings); `e_lim`, `X_1`, `Y_1`, `X`, `Y`, `X_0`, `Y_0` (Bearing X, Y, e factors); `a_iso` (Bearing aISO life modification factor). Record any other notice you see. No *Block* line appears. | |
| 8 | Tick **Show working**. | Numbered steps appear, each as formula, substitution, result, with the clause tag (« …) where the step cites one. **Go to step** jumps to the governing step. | |
| 9 | Variation — Demand more life. Set `L_req` to 20000 h. | Governing bar reads `FAIL — Required life against modified life: utilisation 1.89`. `L_nmh` is unchanged at 10631 h. The static check still passes (u = 0.18). | |
| 10 | Put `L_req` back to 10000 h. Set `F_a` to 1 kN. | Governing bar reads `Blocked — Y: F_a/F_r = 0.5 exceeds e_lim = 0.3, but Y = 0, so the axial load 1 kN would count for nothing in F_dyn. Enter the bearing's X and Y from the manufacturer, or raise e_lim if the bearing takes no more axial load.` Nothing is calculated. This is the guard against an axial load that a zero `Y` would silently drop. | |
| 11 | Set `Y` to 0.8 (an invented test value, not a bearing figure). | The block clears. Governing bar reads `FAIL — Required life against modified life: utilisation 2.59`. `L_10h` shows 6074 h and `L_nmh` shows 3874 h. Put `F_a` back to 0 and `Y` back to 0 before you go on. | |

**Expected outputs** (case `ka-ball-95`; tolerances are relative):

| Output | Meaning | Expected | Tolerance | Form shows | Result |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `L_10` | Basic rating life in millions of revolutions | 1000 | 1e-9 (exact) | 1000 | |
| `L_10h` | Basic rating life | 16666.67 h | 1e-6 | 16667 h | |
| `a_1` | Reliability factor | 0.637912 | 1e-5 | 0.63791 | |
| `L_nmh` | Modified rating life | 10631.86 h | 1e-5 | 10632 h | |
| `u_life` | Life utilisation | 0.940569 | 1e-5 | 0.94 | |

**Expected verdict:** PASS (governing: Required life against modified life). **Script result:** (blank)  **Tester / date:** (blank)

#### 6.13.1 Long form T-S5: Load combinations (EN 1990)

**Written for version 1.0.0; brought to version 1.3.0 on 2026-10-27.** The Addendum C fixes touched this template: `struct.load-combinations-01`, `struct.load-combinations-02`. Every number, name and message below is what version 1.3.0 shows.

**Inputs from** known-answer case `ka-610-single` — Permanent plus office imposed, 6.10. Status: Confirmed (analytic; see Appendix A for what is and is not checked against a primary source).

Permanent 10 kN plus office imposed 5 kN. The unit (`kN`), the method (`6.10`) and the factors stay at their defaults. This sheet has no pass/fail check by design: it reports design effects.

**Table inputs.** A new sheet opens with every table empty and the form has no row editor (D-37), so load these by import. Copy this block to the clipboard from PowerShell, then press **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)**:

```powershell
@'
[{"Symbol":"actions","Rows":[{"Cells":{"name":{"Text":"Dead load"},"category":{"Text":"G permanent"},"F_k":{"Number":10}}},{"Cells":{"name":{"Text":"Office imposed"},"category":{"Text":"B office"},"F_k":{"Number":5}}}]}]
'@ | Set-Clipboard
```

| # | Action | Expected | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Open **Engineering → Modules → Calculations → Worksheets**. Pick **Load combinations (EN 1990)** in the catalogue (or press Ctrl+K and type its name). | The right pane heading reads **Load combinations (EN 1990)**. The reference line starts `struct.load-combinations@1.3.0  ·  hash`. | |
| 2 | Before you type or import anything, read the governing bar. | The bar reads `Blocked — actions: enter at least one row of 'Actions (one effect, signed)' (the table takes up to 30 rows): fill in name (Action), category (Category), F_k (Characteristic value (signed, in the effect unit)).`. A blocked bar names the next thing to enter. This reading comes from the template defaults alone; it is not a result to keep. | |
| 3 | Copy the JSON block above to the clipboard and press **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)**. Do this first: an import re-seeds the whole form. | Status line reads `Imported 1 input(s).` Nothing is reported as skipped or blocked. | |
| 4 | Check each value in the table above against the form. Change nothing. | Every value matches. The *Recalculating…* marker clears. | |
| 5 | Compare the **Results** list with the table of expected outputs below. | Each result matches the *Form shows* column. Where the KAT tolerance is tighter than the form's rounding (4 or 5 significant figures), the *Form shows* column is the test. | |
| 6 | Read the governing bar above the results. | The bar reads `No pass/fail check in this calculation.`. | |
| 7 | Read the notices under the checks. | Warnings, not failures. One `Warning — <symbol>: a tabulated value not taken from a reference record (<record kind>); enter it as one so its source, edition and licence travel with it.` for each of `gamma_G_sup`, `gamma_G_inf`, `gamma_Q`, `xi` (EN 1990 partial and ψ factors). Record any other notice you see. No *Block* line appears. | |
| 8 | Tick **Show working**. | Numbered steps appear, each as formula, substitution, result, with the clause tag (« …) where the step cites one. **Go to step** jumps to the governing step. | |
| 9 | Variation — Switch the ULS expression. Set `method` to `6.10a and 6.10b`. | `E_d_max` shows 18.98 kN (case `ka-610ab-single`, 18.975 kN, tolerance 1e-9 relative). Show working lists 6.10a (18.75 kN) and 6.10b (18.975 kN); 6.10b governs. | |

#### 6.16.1 Long form T-M8: Snap-fits

**Written for version 1.3.0; brought to version 1.5.0 on 2026-10-27.** The Addendum C fixes touched this template: `mech.snap-fit-01`. Every number, name and message below is what version 1.5.0 shows.

M8 takes the friction pair, the secant modulus and the permissible strain from your records or from a W3 material (T-W3). The example types the secant modulus (`Es_source`) and states the permissible strain. The strain is the nominal surface strain at the critical section; the root fillet is not modelled (the sheet says so).

**Inputs from** worked example `enclosure-lid-hook` (`docs\worked-examples\mechanical\mech.snap-fit\enclosure-lid-hook`) — Enclosure lid hook: cantilever snap-fit, thickness tapered to half. Status: Confirmed (analytic; recomputed in python3 in the repository).

A tapered-thickness cantilever hook on an enclosure lid, 18 mm long, 2.2 mm thick at the root and 7 mm wide, deflected 1.2 mm, in a plastic with a 2000 MPa secant modulus.

The worked example carries a source on every input. Copy its inputs to the clipboard from PowerShell, run from the repository root, then press **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)** (step 3):

```powershell
Get-Content -Raw docs\worked-examples\mechanical\mech.snap-fit\enclosure-lid-hook\inputs.json | Set-Clipboard   # 14 inputs
```

| # | Action | Expected | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Open **Engineering → Modules → Calculations → Worksheets**. Pick **Snap-fits** in the catalogue (or press Ctrl+K and type its name). | The right pane heading reads **Snap-fits**. The reference line starts `mech.snap-fit@1.5.0  ·  hash`. | |
| 2 | Before you type or import anything, read the governing bar. | The bar reads `Blocked — mu: enter a finite number.`. A blocked bar names the next thing to enter. This reading comes from the template defaults alone; it is not a result to keep. | |
| 3 | Run the PowerShell line above and press **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)**. Do this first: an import re-seeds the whole form. | Status line reads `Imported 14 input(s).` Nothing is reported as skipped or blocked. | |
| 4 | Check each input against the *Inputs* table of the example's `README.md`. Change nothing. | Every value, unit and source matches. The *Recalculating…* marker clears. | |
| 5 | Compare the **Results** list with the table of expected outputs below. | Each result matches the *Form shows* column. Where the tolerance is tighter than the form's rounding (4 or 5 significant figures), the *Form shows* column is the test. | |
| 6 | Read the governing bar above the results. | The bar reads `PASS — Strain at the critical section against the permissible strain: utilisation 0.19`. | |
| 7 | Read the checks list under the results (governing first). | PASS — Strain at the critical section against the permissible strain: 0.7473 % vs 4 % (u = 0.19); PASS — Entry-face friction μ·tan α against the jamming limit: μ·tan α = 0.20207 vs < 1. | |
| 8 | Read the notices under the checks. | Warnings, not failures. One `Warning — <symbol>: a tabulated value not taken from a reference record (<record kind>); enter it as one so its source, edition and licence travel with it.` for each of `eps_perm_once` (Material properties); `mu` (Coefficient of friction); `Es` (Secant modulus at the design strain). Record any other notice you see. No *Block* line appears. | |
| 9 | Tick **Show working**. | Numbered steps appear, each as formula, substitution, result, with the clause tag (« …) where the step cites one. **Go to step** jumps to the governing step. | |
| 10 | Variation — Shorten the beam. Set `l` to 8 mm. Afterwards put `l` back to 18 mm. | The sheet is refused (since `mech.snap-fit` 1.4.0 the `slender` notice is a block, DC-18). Governing bar reads `Blocked — l: the beam is 3.6364 thicknesses long, and beam theory is not reliable below 5. Lengthen the beam, thin it, or check the strain with a finite-element model or a test.` No result is shown. | |
| 11 | Variation — Make the joint self-locking. Set `mu` to 0.5. Afterwards put `mu` back to 0.35. | Governing bar reads `PASS — Strain at the critical section against the permissible strain: utilisation 0.19`. `alpha_lock` shows 63.43°, `assembly_force` shows 7.101 N, `retention_force` shows "self-locking (infinite)", `self_locking` shows "Yes: α′ ≥ arctan(1/μ); the parts cannot be separated by pulling". Warning: `alpha_r: the return angle 65° is at or above arctan(1/μ) = 63.435°, so the joint cannot be undone by pulling. That suits a permanent joint and not a serviceable one.` | |

| `assembly_force` | Assembly force | 5.44905 N | 5.449 N | |

| `retention_force` | Separation force | 46.891 N | 46.89 N | |

#### 6.18.1 Long form T-S9: Plate bending

**Written for version 1.2.1; brought to version 1.4.0 on 2026-10-27.** Every number, name and message below is what version 1.4.0 shows.

S9 covers circular and rectangular plates with uniform, patch and ring loads. The worked example is a simply supported rectangle under uniform pressure (Navier series). The known-answer cases that compare with the classical coefficients for square plates are **Unconfirmed** (section A.5, U-26 to U-29). The example leaves the patch and ring inputs at their defaults, so the sheet warns about each.

**Inputs from** worked example `enclosure-lid-aluminium` (`docs\worked-examples\structural\struct.plate-bending\enclosure-lid-aluminium`) — Aluminium enclosure lid 300 × 200 × 4 mm, simply supported, 20 kPa internal pressure rise. Status: Confirmed (analytic; recomputed in python3 in the repository).

A 300 × 200 × 4 mm aluminium lid under 20 kPa, simply supported on four edges, with a span/250 deflection limit.

The worked example carries a source on every input. Copy its inputs to the clipboard from PowerShell, run from the repository root, then press **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)** (step 3):

#### 6.22.1 Long form T-B8: Pipe expansion, loops and anchor forces (screening)

**Written for version 1.2.1; brought to version 1.5.2 on 2026-10-27.** The Addendum C fixes touched this template: `bs.pipe-expansion-R1`. Every number, name and message below is what version 1.5.2 shows.

B8 is a screening for pipe expansion: movement, restrained force, the leg or loop that absorbs it, the anchor force and moment, and the OD·Y/(L−U)² ratio against a limit K1. The sheet says it is a screening only; a line that fails it needs a formal stress analysis. Sa and K1 are your records (section 2.11). The examples' values (Sa 60 and 100 MPa, K1 100 and 120 mm²/m²) are invented. Run all three examples.

**Part A. U-loop on a low-temperature hot-water main**

**Inputs from** worked example `lthw-main-expansion-loop` (`docs\worked-examples\building-services\bs.pipe-expansion\lthw-main-expansion-loop`) — Low-temperature hot-water main, 50 m between anchors: a U-loop of two 4 m legs. Status: Confirmed (analytic; recomputed in python3 in the repository).

A 50 m steel main, 88.9 × 3.2 mm, from 10 °C to 80 °C, with a U-loop whose legs are 4 m.

The worked example carries a source on every input. Copy its inputs to the clipboard from PowerShell, run from the repository root, then press **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)** (step 3):


Pick **Pipe expansion, loops and anchor forces (screening)** in the catalogue (or press Ctrl+K and type its name). | The right pane heading reads **Pipe expansion, loops and anchor forces (screening)**. The reference line starts `bs.pipe-expansion@1.5.2  ·  hash`. | |

A blocked bar names the next thing to enter. This reading comes from the template defaults alone; it is not a result to keep. | |

Afterwards put it back to 120 GPa. | At 40 MPa the sheet is refused and no result is shown. The block `modulus-low` reads `E: 0.04 GPa is below the 0.05 GPa floor for a pipe material. Enter E in GPa, not MPa, or take it from a material.` At 120 MPa the sheet runs with no block: 0.12 GPa is a real soft plastic. The owner lowered the floor from 0.5 to 0.05 GPa in `bs.pipe-expansion` 1.5.2 (DC-27). The input's own text ("not MPa") and the material library are now the guard against that slip. The usual range of `E` is 0.05 to 500 GPa. The block also fires for `E_hot`. A modulus below the hard minimum of 0.01 GPa (for example 5 MPa) is refused as an input. | |

#### 6.23.1 Long form T-B4: U-values and room heat loss

**Written for version 1.2.0; brought to version 1.6.0 on 2026-10-27.** The Addendum C fixes touched this template: `bs.room-heat-loss-R1`, `bs.room-heat-loss-01`, `bs.room-heat-loss-R2`. Every number, name and message below is what version 1.6.0 shows.

**Inputs from** known-answer case `ka-cavity-wall` — Insulated cavity wall, a window and natural ventilation. Status: Confirmed (analytic; see Appendix A for what is and is not checked against a primary source).

The layer conductivities in this case are illustrative inputs, not design data (the spec says so). The window has its own U (1.4). Natural ventilation. The `elements` table has an optional `flow` column (blank = horizontal; `upward` for a roof or ceiling, `downward` for a floor) that sets the internal surface resistance; this case leaves it blank. The roof case at the end of the script uses it.

**Values to enter** (a new sheet opens on the declared defaults only; caption is `symbol — description`):

| Input | Value | Unit | On a new sheet |
|---|---|---|---|
| `T_int` — Internal design temperature | 21 | degC | already set |
| `theta_e` — External design temperature (location record) | -3 | degC | already set |
| `U_target` — Target U for built-up elements without their own target (optional; blank = no global target) | 0.3 | W/(m^2*K) | type it |
| `n` — Air change rate | 0.5 | 1/h | already set |
| `V` — Room volume | 40 | m^3 | already set |

**Table inputs.** A new sheet opens with every table empty and the form has no row editor (D-37), so load these by import. Copy this block to the clipboard from PowerShell, then press **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)**:

```powershell
@'
[{"Symbol":"elements","Rows":[{"Cells":{"name":{"Text":"Cavity wall"},"area":{"Number":20,"Unit":"m^2"}}},{"Cells":{"name":{"Text":"Window"},"area":{"Number":3,"Unit":"m^2"},"U_user":{"Number":1.4,"Unit":"W/(m^2*K)"}}}]},{"Symbol":"layers","Rows":[{"Cells":{"element":{"Text":"Cavity wall"},"name":{"Text":"Outer brick leaf"},"d":{"Number":102.5,"Unit":"mm"},"lambda":{"Number":0.84,"Unit":"W/(m*K)"}}},{"Cells":{"element":{"Text":"Cavity wall"},"name":{"Text":"Insulation"},"d":{"Number":100,"Unit":"mm"},"lambda":{"Number":0.03,"Unit":"W/(m*K)"}}},{"Cells":{"element":{"Text":"Cavity wall"},"name":{"Text":"Inner block leaf"},"d":{"Number":100,"Unit":"mm"},"lambda":{"Number":0.2,"Unit":"W/(m*K)"}}},{"Cells":{"element":{"Text":"Cavity wall"},"name":{"Text":"Plaster"},"d":{"Number":13,"Unit":"mm"},"lambda":{"Number":0.4,"Unit":"W/(m*K)"}}}]}]
'@ | Set-Clipboard
```

| # | Action | Expected | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Open **Engineering → Modules → Calculations → Worksheets**. Pick **U-values and room heat loss** in the catalogue (or press Ctrl+K and type its name). | The right pane heading reads **U-values and room heat loss**. The reference line starts `bs.room-heat-loss@1.6.0  ·  hash`. | |
| 2 | Before you type or import anything, read the governing bar. | The bar reads `Blocked — elements: enter at least one row of 'Building elements bounding the room' (the table takes up to 60 rows): fill in name (Element), area (Area, m^2).`. A blocked bar names the next thing to enter. This reading comes from the template defaults alone; it is not a result to keep. | |
| 3 | Copy the JSON block above to the clipboard and press **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)**. Do this first: an import re-seeds the whole form. | Status line reads `Imported 2 input(s).` Nothing is reported as skipped or blocked. | |
| 4 | Enter the values marked *type it* or *pick it* in the table above. Leave every unit picker on the unit shown. | No red or orange problem text appears under the inputs you entered. The *Recalculating…* marker clears. | |
| 5 | Compare the **Results** list with the table of expected outputs below. | Each result matches the *Form shows* column. Where the KAT tolerance is tighter than the form's rounding (4 or 5 significant figures), the *Form shows* column is the test. | |
| 6 | Read the governing bar above the results. | The bar reads `PASS — U-value of Cavity wall against its target: utilisation 0.80`. | |
| 7 | Read the checks list under the results (governing first). | PASS — U-value of Cavity wall against its target: 0.2405 W/(m²·K) vs 0.3000 W/(m²·K) (u = 0.80). | |
| 8 | Read the notices under the checks. | Warnings, not failures. One `Warning — <symbol>: a tabulated value not taken from a reference record (<record kind>); enter it as one so its source, edition and licence travel with it.` for each of `theta_e` (External design temperature); `layers` (Layer thermal conductivity); `y_tb` (Thermal bridging allowance); `T_int` (Internal design temperatures); `n` (Air change rates). Record any other notice you see. No *Block* line appears. | |
| 9 | Tick **Show working**. | Numbered steps appear, each as formula, substitution, result, with the clause tag (« …) where the step cites one. **Go to step** jumps to the governing step. | |
| 10 | Variation — Tighten the target. Set `U_target` to 0.15 W/(m^2*K). | Governing bar reads `FAIL — U-value of Cavity wall against its target: utilisation 1.61`. The check reads `0.2406 W/(m²·K) vs 0.1500 W/(m²·K)`. The target applies to external, built-up elements only: the window (own U, 1.4) has no check line. | |
| 11 | Cross-check against a second worksheet. The older Calculators surface is gone (section 1.8). Open **Plane wall heat transfer (layers and films)** (`bs.plane-wall`) and enter the same wall layers and films. Compare its U-value with `U_1`. | Record both values. They agree when both sheets use the same layers and the same surface resistances. A difference is a finding: note it. | |

| Output | Meaning | Expected | Tolerance | Form shows | Result |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `R_1` | Cavity wall: thermal resistance | 4.157857 m^2*K/W | 1e-6 | 4.158 m²·K/W | |
| `U_1` | Cavity wall: U-value | 0.2405085 W/(m^2*K) | 1e-6 | 0.2405 W/(m²·K) | |

| `q_v` | Ventilation air flow | 20 m^3/h | 1e-9 (exact) | 20 m³/h | |

**Expected verdict:** PASS (governing: U-value of Cavity wall against its target). **Script result:** (blank)  **Tester / date:** (blank)

**B4 second case: a roof and a heated neighbour (`ka-roof-upward`).** This case tests the `flow` column and the rule that a target applies to external elements only.

Start from a fresh B4 sheet (pick another worksheet, then B4 again). Copy this block, press **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)**, then type `U_target` 0.5 W/(m^2*K), `n` 0.1 1/h and `V` 5 m^3. Leave `T_int` 21 degC and `theta_e` -3 degC.

```powershell
@'
[{"Symbol":"layers","Rows":[{"Cells":{"element":{"Text":"Roof"},"name":{"Text":"Insulation"},"d":{"Number":100,"Unit":"mm"},"lambda":{"Number":0.05,"Unit":"W/(m*K)"}}},{"Cells":{"element":{"Text":"Partition"},"name":{"Text":"Board"},"d":{"Number":100,"Unit":"mm"},"lambda":{"Number":0.5,"Unit":"W/(m*K)"}}}]},{"Symbol":"elements","Rows":[{"Cells":{"name":{"Text":"Roof"},"flow":{"Text":"upward"},"area":{"Number":10,"Unit":"m^2"}}},{"Cells":{"name":{"Text":"Partition"},"area":{"Number":12,"Unit":"m^2"},"theta_adj":{"Number":18,"Unit":"degC"}}}]}]
'@ | Set-Clipboard
```

|---|---|---|---|

| 2 | Read `U_2`. | `U_2` shows 2.174 W/(m²·K). The partition to the 18 °C neighbour has no check line against the outside target. | |

#### 6.25.1 Long form T-E7: PCB trace width (IPC-2221)

**Written for version 1.0.2; brought to version 1.3.0 on 2026-10-27.** The Addendum C fixes touched this template: `elec.pcb-trace-02`. Every number, name and message below is what version 1.3.0 shows.

E7 is the legacy IPC-2221 curve-fit. The sheet labels it "legacy IPC-2221 curve-fit, conservative for multilayer boards". The constants (`k_ext`, `k_int`, the two exponents, the copper thickness per ounce) are published closed-form constants in a method record; the sheet warns until you apply one (section 2.11).

**Inputs from** worked example `motor-driver-supply-rail` (`docs\worked-examples\electrical\elec.pcb-trace\motor-driver-supply-rail`) — Motor-driver supply rail on a 2 oz outer layer, 5 A at 20 K rise. Status: Confirmed (analytic; recomputed in python3 in the repository).

5 A, 20 K rise, 2 oz copper on an external layer, 100 mm long, drawn 2.5 mm wide, ambient 25 °C.

The worked example carries a source on every input. Copy its inputs to the clipboard from PowerShell, run from the repository root, then press **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)** (step 3):

```powershell
Get-Content -Raw docs\worked-examples\electrical\elec.pcb-trace\motor-driver-supply-rail\inputs.json | Set-Clipboard   # 14 inputs
```

| # | Action | Expected | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Open **Engineering → Modules → Calculations → Worksheets**. Pick **PCB trace width (IPC-2221)** in the catalogue (or press Ctrl+K and type its name). | The right pane heading reads **PCB trace width (IPC-2221)**. The reference line starts `elec.pcb-trace@1.3.0  ·  hash`. | |
| 2 | Before you type or import anything, read the governing bar. | The bar reads `PASS — Minimum trace width against the width as drawn: utilisation 0.68`. This reading comes from the template defaults alone; it is not a result to keep. | |
| 3 | Run the PowerShell line above and press **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)**. Do this first: an import re-seeds the whole form. | Status line reads `Imported 14 input(s).` Nothing is reported as skipped or blocked. | |
| 4 | Check each input against the *Inputs* table of the example's `README.md`. Change nothing. | Every value, unit and source matches. The *Recalculating…* marker clears. | |
| 5 | Compare the **Results** list with the table of expected outputs below. | Each result matches the *Form shows* column. Where the tolerance is tighter than the form's rounding (4 or 5 significant figures), the *Form shows* column is the test. | |
| 6 | Read the governing bar above the results. | The bar reads `PASS — Minimum trace width against the width as drawn: utilisation 0.36`. | |
| 7 | Read the checks list under the results (governing first). | PASS — Minimum trace width against the width as drawn: 0.9079 mm vs 2.500 mm (u = 0.36). | |
| 8 | Read the notices under the checks. | Warnings, not failures. One `Warning — <symbol>: a tabulated value not taken from a reference record (<record kind>); enter it as one so its source, edition and licence travel with it.` for each of `k_ext`, `k_int`, `n_dT`, `n_A`, `t_oz` (IPC-2221 legacy curve-fit method constants); `rho_20`, `alpha` (Copper material constants). Record any other notice you see. No *Block* line appears. | |
| 9 | Tick **Show working**. | Numbered steps appear, each as formula, substitution, result, with the clause tag (« …) where the step cites one. **Go to step** jumps to the governing step. | |
| 10 | Variation — Draw the trace narrower. Set `w_act` to 0.8 mm. Afterwards put `w_act` back to 2.5 mm. | Governing bar reads `FAIL — Minimum trace width against the width as drawn: utilisation 1.14`. `w_min` shows 0.9080 mm, `u_w` shows 1.14. Warning: `w_act: the trace is narrower than the minimum for 20 K, so it runs about 24.6 K above ambient. The resistance, drop and loss use that rise, so widen the trace.` | |
| 11 | Variation — Carry more current. Set `I` to 36 A. Afterwards put `I` back to 5 A. | The input is refused: the hard maximum of `I` is 35 A (DC-18). Governing bar reads `Blocked — I: enter at most 35 A (Trace current (the curve-fit was drawn for up to 35 A: above it the sheet is refused)); 36 A was given.` No result is shown. | |
| 12 | Variation — Type a thickness in micrometres. Set `Cu_oz` to 18 (an 18 µm foil typed as ounces). Afterwards put `Cu_oz` back to 2. | The input is refused: the hard maximum of the copper weight is 6 oz/ft², so 17.5, 18, 35, 70 and 105 are all refused. Governing bar reads `Blocked — Cu_oz: enter at most 6 (Finished copper weight in oz/ft² (0.5 to 6), not a thickness in µm: 17.5 µm is ½ oz, 35 µm is 1 oz, 70 µm is 2 oz, 105 µm is 3 oz); 18 was given.` Before version 1.0.2 an 18 typed here ran and gave a PASS at u 0.04 where the real half ounce fails. | |

#### 6.29.1 Long form T-M9: Mechanism kinematics

**Written for version 1.2.0; brought to version 1.3.0 on 2026-10-27.** Every number, name and message below is what version 1.3.0 shows.

M9 has two worked examples, a four-bar linkage and a cam. Run both. The sweep outputs (crank angle, coupler angle, torque and the like) are series of 73 values; the form shows them as "(73 items)". The loop-closure residual checks are self-consistency checks: they stay at about 1e-14 mm.

**Part A. Four-bar crank-rocker**

**Inputs from** worked example `carton-tucker-crank-rocker` (`docs\worked-examples\mechanical\mech.mechanism\carton-tucker-crank-rocker`) — Carton flap tucker: crank-rocker 150/45/140/100 mm at 60 cycles/min. Status: Confirmed (analytic; recomputed in python3 in the repository).

A 150 / 45 / 140 / 100 mm four-bar (ground, crank, coupler, rocker) turned at 60 rpm against a 4 N·m load that opposes the motion. The crank is the shortest link, so it is a Grashof crank-rocker.

The worked example carries a source on every input. Copy its inputs to the clipboard from PowerShell, run from the repository root, then press **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)** (step 3):

```powershell
Get-Content -Raw docs\worked-examples\mechanical\mech.mechanism\carton-tucker-crank-rocker\inputs.json | Set-Clipboard   # 14 inputs
```

| # | Action | Expected | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Open **Engineering → Modules → Calculations → Worksheets**. Pick **Mechanism kinematics** in the catalogue (or press Ctrl+K and type its name). | The right pane heading reads **Mechanism kinematics**. The reference line starts `mech.mechanism@1.3.0  ·  hash`. | |
| 2 | Before you type or import anything, read the governing bar. | The bar reads `PASS — Required transmission angle against the smallest transmission angle: utilisation 0.80`. This reading comes from the template defaults alone; it is not a result to keep. | |
| 3 | Run the PowerShell line above and press **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)**. Do this first: an import re-seeds the whole form. | Status line reads `Imported 14 input(s).` Nothing is reported as skipped or blocked. | |
| 4 | Check each input against the *Inputs* table of the example's `README.md`. Change nothing. | Every value, unit and source matches. The *Recalculating…* marker clears. | |
| 5 | Compare the **Results** list with the table of expected outputs below. | Each result matches the *Form shows* column. Where the tolerance is tighter than the form's rounding (4 or 5 significant figures), the *Form shows* column is the test. | |
| 6 | Read the governing bar above the results. | The bar reads `PASS — Required transmission angle against the smallest transmission angle: utilisation 0.83`. | |
| 7 | Read the checks list under the results (governing first). | PASS — Required transmission angle against the smallest transmission angle: 40° vs 48.44° (u = 0.83); PASS — Loop-closure residual against its limit: max residual = 9.3095e-14 mm vs < 1e-9 mm; PASS — Velocity-loop residual against its limit: max residual = 2.1412e-16 vs < 1e-8. | |
| 8 | Read the notices under the checks. | No warning and no default note. Record any other notice you see. No *Block* line appears. | |
| 9 | Tick **Show working**. | Numbered steps appear, each as formula, substitution, result, with the clause tag (« …) where the step cites one. **Go to step** jumps to the governing step. | |
| 10 | Variation — Demand a larger transmission angle. Set `mu_req` to 50 deg. Afterwards put `mu_req` back to 40 deg. | Governing bar reads `FAIL — Required transmission angle against the smallest transmission angle: utilisation 1.04`. `mu_min` shows 48.44°, `u_mu` shows 1.04. | |
| 11 | Variation — Reach a toggle. Set `L1` to 60 mm, `L2` to 40 mm, `L3` to 50 mm and `L4` to 35 mm, and leave `mu_req` at 40 deg (the geometry of the regression test `Yellow_Y_B_03`; the rest of the example is laid over it). Afterwards put the four lengths back to 150, 45, 140 and 100 mm. | The sweep reaches a toggle (`mu_min` shows 0°), so there is no finite ratio. `u_mu` reads `— (μ_min = 0°: the sweep reaches a toggle, so there is no ratio)`. Governing bar reads `FAIL — Required transmission angle against the smallest transmission angle`, with no utilisation. Its check reads `μ_min = 0° (toggle in the sweep) vs μ_req = 40°`. The sheet prints no `1000000000`. Warnings say the linkage is not a Grashof crank, the sweep is cut to 0° … 114.95°, and the peaks, torque and forces are not reported (DB-04). | |

**Expected outputs** (worked example `carton-tucker-crank-rocker`; the expected values are the README's, to six significant figures; the *Form shows* column is the test):

| Output | Meaning | Expected (README) | Form shows | Result |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `S_plus_L` | Shortest plus longest link | 195 mm | 195 mm | |
| `P_plus_Q` | Sum of the other two links | 240 mm | 240 mm | |
| `theta4_0` | Rocker angle at the first input angle | 93.8909 deg | 93.89° | |
| `mu_min` | Smallest transmission angle over the sweep | 48.4409 deg | 48.44° | |
| `swing` | Rocker swing over the sweep | 54.7864 deg | 54.79° | |
| `omega_out_pk` | Peak output (rocker) angular velocity | 3.54346 rad/s | 3.543 rad/s | |
| `output_motion` | Peak speed of the output pin at the rocker length c (a kinematic proxy for a cylinder only if the cylinder acts perpendicular to the rocker at that radius; the angular velocity is omega_out_pk) | 0.354346 m/s | 0.3543 m/s | |
| `peak_accel` | Peak acceleration of the output pin | 3.16246 m/s^2 | 3.162 m/s² | |
| `input_torque` | Peak input (crank) torque by virtual work | 2.26544 N*m | 2.265 N·m | |

**Expected verdict:** PASS (governing: Transmission angle μ_min at or above the required minimum). 

**Part B. Cam with an offset roller follower**

**Inputs from** worked example `indexing-cam-offset-roller` (`docs\worked-examples\mechanical\mech.mechanism\indexing-cam-offset-roller`) — Indexing-station cam: offset roller follower, cycloidal rise and 3-4-5 return, 120 rpm. Status: Confirmed (analytic; recomputed in python3 in the repository).

A 40 mm base circle cam with an 8 mm roller, 10 mm offset, 20 mm cycloidal rise over 120°, a 3-4-5 polynomial return over 120° and two 60° dwells, at 120 rpm against a 5 N/mm spring with 20 N preload.

#### 6.36.1 Long form T-E9: Motor start voltage dip and PF correction

**Written for version 1.2.0; brought to version 1.3.0 on 2026-10-27.** Every number, name and message below is what version 1.3.0 shows.

E9 gives the voltage dip at the motor terminals and at the point of common coupling when a motor starts, and the capacitor kvar to a target power factor. It is a screening: the dip is a lower bound when only the transformer terminals are modelled. The two examples leave seven inputs at their template defaults (fault level basis and the soft-starter and drive data), so the sheet warns about each.

**Part A. Direct-on-line start from a 500 kVA transformer**

**Inputs from** worked example `pump-dol-500kva` (`docs\worked-examples\electrical\elec.motor-start\pump-dol-500kva`) — 75 kW pump motor started direct on line from a 500 kVA transformer. Status: Confirmed (analytic; recomputed in python3 in the repository).

A 75 kW pump motor on 40 m of 50 mm² cable, started direct on line, supplied by a 500 kVA transformer with 4 % impedance.

The worked example carries a source on every input. Copy its inputs to the clipboard from PowerShell, run from the repository root, then press **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)** (step 3):

```powershell
Get-Content -Raw docs\worked-examples\electrical\elec.motor-start\pump-dol-500kva\inputs.json | Set-Clipboard   # 23 inputs
```

| # | Action | Expected | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Open **Engineering → Modules → Calculations → Worksheets**. Pick **Motor start voltage dip and PF correction** in the catalogue (or press Ctrl+K and type its name). | The right pane heading reads **Motor start voltage dip and PF correction**. The reference line starts `elec.motor-start@1.3.0  ·  hash`. | |
| 2 | Before you type or import anything, read the governing bar. | The bar reads `PASS — Motor-terminal dip against the limit: utilisation 0.54`. This reading comes from the template defaults alone; it is not a result to keep. | |
| 3 | Run the PowerShell line above and press **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)**. Do this first: an import re-seeds the whole form. | Status line reads `Imported 23 input(s).` Nothing is reported as skipped or blocked. | |
| 4 | Check each input against the *Inputs* table of the example's `README.md`. Change nothing. | Every value, unit and source matches. The *Recalculating…* marker clears. | |
| 5 | Compare the **Results** list with the table of expected outputs below. | Each result matches the *Form shows* column. Where the tolerance is tighter than the form's rounding (4 or 5 significant figures), the *Form shows* column is the test. | |
| 6 | Read the governing bar above the results. | The bar reads `PASS — Point-of-common-coupling dip against the limit: utilisation 0.93`. | |
| 7 | Read the checks list under the results (governing first). | PASS — Point-of-common-coupling dip against the limit: 4.675 % vs 5 % (u = 0.93); PASS — Motor-terminal dip against the limit: 7.776 % vs 10 % (u = 0.78). | |
| 8 | Read the notices under the checks. | Warnings, not failures. One `Warning — <symbol>: a tabulated value not taken from a reference record (<record kind>); enter it as one so its source, edition and licence travel with it.` for each of `X_km` (Cable impedance (resistance and reactance per kilometre)); `rho_20`, `alpha` (Conductor material constants); `I_LR_r`, `cos_phi_st` (Motor starting data); `k_soft`, `k_vfd` (Soft-starter and drive limits); `u_k`, `XR` (Transformer impedance data). Record any other notice you see. No *Block* line appears. | |

**Expected outputs** (worked example `pump-dol-500kva`; the expected values are the README's, to six significant figures; the *Form shows* column is the test):

| Output | Meaning | Expected (README) | Form shows | Result |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `I_rated` | Motor rated current | 131.671 A | 131.67 A | |
| `I_start` | Starting current at rated voltage | 921.696 A | 921.70 A | |
| `Z_src` | Source impedance (magnitude) | 0.0128 ohm | 0.01280 Ω | |
| `I_dip` | Starting current at the dipped voltage | 850.027 A | 850.03 A | |
| `V_m` | Voltage at the motor terminals while starting | 368.897 V | 368.9 V | |
| `start_dip` | Voltage dip at the motor terminals | 7.77574 % | 7.776 % | |
| `dip_pcc` | Voltage dip at the point of common coupling (governing) | 4.67456 % | 4.675 % | |

**Part B. Star-delta start on a weak supply**

**Inputs from** worked example `pump-star-delta-weak-supply` (`docs\worked-examples\electrical\elec.motor-start\pump-star-delta-weak-supply`) — 160 kW pump motor started star-delta from a 500 kVA transformer on a weak 15 MVA network. Status: Confirmed (analytic; recomputed in python3 in the repository).

A 160 kW pump motor on 60 m of 95 mm² cable, started star-delta, behind the same transformer and a 15 MVA upstream network.
