## Part 7 — Security spot checks

These checks try to break the guards of the program. Each one should be refused, and nothing should be saved or changed. They come last so that they do not disturb your earlier results. Record the exact refusal text for each one.

The sources are the Security and Red reviews of Addenda A, B and C (`docs/reviews/security-review.md` and `red-team.md`). The reviews found no open Critical or High issue. These steps let you see the guards work on your own machine.

Use a spare data folder for the steps that change state, for example `$HOME\TempestOS-Test-Data-security`. Do not use the folder that holds your real results.

### 7.1 The licensed export lock

A value taken from a licensed record never leaves the program in an export. The lock follows the record, not one revision. Part 2 tested the mechanism. These steps check the cases that matter most.

| Step | Action | Expect | Guided | Result |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 7.1-1 | Do steps R5 and R6 of T-REC (section 2.8). Export a sheet that cites a licensed record. Then import the CSV again. | The licensed rows have an empty value and read `record:<id>@<rev> · licensed value withheld`. The re-import is refused. Nothing is imported. | `import-w3` |  |
| 7.1-2 | Import a record from a CSV on the records screen with **Licensed source** ticked. Apply it to a sheet. Press **Export inputs (CSV)**. | The record's values are withheld from the export. A table pinned to it is written as its own row with no rows. | `import-13` |  |
| 7.1-3 | Look on the records screen for any way to export a record's values. | There is none. No button exports. After a save the screen shows symbols and row counts, never the values of a licensed record. Record that no button exists (step R30). | `import-13` |  |
| 7.1-4 | Revise a licensed record. Import a file with the same title. Tick **Save as the next revision…** and untick **Licensed source**. | The new revision is still `LICENSED (never exported)`. A revision cannot clear the flag. | `import-13` |  |
| 7.1-5 | Issue a sheet that cites a licensed table (T-REC step R17). Open the PDF. | The PDF prints the record pin and `rows withheld (licensed record); the values used appear in the working`. No table row is printed. | `import-w3` |  |
| 7.1-6 | Apply a licensed material to a sheet (T-W3 step 9), then export the inputs. | The material's values are withheld from the CSV. | `import-w3` |  |

### 7.2 Hostile import refusal

Every import reads untrusted bytes through the same strict reader. It reads at most 5 MB, accepts only strict UTF-8 and refuses control and bidirectional characters. It never evaluates an expression. A refused file leaves the calculation exactly as it was.

#### 7.2.1 Inputs, CAD and scan files

| Step | Action | Expect | Guided | Result |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 7.2.1-1 | Copy a CSV that holds `symbol,value` and `P,=1+1`. Open E4. Press **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)**. | `Nothing imported: Row 2: P looks like a spreadsheet formula and was refused.` | none |  |
| 7.2.1-2 | On E6, import a CSV that numbers a table's rows with a gap. Use `loads[1]` and `loads[3]` but no `loads[2]`. | Nothing is imported. The message says that the rows must be numbered 1 to n without gaps and names the missing row. | none |  |
| 7.2.1-3 | Import JSON with the same symbol twice. | Nothing is imported. The notice names the symbol, says how many times it appears and says to keep one. | none |  |
| 7.2.1-4 | In T-W1 step 6, import a CAD file whose right-hand side is an expression (`[mm]derived=plate_len-spacer_len`). | The preview lists it as read-only text that is **never evaluated**. No input takes its value. | `import-w1` |  |
| 7.2.1-5 | Make a 6 MB file with the command below. Pick it in **Choose scan table…** and in **Choose CAD file…**. | Both are refused. The cap is 5 MB. Nothing is applied. Record the message. | `import-w1` |  |
| 7.2.1-6 | In the `groove.csv` of T-W2, change one `Actual` cell to `=1+1` and import it. | The cell is refused as a spreadsheet formula, or the table is. Nothing is applied. | `import-w2` |  |

```powershell
$w = "$HOME\TempestOS-Test-Data\w2"; New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force $w | Out-Null
[IO.File]::WriteAllBytes("$w\big.csv", (New-Object byte[] 6291456))
```

#### 7.2.2 Record files on the records screen

| Step | Action | Expect | Guided | Result |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 7.2.2-1 | Choose the 6 MB file from the command above on the records screen. | It is refused in one sentence that names the size cap. Nothing is saved. | `import-13` |  |
| 7.2.2-2 | Make a file for template `04` with 1,100 data rows. Choose it. | It is refused in one sentence that names the row cap and says to split the file. Nothing is saved. | `import-13` |  |
| 7.2.2-3 | Make a file for template `04` with 50 good rows and one row whose `I_t` is `x`. Choose it. | The preview shows the error with its line. **Import file** is disabled. None of the 51 rows is saved (all or nothing). | `import-13` |  |
| 7.2.2-4 | Set the **Number format** to decimal comma. Choose a comma-delimited file with an unquoted number such as `1,5` in the `CSA` column. | The file is refused. The message says the number has no quotes. It says the fields are separated by commas and the number format is a decimal comma. Nothing is saved (SEC-C-01). | `import-13` |  |
| 7.2.2-5 | Put a formula prefix in the **Title** (for example `=1+1`) and choose a good file. | The title is refused. A formula prefix is not allowed in text. | `import-13` |  |
| 7.2.2-6 | Edit a copy of `_provenance.csv`: set `origin` to `7` for one row. Press **Load provenance file…**. | The row is refused. An origin must be a defined name, not a number (SEC-C-04). | `import-13` |  |
| 7.2.2-7 | Pick a unit of another kind in a column header (volts for amps). | `WRONG unit` shows at once. Nothing can be imported (step R25). | `import-13` |  |

### 7.3 Independence, issued records and stale records

| Step | Action | Expect | Guided | Result |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 7.3-1 | As the person who created an E4, E5 or S6 sheet, press **Record check** (T-IND step I1). | Refused. The message says the calculation needs an independent check and names its author. | none |  |
| 7.3-2 | Edit an input as a second person and then check as that person (T-IND step I3). | Refused. A person who changed an input counts as an author. | none |  |
| 7.3-3 | Edit an issued sheet, or a superseded one (T-00 steps 6 and 9). | The form is read-only. Revise starts a new revision. | none |  |
| 7.3-4 | Revise a record that a calculation cites. Open the calculation. | It is stale until you apply the record again. A stale calculation cannot be checked or issued. | `import-13` |  |
| 7.3-5 | On S6, type a made-up reference with source *evidence* in `profis_ref`. | The anchorage condition does not pass. An evidence source must point to a file attached to the calculation's own Evidence. | none |  |
| 7.3-6 | On S8 (flag on), attest with the name `Nobody` and the qualification `None` (T-S8 step C16). | It is accepted as typed. This is the known limit: nothing authenticates a typed name or its competence. Record it. | `lifting-4` |  |
| 7.3-7 | Open **Settings** → **Switch person…** and switch to another person. | It works with no password. This is the accepted platform limit (Addendum B). Record that you saw it. | none |  |

### 7.4 Demo remove scope

Removal is the one delete that the Addendum C features make. These steps prove it keeps to the demo. Do the issued-record step on a spare data folder, because an issued demo can never be removed.

| Step | Action | Expect | Guided | Result |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 7.4-1 | Note the number of projects, calculations, packs and records you hold outside the demo. Install the demo, then remove it (Part 3). | The numbers are the same before and after. Your own rows are unchanged. | none |  |
| 7.4-2 | Install the demo. Leave it open. Press **Remove demo project**. | Refused. The status line reads `Close the demo project 'Demo — Hydraulic Press Cell' before removing it.` | none |  |
| 7.4-3 | On a spare data folder, install the demo. Check one demo pack as a second person and issue it. Close the project. Press **Remove demo project**. | Removal is refused and names the record that is issued. Nothing is deleted. | none |  |
| 7.4-4 | Start the app on a new empty data folder. Open **Settings** → **Demo project**. | The status reads `The demo project is not installed.` Nothing installs by itself, at start-up or when a database is empty. | none |  |
| 7.4-5 | Run `dotnet test tests/Tempest.Calc.Tests.Library --configuration Release --no-build --filter "FullyQualifiedName~DemoProjectTests|FullyQualifiedName~AddendumCSecurityRegressionTests|FullyQualifiedName~AddendumCColourRegressionTests"`. | Pass. They show four things. A scan profile you saved under the demo's id survives Remove (SEC-C-03). No other project's profile is deleted (B-C-02). Nothing can be created in a removed project (SEC-C-05). | none |  |

### 7.5 The Lifting feature flag

S8 and the lifting lug hang a load over people. They stay hidden until a machine administrator switches the flag on. The flag is read once, at start-up. Only the machine policy counts: the `HKLM` DWORD `Lifting` = 1 on Windows, or the root-owned `/etc/tempestos/features.json` on Linux and macOS.

| Step | Action | Expect | Guided | Result |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 7.5-1 | With the flag off, look for the lifting worksheets (T-S8 step A1). | Neither is listed anywhere. Ctrl+K finds neither. | `lifting-1` |  |
| 7.5-2 | Try four ways, one at a time, and restart after each (T-S8 step A2). The ways are an `appsettings.json` beside the executable, the variable `Calc__Features__Lifting`, the switch `--Calc:Features:Lifting=true`, and the policy value under `HKCU`. | S8 stays hidden after each one. Undo each one before the next. | none |  |
| 7.5-3 | Set the `HKLM` value to the string `1`. Then set it to the DWORD `0`. Then set it to the DWORD `2` (T-S8 step A3). | S8 stays hidden after each one. Only the DWORD 1 switches it on. | none |  |
| 7.5-4 | Set the DWORD `Lifting` = 1 as administrator. Restart. | Both lifting worksheets appear. The log holds a warning that the optional feature is enabled, with its source. | `lifting-2` |  |
| 7.5-5 | Remove the value. Restart. | The worksheets are hidden again. An issued S8 sheet stays readable. Nothing can create, import or issue S8. | `lifting-6` |  |

### 7.6 The Guided test and the dashboard change nothing

Both are read-only over calculations. The guided run writes only its own test-run record.

| Step | Action | Expect | Guided | Result |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 7.6-1 | Count the calculations and Evidence records in a project. Run three guided steps, including **Compare with the form** and **Record result**. Count again. | The counts are the same. A guided run never saves, checks or issues a calculation. | none |  |
| 7.6-2 | Start a guided run. Switch person. Open **Guided test**. Try to record a result in the first person's unfinished run. | Only the person who started a run can add to it. The second person cannot. Nobody can start or add to a run while nobody is signed in. | none |  |
| 7.6-3 | Finish a run. Try to change one of its results. | A finished run is read-only. | none |  |
| 7.6-4 | Type a note of more than 2,000 characters and press **Record result**. | The note stays whole in the box. A count under it says how far over it is. Recording is refused until you shorten it. It is never cut. | none |  |
| 7.6-5 | Record the note `<b>x</b> & y / z`. Press **Copy results sheet**. Paste it into a viewer. | The note shows as written. `<`, `>` and `&` are encoded in the markdown. | none |  |
| 7.6-6 | Press **Save results sheet…** and name the file `notes.txt`. | The file is saved as `notes.txt.md`. A name with another ending gets `.md` added. | none |  |
| 7.6-7 | On the dashboard press **Export markdown…**, name the file `report.txt`, and save it. Export again with the same name. | The first export saves `report.txt.md`. The second would replace it, so nothing is saved and the screen says so. | none |  |
| 7.6-8 | On the dashboard press **Export PDF…** and cancel the file dialog. | Nothing is saved. | none |  |
| 7.6-9 | Run `dotnet test tests/Tempest.Calc.Tests.Library --configuration Release --no-build --filter "FullyQualifiedName~GuidedRunNoSideEffectsTests"`. | Pass. The run service holds no calculation or Evidence service. | none |  |

### 7.7 What these checks do not cover

- **Signatures are not authenticated.** Anyone who can use **Switch person…** can hold every role. Every sheet that depends on it says so.
- **The lock follows the provenance, not the numbers.** If you retype a licensed value with another source, it exports (DA-18).
- **A person who patches the application binaries can bypass any gate.** A configuration gate cannot stop code tampering.
- **Two app instances on one database** could each install the demo (RR-C-06, accepted).

## Appendix A — Unconfirmed and Secondary values

This appendix is for checking against your own books. It lists the known-answer cases whose reference value could not be checked against its source, and the sources nobody pinned. You check each one against your own printed copy. You can do it at the same time as Parts 2 to 6. The text is kept whole from the earlier packs.

Unconfirmed means the reference value could not be checked. Secondary means it comes from a solutions manual, a website, course notes or a vendor check. The Verification dashboard shows the counts for each calculator.

A KAT is **Unconfirmed** when its expected values could not be verified against the primary source. Each is still run by the test suite against the repository's own computation. This list is your verification checklist. There are **13** Unconfirmed KAT cases over the 28 templates of Addendum A and the base set: 10 in Addendum A and 3 in the base set (U-01 to U-13). Addendum B adds 17 more, six of them also Secondary (section A.5, U-14 to U-30). That makes **30** in all. S8 adds a seventh Secondary case that is not Unconfirmed (section A.6). I counted them from the `CalcReferenceStatus.Unconfirmed` tags in the code. The "location text" is quoted from each case's `CalcReferenceSource`.

### A.1 Unconfirmed test cases (grep `Unconfirmed`)

**U-01 M4 `mech.shaft` — case `ka-shigley-7-1`** (Shoulder fatigue: 1260 lbf·in reversed bending, 1100 lbf·in steady torque, d = 1.100 in (Shigley Ch. 7 example))

- Source to check: Budynas & Nisbett, Shigley's Mechanical Engineering Design.
- Edition: 10th ed., Ch. 7, Example 7-1.
- Location text in the repository: Data of the example converted to SI exactly: M 1260 lbf·in = 142.361 N·m, T 1100 lbf·in = 124.283 N·m, d 1.100 in = 27.94 mm, S_ut 105 kpsi = 723.949 MPa, S_y 82 kpsi = 565.370 MPa; machined, R 0.99, K_t 1.68, K_ts 1.42, q 0.85, q_s 0.92; computed here (python3, independent): k_a 0.78769, k_b 0.86831, k_e 0.81389, S_e 201.50 MPa (29.23 kpsi), σ_a 104.91 MPa, τ_m 40.23 MPa, n_f (DE-Goodman) 1.621, n_y 4.489. The book's printed answers are to be compared by the owner against their copy; K_t, K_ts, q and q_s are those of the earlier version of this case and are equally to be checked
- Expected values: `S_e` = 201.4997 MPa (relative tolerance 1e-5); `n_f_min` = 1.620988 (relative tolerance 1e-5); `n_y_min` = 4.488973 (relative tolerance 1e-5).
- What to do: find the example in your copy. Compare its inputs and printed answers with the values above. Note any difference. Result: (blank)

- Edition: 2018.

- Expected values: `L_10` = 535.3873 (relative tolerance 1e-6); `L_10h` = 5948.748 h (relative tolerance 1e-6); `s_0` = 4.48 (relative tolerance 1e-6).

### A.5 Addendum B: Unconfirmed and Secondary test cases (grep `Unconfirmed`, `Secondary`)

Addendum B adds **17** Unconfirmed KAT cases, numbered U-14 to U-30: S7 four (U-14 to U-17), M7 three (U-18 to U-20), E7 two (U-21, U-22), F2 two (U-23, U-30), F3 two (U-24, U-25) and S9 four (U-26 to U-29). **Six are also Secondary** (U-20 to U-25). A Secondary case takes its value from a secondary source: a solutions manual, a website, course notes, a vendor example or a number that Addendum B itself quotes. It does not take it from the primary publication (§B4). Each case is still run against the repository's own computation, as in section A.1. I counted both tags from the code (`CalcReferenceStatus.Unconfirmed` and `Secondary: true`).

M8, M9, E8, E9, B7, B8, B9, F1 and S8 carry **no** Unconfirmed case: their cases are analytic and Confirmed. Section A.8 still lists the sources that Addendum B names for them and that nobody pinned. The S8 case `ka-pdhonline-s106` (PDHonline course S106) is Secondary but Confirmed: the repository recomputed every published value from the sheet's equations, and they agree to the published rounding. It checks the sheet against the course example, not against BTH-1 (section 4.6.2, T-S8 step B16). Section A.6 lists it.

Where the repository records no edition for a case, the entry says so. Write down the edition you hold in the **Result** line.

**U-14 S7 `struct.ltb-interaction` — case `ka-annex-b-class2`** (Annex B, Table B.2, class 2: N = 1200 kN with M_y = 200 kN·m (ψ = 0.5) and M_z = 15 kN·m)

- Source to check: Eurocode 3: Design of steel structures — Part 1-1: General rules and rules for buildings (EN 1993-1-1), Annex B, Table B.2 (interaction factors) with the equivalent uniform moment factors table of Annex B.
- Edition: EN 1993-1-1:2005+A1:2014 (UK: BS EN 1993-1-1 with its National Annex).
- Location text in the repository: Analytic from the Annex B expressions as implemented, recomputed in python; the coefficients of Tables B.1 and B.2 are typed from the published expressions and are to be checked against the owner's copy of the standard. Same section, 6.3.2.3 (curve d), C₁ = 1.31, M_cr = 521.515 kN·m, λ̄_LT = 1.194575, χ_LT = 0.457851; C_my = C_mLT = 0.8, C_mz = 1.0; n_y = 0.1, n_z = 0.50548: k_yy = 0.848513, k_yz = 0.6·k_zz = 0.960284, k_zz = 1.600473, k_zy = 0.917840; equations 6.61 = 0.94775, 6.62 = 1.24809
- Expected values: `C_1` = 1.31 (relative tolerance 1e-12); `Mcr` = 521.5153 kN*m (relative tolerance 1e-6); `chi_LT` = 0.457851 (relative tolerance 1e-6); `k_yy` = 0.848513 (relative tolerance 1e-6); `k_yz` = 0.9602837 (relative tolerance 1e-6); `k_zy` = 0.91784 (relative tolerance 1e-6); `k_zz` = 1.600473 (relative tolerance 1e-6); `u_61` = 0.9477492 (relative tolerance 1e-6); `u_62` = 1.248094 (relative tolerance 1e-6).
- What to do: The sheet chooses the Annex B table from the input `torsion`: *susceptible to torsional deformation* uses Table B.2 (the default and the worked example), *not susceptible* uses Table B.1. Section class only picks the plastic (class 1, 2) or elastic (class 3) column. Confirm in your copy that Table B.2 is the table for members susceptible to torsional deformation and Table B.1 the table for members not susceptible. U-14 runs Table B.2, plastic; U-15 runs Table B.1, elastic. Compare the k_yy, k_yz, k_zy and k_zz expressions and the C_my, C_mz and C_mLT values for ψ = 0.5 term by term with the typed ones. Result: (blank)

- Expected values: `n_a` = 6.397887 (relative tolerance 1e-7); `q` = 1.113103 (relative tolerance 1e-9); `sigma_2` = 212.5871 MPa (relative tolerance 1e-7); `u_2` = 0.1574719 (relative tolerance 1e-7); `u_f` = 0.2204607 (relative tolerance 1e-7).
- What to do: Find the curvature (stress correction) factor q of the torsion spring in your copy. Compare it with the typed relation at C = 8 (q = 1.1131). **U-20 M7 `mech.spring` — case `ka-shigley-10-3`** (Shigley 10th ed., problem 10-3 (solutions manual): music-wire compression spring, plain and ground ends)

- Source to check: Budynas and Nisbett, Shigley's Mechanical Engineering Design, problem 10-3, and its solutions manual. **Secondary**: the answers come from the solutions manual.
- Edition: 10th ed., problem 10-3 (the repository does not say which printing of the solutions manual).
- Location text in the repository: Inputs as stated for the problem: d 2.5 mm, D 28.5 mm, n 13 active coils, plain and ground ends (n_t 14), G 81 GPa, R_m 1935.9 MPa, permissible static shear 0.45·R_m. The manual's results k = 1.3142 N/mm, K_B = 1.1174, F_s = 167.86 N and L₀ = 162.72 mm are reproduced by the sheet's method (k = 1.3142474, K_B = 1.1173709, L_s = 35 mm, F_s = π d³·0.45 R_m/(8 K_B D) = 167.8546 N, L₀ = L_s + F_s/k = 162.7191 mm); the published L₀ is entered here, which gives F_c = 167.856 N and u_c = 1.0000 at solid length. The working points, E, ρ and S_e are not part of the problem. To be checked against the owner's copy of the book and its solutions manual.
- Expected values: `C` = 11.4 (relative tolerance 1e-9); `k` = 1.3142 N/mm (relative tolerance 1e-4); `k_c` = 1.1174 (relative tolerance 1e-4); `L_s` = 35 mm (relative tolerance 1e-9); `F_c` = 167.86 N (relative tolerance 2e-4); `u_c` = 1 (relative tolerance 1e-4).
- What to do: Read the problem statement in the book (the primary source). Then read the manual's answers: k = 1.3142 N/mm, K_B = 1.1174, F_s = 167.86 N, L₀ = 162.72 mm. Say whether the book's units are SI as the repository assumes. Confirm the permissible static shear of 0.45·R_m. Result: (blank)

- Source to check: The accumulator maker's sizing guide for a mixed isothermal charge and adiabatic discharge. The repository cites Parker Hannifin, HY10-4004/UK, "as in manufacturers' sizing guides". The relation is recomputed, not pinned to a published worked example.
- Edition: No edition is recorded. Write down the guide you hold.
- Location text in the repository: V2 = V0·p0/p2 (isothermal charge), V1 = V2·(p2/p1)^(1/n) (discharge): V0 = ΔV·(p2/p0)/[(p2/p1)^(1/n) − 1] = 14 × 2.333333/(2.1^(1/1.4) − 1) = 46.74304 L, against 36.69279 L when the charge is as fast as the discharge (`ka-adiabatic`); the gas held at p2 is V0·p0/p2 = 20.03273 L and expands to 34.03273 L at p1 (ΔV = 14 L); T_min = T_max, so no temperature scaling; p2/p0 = 2.3333, u_rp = 0.58333; to be checked against the manufacturer's sizing guide
- Expected values: `V0` = 46.74303967 L (relative tolerance 1e-9); `u_r0` = 1 (1e-9); `u_rp` = 0.5833333333 (1e-9).
- What to do: Find the sizing relation for a held accumulator in your guide. Say whether it charges isothermally and discharges adiabatically, and whether it sizes on the pre-charge at the highest or the lowest operating temperature. Compare 46.74 L. This is the relation behind the High finding G-AB-01 (DB-09). Result: (blank)

## Appendix B — External oracles

Track P wrote a fuller oracle map: `docs/verification/oracles.md`. It covers 40 calculators and one pack, with the inputs to use, the output to compare and the tolerance. Use it first. The table below comes from the earlier packs. It is kept whole.

### B.1 Oracles from the earlier packs

Use these to cross-check results. None is a dependency: nothing in the build calls them. Enter the KAT inputs from the long-form scripts, then compare.

| Calculator | Oracle | Compare | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| E5 | Free MCB maximum-Zs tables from manufacturers | `Z_s_max` for MCB B, C and D at In | The calculator uses C_min × U_0 / I_a with C_min 0.95 and I_a = 5, 10 or 20 × In. A manufacturer's table may use other factors. Record the difference and the reason. KAT: 32 A type B, 230 V gives 1.3656 Ω. |
| E5 | IET Wiring Matters (March 2023) article; Schneider guide on protective conductors | `Z_s_max` 1.37 Ω; derived k 115 (PVC) and 143 (XLPE) | These are two Unconfirmed KATs (U-items in Appendix A). |
| M5 | SKF online bearing tool; NTN Bearing Wizard | `L10`, `L10h`, and the a_ISO you then type in | a_ISO comes from the manufacturer's tool; the calculator does not compute it. Record the edition (ISO 281:2007 or 1990) each tool uses. a_1 differs between them (0.64 and 0.62 at 95 %). |
| B4 | MCS heat load calculator (if you have access) | Room `Φ_HL` and `HLC` for the same room | Not available to the author. |
| B4 | OpenHeatLoss | Room totals only | **Black box only.** OpenHeatLoss is AGPL v3. Do not read, copy or port its code. Run it, read its answer, and write the number down. |
| B4 | The older calculator `calc.plane-wall-heat-transfer` | `U_1` | The Library test `RoomHeatLossAgreementTests` (5 tests) compares the two (script T-B4, last step). |
| S6 | Hilti PROFIS | Anchor design from `F_t_Ed` per bolt and `V_b_Ed` per anchor | The worksheet hands anchorage to PROFIS. File the PROFIS PDF with **Attach external report…**. |
| S4 | SCI P363 Blue Book resistance tables | Bolt shear and tension resistances | §A6 asks for agreement within 1 %. These are KATs `ka-p363-m20-88` (Unconfirmed). Values are not reproduced here. |
| B6 | Vessel manufacturer sizing guide (Flamco, Reflex, Zilmet); radiator manufacturer ΔT correction factors | `V_e`; the exponent n and `f_dT` at ΔT 30 K | §A6 lists these as KAT sources. The vessel excludes the water reserve and the safety-valve margin (DA-24), so add them before you compare. |
| E6 | PSU manufacturer derating curve (for example Phoenix Contact QUINT, Siemens SITOP); battery manufacturer data | `f_derate`; capacity factors | `ka-psu-derating` is Unconfirmed. |
| M7 | FED1+ (spring design software), or the MITcalc spring calculators | For `plunger-return-spring`: rate `k` 6.4535 N/mm, solid length `L_s` 33 mm, stress at point 2 `tau_2` 477.68 MPa, surge frequency `f_n` 207 Hz | The tools may correct the stress with another factor and take another solid-length rule. The sheet uses the EN 13906-1 solid length by default. Record both choices for each tool. Wire strength R_m(d) is your record, so enter the same R_m in both. |
| S7 | The SCI/BCSA M_cr tool, or LTBeam (CTICM) | `Mcr` 808.38 kN·m for `ub-457x191x98-beam-column` (UB 457×191×98, 5 m between restraints, ψ = 0.5, C₁ = 1.31, k = 1) | Compare M_cr only. These tools do not use the Annex A or B interaction factors. Check that each tool takes the same C₁ and the same load position. U-14 to U-17 still need your copy of the standard. |
| S8 | MechaniCalc (lug analysis) | The PDHonline S106 lug (T-S8 step B16): `Cr` 0.78, `phi` 33°, `A_v` 2.964 in², tension 25.448 kip, bearing 10.875 kip, double-plane shear 19.849 kip, single-plane fracture 20.539 kip | A lug tool may use another code basis from BTH-1. Say which. Treat the tool as a black box: run it, read its answer, write the number down. The sheet matches the course example, not BTH-1 (section 4.6.2). |
| S9 | None named in Addendum B. Use the classical coefficients of U-26, U-28 and U-29 as the check | `w_max` 0.2577 mm (simply supported square, U-26) and 0.07998 mm (clamped square, U-28) | A finite-element check of the worked-example plate is the independent oracle if you have one. |
| B7 | WUFI (Pro or Plus), or Condensa | For `internal-insulation-no-vcl`: `n_start` 11 (the first month of condensation), `Mc_max` 3022.6 g/m², `fRsi` 0.8773 | WUFI is dynamic and Glaser is steady-state, so expect WUFI to differ. The check is that the Glaser result is on the safe side and shows condensation in the same layer. The worked example's climate is invented, so enter that climate in the tool. |
| B9 | NAIMA 3E Plus | For `chilled-water-pipe-condensation`: dew point 22.012 °C, heat gain 9.593 W/m, outer surface 25.274 °C, thickness 13.162 mm (anti-condensation) and 17.814 mm (heat-gain limit) | Match the ambient, emissivity and wind. 3E Plus follows US practice and units. Say whether its surface coefficient is a fixed value or a correlation. The sheet offers both. |
| F2 | HYDAC or Parker accumulator sizing tools | For `clamp-circuit-bladder-accumulator`: `V0` 31.656 L, pre-charge `p0` 81.645 bar (absolute) and selection 32 L; for U-23 the Parker example (29.7 to 29.8 L, 32 L) | Settle gauge against absolute first. Match the gas exponent n (1 isothermal). Above 200 bar the sheet warns that the ideal-gas value is shown and takes a real-gas factor `f_rg` from you. |
| F3 | Parker inPHorm | For `locking-cylinder-piston-seal`: largest stretch 3.5104 %, largest extrusion gap 0.10606 mm, C_pk of squeeze 1.561 | inPHorm holds Parker's own size and gland tables. The sheet holds none, so enter the same ring and gland by hand. U-24 and U-25 depend on your copy of ORD 5700 and PTD 5705. |
| M9 | Linkage (the planar mechanism simulator) | For `carton-tucker-crank-rocker`: smallest transmission angle 48.441°, rocker swing 54.786°, peak output angular velocity 3.5435 rad/s | Enter the same four link lengths, the same ground, the same input speed. A cam has no oracle here. |
| E7 | Saturn PCB Toolkit | For `motor-driver-supply-rail`: `w_min` 0.9079 mm, trace resistance 10.819 mΩ, power loss 270.47 mW; for U-21 and U-22: 1.37 mm external and 3.56 mm internal | The toolkit offers more than one method. Pick the IPC-2221 curve-fit. The sheet implements the legacy curve-fit only, not the IPC-2152 charts. |

The Addendum B rows (M7 to E7) name the tools Addendum B §B10 lists. **I have not run any of them.** For each tool, run it, read its answer and write the number down. Do not copy its tables or code into the product. Record the edition or version of the tool and the method it used, because two tools can disagree for a reason that is not a defect of ours.

## Appendix C — Known limitations and open decisions

The sections below come from the earlier packs. They list the decisions, the limits of each calculator and the residual risks. The column *Your call?* says *Yes* where a decision is yours. It says *Confirm* where it is recorded and wants your agreement. It says *No* where it is information. The decisions that came after them (Addendum C) are in section 8.8 and in the handover. Some rows record the state when the earlier packs were written. A row that says Addendum B is not merged, or names an old commit, is out of date. The merge is done.

### C.2 Limitations by calculator (spec pages)

| Calculator | Limitations |
|---|---|
| M4 shaft | Linear elastic; two simple supports; no torsional vibration; first lateral mode only; K_t from records or fit equations, never a digitised chart. |
| M5 bearing | Constant speed per step; a_ISO is the manufacturer's value, not computed; P > 0.5 C warns. κ < 0.1 is a hard FAIL; κ > 4 is treated as 4. An axial load above e with `Y` = 0 is refused. |
| M6 weld group | Elastic, static, fillet welds of one leg size; rigid parts. |
| E4 conductor | Single circuit, one installation method per run; factors applied as a product and entered by you; derived C_a is a temperature-difference approximation; I²t and voltage drop are E5 and E3; a CSA below 0.5 mm² (AWG 21 and finer) cannot link to E5; no tables embedded. |
| E5 fault | TN systems; single earth fault; no motor contribution, arc fault or IT systems; no BS 7671 table embedded; when linked from E4 the device must match. |
| E6 load budget | No harmonic or unbalance assessment; inrush only for the single largest start. |
| S4 connection | Static; categories A and D only; no slip-resistant joints, no moment end plates, no countersunk or long joints. |
| S5 combinations | One scalar effect by superposition; buildings only; no EQU set B/C split, bridges, fatigue or seismic. |
| S6 base plate | Static; uniaxial moment; unstiffened; simplified T-stub model (the web gets no credit in the moment check); `m` and `l_eff` are yours to enter (one `l_eff` for modes 1 and 2); prying is your switch, not computed; anchor shear assumes standard clearance holes and equal sharing; β_j = 2/3 depends on grout conditions; the CHS compression side is half the annulus (approximate); anchorage, pull-out, breakout, edge distances and tension–shear interaction are designed externally in PROFIS; no shear key, weld design or fatigue. |
| B4 room heat loss | Steady state, no gains, no thermal mass; homogeneous layers only (no bridged-layer method); ground-floor U is entered by you; one thermal-bridge allowance per area facing outside; a target applies to external built-up elements only; a warning if Φ_HL ≤ 0; no intermittency or reheat allowance except the DHDG user allowance. |
| B5 heat exchanger | Single phase; constant properties; no pressure drop. Crossflow is approximate and flagged. One side at constant temperature is the `const_side` choice of the C_r = 0 arrangement. |
| B6 sealed system | Closed LTHW below 100 °C; no pipe-loss or emitter-position corrections; Kell density at atmospheric pressure; the vessel excludes the water reserve and the safety-valve margin. |

### C.5 Decisions (Addendum B)

Source: `docs/decisions/v0.26.0-DECISIONS.md` (DB-01 to DB-12), ADR-0170 to ADR-0173 and the Addendum B section of `docs/build-log/addendum-B-orientation.md`. **Your call?** is *Yes* where a decision is yours, *Confirm* where it is recorded and wants your nod, *No* where it is information.

| ID | Item | Your call? |
|---|---|---|
| DB-01 | Addendum B was queued until Addendum A reached its gates. Its decisions carry the prefix DB-nn. Its "ADR-0165 (or next free number)" became ADR-0170. | No. |
| ADR-0170 | Fluid Power is the fifth calculation discipline and the sixth engineering domain. Its three kinds of object (Circuit, Actuator, Power unit) hold typed attributes and **no rating or design-code check**. Pneumatics (ISO 6358) is out of scope and has no placeholder. No pressure-vessel code check (PD 5500, EN 13445, ASME VIII) and no hydraulic-component rating check exists: F2 reports the bar·litre value against the PSSR Schedule 1 exception and does nothing more. Two kernels are shared, not copied: the gas law (B6, F2) and the tolerance-stack engine (M1, F3, W2). | Confirm. |
| DB-02, DB-03 | Hydraulic power unit chain (F1 → F2 → B1 → E1 → E4, later E3, E5, E9). F1's pressure feeds F2's highest working pressure. B1 takes F1's flow, density and line bore by link. The bore is a cell link, so a re-bored cylinder stales the pressure line. B1 runs at Reynolds number 2327 on Colebrook. It warns `transition`. Its fittings term is flagged as understated below 4000. Neither drop is fed back to F1. E4 is sized on the **candidate motor's nameplate power**. E1 1.4.0 gives `P_rated` from an optional `n_rated` in the `motor.rating` record kind. There is no running-speed fallback. **The example's 1460 rpm nameplate is invented.** F1 judges rod buckling at the stall thrust `p·A_b` with no efficiency and no back pressure. F2 warns when a linked gauge pressure meets the Absolute basis. | **Yes.** Is sizing the cable on the nameplate power right for your jobs, and is the invented nameplate fine as an example? |
| DB-04 | M9 keeps its four ports and adds six (`F_pin_pk`, `T_rms_in`, `n_in`, `P_mean`, `lift`, `F_spring_max`) so chains 4 and 5 link physically. **M9 → F1 is a kinematic proxy**: a cylinder drives at about constant speed, not at constant crank speed. A sweep that reaches a toggle or a slider limit is clipped and its peaks and ports are withheld, not reported unbounded. E9 models a soft starter in current limit and a drive as current sources, takes E5's `I_k3` with c = 1.1 as its source, and offers `I_rated` for E4. `I_start` is for coordination only. | **Yes.** Is the proxy acceptable? |
| DB-05 | Chains 7 to 10 are packs. Envelope moisture: the wall's layers are **typed twice** (B4 and B7), and B7 warns when its U-value departs from B4's by more than 2 %. Cold pipe: B1's coldest temperature and outside diameter, and B2's ambient, dew point and humidity, feed B9. Pipework supports: B8's anchor force becomes S2's first load row; B8's moment is not carried; anchorage stays Evidence. Electronics enclosure: E7's loss is a cell of E6's load table; E2 gives M8 and S9 the breather-limited pressure and force. | Confirm. |
| DB-06 | Chains 1, 4 and 5 after verification. Chain 1 adds E3, E5 and E9: E5's `c_k3` feeds E9's `c_src`; the pump starts **unloaded** (an invented 20 N·m unloaded torque; a test checks that a third of the direct-on-line peak covers it); E5's external loop Z_e of 0.05 Ω is invented (a close-coupled 400 kVA transformer); the dip is a **lower bound**, because only the transformer terminals are in it. Chain 4: M9 feeds M7's working travel and operating frequency by link; the gear ratio is 9.6; **M4 takes the cam's RMS torque as a stand-in** for a fully alternating torque, guarded by a test (n_f 9.00 against 8.97). Chain 5: M9 → F1 `v_req` → F2. | **Yes.** Are the stand-in and the invented values acceptable? |
| DB-07 | W1 and W2 share one hardened assembly (`Tempest.Calc.Import`, ADR-0171, ADR-0172): reads capped at 5 MB, a declared number format (no locale guessing), path-refusal rules, strict CSV with formula-safe export. A CAD expression is **never evaluated**. A scan table is read through your own named column profile. Statistics are never trimmed silently. A fitted normal is pushed into M1 with CadImport or ScanImport provenance, which the verifiers downgrade to Manual when they cannot verify it. Provenance records are capped at 20 and kept while any input cites them. | Confirm. |
| DB-08 | CI licence check (White W-B-01). `scripts/check-licences.py` reads every `packages.lock.json` offline and fails on a package that is outside MIT, BSD-2-Clause, BSD-3-Clause and Apache-2.0 and is not on the allow list; the CI Gate requires the new Licence Check job. It found one shipped package outside the set: `Svg.Custom` 2.0.0.8 (MS-PL), a transitive of `Svg.Skia`, in the Desktop since v0.23.0. v0.26.0 adds no package. It stays as a visible, flagged exception, not an approved waiver, and is listed in `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md`. | **Yes.** Approve MS-PL for `Svg.Custom`, or replace the SVG renderer. |
| DB-09 | F2 gas process (Green G-AB-01, High). `fp.accumulator` 1.3.0 sizes a held accumulator as charged slowly (isothermal) and discharged at n, so `V0 = ΔV·(p2/p0)/[(p2/p1)^(1/n) − 1]`. A new `process` input defaults to *By duty*: *Emergency stroke* takes the slow charge, *Energy storage* keeps the single exponent (either can be chosen), *Shock and pulsation* always uses one exponent. HPU pack F2 26.70 → 30.08 L; actuated-linkage F2 1.078 → 1.310 L; both still pass. `ka-adiabatic` is kept under *Same exponent both ways*; the new mixed-process case is Unconfirmed (U-30). Emergency-stroke sheets issued under 1.2.0 should be re-run. Version 1.3.1 adds the absolute-pressure block and the `bar a` and `bar g` labels. | **Yes.** Is the slow-charge default right for your accumulators? |
| DB-10 | Chains 2 and 3 are workflows, not pack templates (Green G-AB-02). Scan-to-seal and CAD-driven tolerance are proved by integration tests: import, diff, staleness, Check blocked and Issue refused while stale. They have no folder under `docs/worked-examples/packs/`. Chain 3's M8 leg takes beam length and thickness from the file and the undercut from the CAD-driven stack by link. | Confirm. |
| DB-11 | W2 within-subgroup σ is c4-corrected (Green G-AB-03). The pooled σ is divided by c4(d), d = Σ(nⱼ − 1) + 1, shown on the output and in the CSV (`within_c4`). Cp and Cpk fall about 3 % at ten parts and less than 1 % above about 30 degrees of freedom. With no subgrouping nothing is corrected. This reverses the ADR-0172 choice (amendment noted). | Confirm. |
| DB-12 | Colour-team Addendum B items accepted, not fixed in v0.26.0. §B6 ranges stay soft (they warn) outside S7, S8, F1 and F2, which hard-fail as §B4 asks (B-B-06). Checks with no demand print "utilisation 0" (Y-B-02; reworded in v0.26.1 with B-A-14). "Was not entered; default used" notices for inputs a mode does not use, now including F2 `process` (Y-B-05; pruned in v0.26.1). One worked example per Addendum B calculator is enough (Y-B-08). S7 and F2 get no `IndependentCheck` (O-B-01). GR-B-04 and GR-B-05 (Info) stand as written. | **Yes.** Accept? |
| §B2 drop | The W2 PDF table path is **not built**, under the §B2 drop order. A table must be exported as CSV or `.xlsx`. | **Yes.** Is CSV and `.xlsx` enough? |
| ADR-0173 | S8 `struct.lifting` is behind the `lifting` feature flag, off by default. Only a machine administrator switches it on: the `HKLM` policy value `Lifting` (DWORD 1) on Windows, a root-owned `/etc/tempestos/features.json` elsewhere. Issue needs a competent-person attestation by a signed-in person who is none of the authors, and a second Checked signature. **Signatures are not authenticated.** The older lifting-lug module is gated by the same flag. The Evidence of every calculation now belongs to its worksheet. | **Yes.** Read section 4.6.2 before the flag is ever enabled. |
| D-37 | (carried from Addendum A) No table row editor and no table-cell link in the *Link* row. This now also limits the Addendum B tables: the B7 layers and climate, the B8 route, the E8 cables and route, the F1 hose list. It is why chain 7 is broken at B2 in T-C12 and why T-C6 makes 19 of the 20 links. | **Yes.** |

### C.6 Limitations by calculator (Addendum B spec pages)

| Calculator | Limitations |
|---|---|
| M7 spring | Linear characteristic. Cold-coiled round wire up to 20 mm. No conical, variable-pitch or rectangular-wire springs. Index outside 4 to 20, and fewer than 2 active coils, only warn. Extension hook fatigue and hook shape beyond two radii are not assessed. Surge is for both ends fixed only. R_m(d), the permissible stress fractions and the endurance strength are your records. The fatigue check is a Goodman line on your endurance strength. |
| M8 snap-fit | Linear beam theory with one secant modulus at the design strain. Nominal strain, without the root-fillet concentration. No creep, relaxation, moisture or impact-speed effect. Rigid mating part. Rectangular cantilever, a thin closed ring and a solid round torsion bar only. One Coulomb coefficient for both faces. A warning when l/h < 5. |
| M9 mechanism | Rigid, planar, no clearance, no friction, one degree of freedom, constant input speed. A translating cam follower only. A linear spring, whose surge M7 reports. No link inertia or gravity beyond the output element. A sweep that reaches a toggle or a slider limit is clipped and its peaks are withheld. A change-point linkage continues on the same branch. |
| E7 PCB trace | A curve-fit for steady current, drawn for 1 to 3 oz copper (the weight is entered in oz/ft², hard maximum 6, never in micrometres), up to 35 A, 10 to 100 K rise and 10 mm width. Conservative for multilayer boards. Not a thermal analysis (no planes, vias, neighbouring heat, convection or altitude). IPC-2152 is not implemented. |
| E8 containment | Circular cables by overall diameter. No packing rules, no cable or conduit factors (you enter the space factor). Straight lengths are horizontal. No inclines, vertical runs, rollers or pulleys. One μ for the route. Bends use the capstan equation only. Load shares equally between the cables on one pulling head. |
| E9 motor start | The source impedance should be the one at the minimum fault level. A passive start is a constant impedance at the locked-rotor instant, which is the worst case. Low-voltage three-phase, balanced. The star-to-delta changeover spike is not modelled. Capacitor switching transients and detuning are not assessed. The dip limit is yours. |
| S7 LTB | Doubly-symmetric uniform members between fork supports. No mono-symmetric or tapered members, cantilevers or intermediate restraint. No class 4 effective properties, no shear check and no 6.2.9 check. One moment diagram about y-y. The edition switch changes the printed label only. |
| S8 lifting lug | Nominal-stress screening of four sections on a plate loaded through a pin. No stress concentration. No fatigue (at most 16 000 cycles, a hard limit). No buckling of the lug. No weld, bolt or beam check (the linked sheets make them). No pin, shackle or sling rating. Equal sharing between the lugs, with three sharing when there are four. One pin diameter; a round-ended or straight-ended lug. The 6° tilt is a pitch case and a roll case taken one at a time. One allowable stress for every BTH-1 section. The notch-stress route of EN 13001-3-1 is cited, not implemented. The design factor, service factor, divisors, bearing multiple, dishing limit and proof-load factor are your records. Signatures are not authenticated. |
| S9 plate | Thin plates (span/t ≥ 10), small deflections (w/t ≤ 0.5), isotropic, constant thickness. One support type all round. Centred loads only. No elastic edges, no local effects under concentrated loads, no temperature or in-plane loads. |
| B7 Glaser | Steady state in each month on a monthly mean climate. No air movement, liquid transport, moisture storage, rain or solar gain. λ and μ constant. Condensation only at the interfaces between layers. One internal temperature. Not for timber or breathable build-ups (EN 15026) and not a mould-growth assessment. |
| B8 pipe expansion | Screening only. A guided-cantilever beam model with rigid elbows. The pipe modulus is entered in GPa (usual range 0.05 to 500; a smaller value is refused, DC-27). **No stress-intensification factor**, so the stress at the elbow root can be higher than the beam stress. No pressure or ovalisation effects, no out-of-plane legs, no branches, no support friction, weight, hangers or cold spring. A Z-bend lies in one plane. `Sa` and `K1` are your records. |
| B9 insulation | Steady state, one layer of constant λ. No supports, joints, bridges, air gaps or jacket resistance. Condensation is judged on the outer surface only (B7 handles a wall). The correlation is indicative. The ISO 12241 surface coefficients are not implemented. |
| F1 cylinder | Steady motion only: no acceleration, cushioning, compressibility or line losses (B1 gives the lines). Efficiencies are yours. A fixed-displacement pump. The rod is a solid round column. **ISO/TS 13725 is not implemented.** The orifice is the sharp-edged turbulent relation with your C_d. |
| F2 accumulator | Ideal gas unless you give `f_rg`. One gas pre-charge. A held accumulator (*Emergency stroke*) is charged slowly and discharged at n; the other duties use one exponent both ways. No gas-side heat transfer, friction, oil compressibility or leakage. The shock duty is a screening of gas volume. The size list and the pre-charge rules are yours. PSSR applicability is for the competent person. |
| F3 O-ring gland | A plain rectangular groove with a corner radius and a circular ring. Edge breaks, wall angles, surface finish, eccentricity, pressure, friction, compression set and stress relaxation are not modelled. The worst case is a stack of independent ends. The extrusion limit comes from your record. Not a seal maker's design review. |
| W1 CAD import | Reads parameters, not geometry or assemblies. A mapping fills inputs and cells of rows that already exist. Real exports vary by version and locale. One configuration of a design table per import. |
| W2 scan import | Normal fits only. No production order, so the within-σ needs a stated subgroup size. A mapping fills existing stack rows (a measured-dimension row may be added). Deviations need upper ≥ 0 ≥ lower. **No PDF path.** Header spellings are unconfirmed. |
| W3 materials | The four seeds are generic and indicative. Temperature tables are read linearly and never extrapolated. No form panel of its own. |

| RR-B-02 | Desktop Cancel abandons the preview task. The abandoned parse sees the same token on its next chunk, and one read is capped at a 64 KB start tag, so it stops within milliseconds. Apply runs with no token, bounded by a 10 s budget. | Low, Low | Confirm. |
| RR-B-03 | The start-tag scanner works on bytes. A UTF-16 part whose text holds a character with a 0x3C byte, followed by more than 64 KB without a 0x3C or 0x3E byte, is refused. Cell text is capped at 8 KB, and Excel and LibreOffice write UTF-8. The failure is a loud refusal, never a wrong value. | Very low, Low | Confirm. |
| RR-B-04 | `IsLicensedInAnyRevisionAsync` reads every revision of a record for each pinned input on export or sheet build. The cost is linear in the revisions and fine at desktop scale. | Low, Low | No. |
| RR-B-05 | A comma-delimited W1 CSV under a declared decimal comma must quote every number (ADR-0171). Honest unquoted files of that shape are refused with instructions. A decimal-point file with a numeric description after a whole number is refused or blocked. Both are loud and never read a wrong value. | Low, Low (usability) | Confirm. |

### C.8 Owner steps

These are yours. None changes code.

1. Run this pack on Windows, in the order of its parts. It is the first real-shell run of this build.
2. Delete the superseded remote branch `v0.26.0/addA-7b-chains`.
3. Delete the local `v0.26.0-rc1` tag. It was never pushed (DA-01). Run `git tag -d v0.26.0-rc1` in the clone that holds it.
4. `Svg.Custom` 2.0.0.8, licence MS-PL (DB-08): decided (DC-21) and done; the SVG renderer is the app's own code and the package no longer ships.
5. Record the SKF catalogue terms for the 6206 ratings in the M5 worked example (DA-25).
6. Check DA-24 (1) against your copy of BS EN 12828: the B6 vessel excludes the water reserve and the safety-valve margin.
7. Check the clause and regulation numbers that were quoted from memory: BS 7671 regulation 522.8 (E8); the LOLER regulation numbers 7 and 9 and the PUWER regulation numbers 4 and 5 (S8; the sheet, its spec page and section 4.6.2); ISO 12241 clause numbers (B9); the EN 13155 clause numbers (S8, which the sheet marks "clause to be confirmed"); and the other citations in section A.9.
8. M8: the spec page prints 0.67 where the sheet uses the derived 2/3. Decide which is right and make the page and the sheet agree (section A.8).
9. S9: confirm the aluminium partial factor γ_M1 = 1.1 (EN 1999-1-1) that the worked example enters in the `gamma_M0` slot (section A.9).
10. Verify the 30 Unconfirmed cases (Appendix A) and the published examples that nobody pinned (section A.8) against your own copies.
11. S8: do the section 4.6.2 review, settle how your procedure authenticates signatures (row E3), then decide on which machines, if any, the flag goes on.
12. RR-B14 and RR-B17 are fixed in `8e5f1190`. Confirm in T-AUTO (Calculators 1693 pass) and in T-S8 steps C9a and C15.

### C.9 Addendum C: open items

The Addendum C items that wait for you are in section 8.8 of the sign-off sheet. They are also in the handover (`docs/handover/v0.26.0-handover.md`). They are DC-03 to DC-15 and the review items SEC-C-06, GR-C-01, GR-C-03 and W-C-02.

## Appendix D — Behaviour changes since v0.25.x

### D.1 Behaviour changes

From `CHANGELOG.md` (v0.26.0, including Addendum A), the ADRs and the decisions log. The block of Addendum A rows ends at the legacy calculators; the Addendum B rows follow it.

| Change | Where | What you see | Check |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Bolt shear scope (ADR-0169)** | Older module `calc.bolt-shear-capacity` | The module is now labelled as the shank-in-plane case of EN 1993-1-8 Table 3.4 (gross area, α_v 0.6 for every class). Classes 4.8, 5.8 and 6.8 are added. The 0.5-class entries say "shank in shear plane". Threads in the shear plane go to the S4 worksheet. **Values for existing classes are unchanged.** | T-BC1 and T-S4 |
| New **Worksheets** node and shared worksheet form | Engineering → Modules → Calculations | Live recalculation, unit pickers, source per input, governing bar, Show working, read-only issued sheets | T-00 |
| Issue in one transaction | Core Evidence | The issue sheet bytes, attachment, issue record and status commit together. The Workspace issue command uses it. | T-00 steps 6 and 7 |
| Concurrent-issue and concurrent-check guards (SEC-05, D-33) | Core Evidence path | Check and Issue commit with the instance in one transaction, with a status re-check. Optimistic concurrency on instance writes. | Automated (`Red_ConcurrencyAndTamper`) |
| Check demands sources (ADR-0166) | All worksheets | A check or issue is refused until every input has a source other than *manual*. A rejected check stays on the instance. A defaulted input raises a warning. | T-00 step 5; T-L1 |
| PDF font subsetting | **Every** generated document, not only calc sheets | Body and brand faces are embedded as glyph subsets. The calc sheet fell from 222 KB to 162 KB. Windows PDFs were 650 KB before D-25. | Open an issued invoice or report PDF and compare its look and size with v0.25.x |
| Sheet text | All worksheets | Utilisation checks read `u = 0.375 vs u ≤ 1`; valid ranges follow the entered unit; repeated "not taken from a reference record" notices print as one line | Any script |
| Stale blocks Check, Issue and pack issue (ADR-0168) | All linked calculations | Banner and **Update linked inputs** / **Update to revision n**; a stale checked calculation can be revised; a checked pack can be reopened | T-L1, T-C1 to T-C5 |
| Saved calculations list and Calculation packs panel | Worksheet form | New left-hand panels: reopen a saved calculation, create a pack from a template, check, reopen and issue it | T-00, T-C1 to T-C5 |
| Records and licence lock (DA-05, DA-06, DA-23) | Worksheet form, CSV, sheets | New *Reference records* panel; licensed values withheld from CSV export; a withheld value is blocked on re-import; issued sheets withhold licensed table rows | T-REC |
| Independent check per template (DA-12) | E4, E5, S6 | The creator and anyone who set or changed an input cannot check; a link re-pin is not an edit. Where no second person is available, the preparer may self-check with a recorded statement (DC-21); the issued sheet prints `Self-checked by the preparer: …` | T-IND |
| **New sheets open on declared defaults only** (DA-23) | Every worksheet | Tables are empty; values with no default are blank; thirteen templates open blocked with a message that says what to enter. A shaft opens on 7850 kg/m³; the wet-heating pump motor on 6000 rpm. | Step 2 of every per-calculator script |
| CSV carries tables | Export and import | Tables export as `name`, `name[r]`, `name[r].column` rows with units and sources; a blank cell is no cell; a gap in the row numbers blocks; yes, no, true, false, 1, 0 are accepted for yes/no cells | T-00 step 4; Part 7 spot checks |
| E1 `duty_mode` and `P_rms` (DA-21) | `elec.motor-sizing` (1.2.0 added them; now 1.4.0) | Choice of point-to-point or continuous rotary duty with a run-up time; new port `P_rms`. The wet-heating pump motor uses continuous duty. | T-C3 |
| E4 carries its device to E5 (`device_code`) | `elec.conductor-sizing`, `elec.fault-protection` | E5 is blocked if its device differs from the one E4 sized | T-C2 part C |
| B5 `const_side` | `bs.heat-exchanger` 1.2.0 | Which side is at constant temperature for the C_r = 0 arrangement; new ports `dT_c`, `T_c_m`, `cp_c` | T-B5 |
| B4 `flow` column; target for external elements only | `bs.room-heat-loss` (1.1.0 added them; now 1.2.0) | The direction of heat flow (horizontal, upward, downward) sets the surface resistance; `U_target` applies to external built-up elements; a heated neighbour's partition is not held to the outside target | T-B4 second case |
| B1 `circuit` option; heat-load flow | `bs.pipe-pump` (1.2.0 added them; now 1.5.0) | A closed flow-and-return loop has no static lift; the design flow can come from a heat load | T-C3, T-C4 |
| S6 needs `l_eff` and `m`; anchorage needs an attached file | `struct.base-plate` 1.1.1 | The sheet is blocked until you enter both lengths; the anchorage condition passes only with a report attached to the calculation's Evidence | T-S6 |
| M5 refuses a zero `Y` with an axial load above e | `mech.bearing-life` 1.1.0 | `Blocked — Y: F_a/F_r = … exceeds e_lim = …, but Y = 0, …` | T-M5 |
| B6 vessel states what it omits | `bs.sealed-system` 1.2.0 | The sheet says the vessel excludes the water reserve and the safety-valve margin and cites CIBSE B1 only | T-B6; DA-24 |
| Template versions rose | Seven templates | A draft saved on an older version no longer runs (DA-24) | None expected |
| Architecture test amended (D-12) | Tests | Desktop may reference `Tempest.Calc.*` beside Workspace | `DependencyDirectionTests` in the Core suite |
| No schema bump (D-26) | Persistence | A v0.25 database opens unchanged | Open a data folder from v0.25.x with this build, after copying it |
| Legacy calculators (D-38, ADR-0167) | Calculations → Calculators | Retired in the merged line: replaced by worksheet templates; stored runs are archive worksheets (read-only). | See the note at the top of this pack |
| **Addendum B starts here.** Fluid Power discipline (ADR-0170) | Engineering tree, a project's Structure tab, Worksheets tree, Ctrl+K, dashboard export | A new discipline, **Fluid Power**, with Circuits, Actuators and Power units. Attributes are recorded as typed with no rating check. The dashboard export's `disciplines` array gains a `fluid-power` entry (schema v3, additive). A project opened on this build shows an empty Fluid Power discipline. | T-FPD |
| Fifteen new worksheets | Worksheets tree | M7, M8, M9, E7, E8, E9, S7, S9, B7, B8, B9, F1, F2 and F3 are in the catalogue, which holds 42 templates. S8 is the fifteenth. It is withheld unless the `lifting` feature is on; then the catalogue holds 43. | T-M7 to T-F3, T-S8 |
| Material library (W3) | **Reference records** panel | Four **indicative** seed materials in the picker for any template that maps an input to a material property. Applying one fills the inputs in their own units, cites the material and pins its revision. A later revision, or an edited value, makes the calculation stale. One licensed value makes the whole material licensed. | T-W3 |
| Licensed values stay withheld through links | Inputs export, issued sheet | A value linked from a calculation that holds an input pinned to a licensed record is withheld from the export. The issued sheet prints the link and "value derived from a licensed record — withheld". | Automated (`LicensedLinkTests`) |
| CAD parameter import (W1) | **Import CAD parameters…** on a saved draft | Reads NX, Creo, SolidWorks, Onshape and generic files into inputs through your mapping profile. Never evaluates an expression. Each imported input shows a CAD-import source. | T-W1, T-C8 |
| Scan deviation import (W2) | **Import scan deviations…** on a saved draft | Computes mean, σ, Cp, Cpk, Pp, Ppk and an Anderson–Darling check through your column profile. Pushes a fitted normal into M1, F3 or M2. No PDF path. | T-W2, T-C7 |
| M1 2.1.0: `sigma` column | `mech.tolerance-stack` | A new optional standard-deviation column in the contributors table. W2 fills it. A stated σ on a non-normal contributor is ignored, with a warning. The numbers of a sheet without σ do not change. | T-REF, T-W2 |
| Shared kernels | B6 and M1 | The gas law (B6, F2) and the stack engine (M1, F3, W2) are shared. B6's sheet, numbers and golden are unchanged. | T-REF |
| B4 1.2.0: port `U_1` | `bs.room-heat-loss` | A new output port, the first element's U-value, for the B7 cross-check. No number changes. | T-C12 |
| B2 1.1.0: port `phi_k` | `bs.psychrometrics` | A new output, relative humidity as a percentage quantity, so that it can link into a percentage input. | T-C12, T-C13 |
| B1 1.5.0 | `bs.pipe-pump` | A warning when fitting losses are used below Reynolds number 4000; an optional `OD` column of the segments table and ports `OD_1` and `T_line` (chain 8). | T-C13 |
| E1 1.4.0 | `elec.motor-sizing` | An optional `n_rated` and a port `P_rated` (the nameplate shaft power, which E4 sizes the cable on). A warning when `n_rated` is missing in continuous duty, and when it is entered where it is ignored. The pack refuses and names `n_rated` if it is missing. | T-C6, T-C9 |
| E2 1.4.0 | `elec.enclosure-heat` | Sealed-enclosure inputs (`T_seal`, `A_cover`, `dp_relief`) and ports `dp_seal` and `F_seal`, limited to the breather's cracking pressure. | T-C15 |
| E5 1.2.0: port `c_k3` | `elec.fault-protection` | A new output, the voltage factor behind `I_k3`, which E9 takes so that the motor start uses the same factor. No number changes. | T-C6 |
| Template versions rose again | Seven older templates and three Addendum B templates | M1, B1, B2, B4, E1, E2 and E5 rose as the rows above say. The §B9 review fixes raised `fp.accumulator` to 1.3.1, `elec.pcb-trace` to 1.0.2 and `bs.pipe-expansion` to 1.2.1. A draft saved on an older version no longer runs, but the worksheet now opens it as a new sheet on the current version (DA-24). No tag was ever pushed, so none is expected. | T-F2, T-E7, T-B8 |
| Hydraulic power unit and six more packs | Calculation packs panel | Seven new pack templates, so the form lists twelve. The hydraulic power unit cannot be created without your cable tables (like the motor circuit). | T-C6, T-C9, T-C10, T-C12 to T-C15 |
| New suites | Tests | Projects `Tempest.Calc.Tests.FluidPower` and `Tempest.Calc.Tests.Import`. | T-AUTO |
| S8 behind a flag (ADR-0173) | Catalogue, Worksheets tree, Ctrl+K, Engineering Calculators, services, packs | Off by default. Only a machine administrator switches it on: the `HKLM\SOFTWARE\Policies\TempestOS\Features` DWORD `Lifting` = 1 on Windows, `"Lifting": true` in the root-owned `/etc/tempestos/features.json` elsewhere. `appsettings.json`, the environment, the command line, the current directory and `HKCU` are ignored. With the flag off, S8 and the older lifting-lug module are absent, and every create, check, issue, revise, link, pack and engine path refuses them with one sentence. | T-S8 parts A and D |
| S8 sign-off and managed Evidence | Worksheet form, Evidence | A new **Sign-off** panel for a template that demands it: **Attest** on a draft, **Countersign** on a checked calculation, **Countersign pack** on a checked pack. Check is refused without an attestation by a signed-in person who is none of the authors. Issue is refused without a second check by a third person. A later input change voids the attestation, and Revise drops it. The Evidence record of **every** calculation now belongs to its worksheet: the generic Evidence Check, Issue and Revise refuse it, and the Evidence editor offers **Open worksheet**. | T-S8 part C |
| Issued-sheet footer | Issued sheets and PDFs | A gated calculator's issued sheet ends with `Optional feature: lifting feature enabled (<source>)`. Ordinary templates print nothing extra. | T-S8 step C15 |
| F2 1.3.1: gas process and pressure labels | `fp.accumulator` | A new input `process` (default *By duty*): an emergency stroke is sized as a held accumulator, `V0 = ΔV·(p2/p0)/[(p2/p1)^(1/n) − 1]`. HPU pack accumulator 26.70 → 30.08 L; actuated linkage 1.078 → 1.310 L. An absolute pressure below the atmosphere is blocked. Every pressure prints `bar a` or `bar g`. | T-F2, T-C6, T-C10 |
| E7 1.0.2: copper weight | `elec.pcb-trace` | The hard maximum of `Cu_oz` is 6 oz/ft². An 18 typed as micrometres is refused. | T-E7 step 12 |
| B8 1.2.1: pipe modulus | `bs.pipe-expansion` | The usual range of `E` is 0.05 to 500 GPa (0.5 before 1.5.2, DC-27). Below 0.05 GPa the sheet is refused (`modulus-low`, a block since 1.4.0; it warned before). The hard minimum is 0.01 GPa. | T-B8 part B step 10 |
| S7 double curvature | `struct.ltb-interaction` 1.1.0 | End moments of opposite sign warn `double-curvature` and print the C₁ and C_my used. No version change. | T-S7 step 13 |
| B9 condensation warning | `bs.insulation` 1.1.0 | A fixed `h_se` on a cold surface is judged against the correlation at the emissivity on the sheet. `h-se-condensation` warns when the correlation asks for more. No version change. | T-B9 part A step 11 |
| M9 toggle output | `mech.mechanism` 1.2.0 | A sweep that reaches a toggle prints `u_mu` as text with a dash, and the check fails as a condition. It no longer prints `1000000000`. | T-M9 part A step 11 |
| W2 c4-corrected σ | Scan import | The within-subgroup σ is divided by c4(d). Cp and Cpk are about 3 % lower at ten parts with subgroups. With subgroup size 1 nothing changes. | T-W2 step 11 |
| W1 CSV under a decimal comma | CAD import | A comma-delimited CSV that declares a decimal comma refuses every unquoted number. | T-W1 |
| Import logging | Desktop | CAD and scan imports log one line per step, with no value from the file. | T-W1 step 15 |
| Old-draft upgrade | Worksheet form | A draft on an older template version opens as a new unsaved sheet named `<name> (<version>)`, with a message. The old draft stays unchanged. | automated (`Grey_GR_B_01`) |
| Drive-train example speed | Pack worked example | The drum turns at 118 rpm so the motor runs at 1475 rpm, under its 1480 rpm nameplate. | T-C1 |
| E1 `speed-above-rated` | `elec.motor-sizing` 1.4.0 | Continuous duty warns when the motor speed is above `n_rated`. | T-C1 speed check |
| M7 `s-work-unused` | `mech.spring` 1.2.0 | An expected travel `s_work` in torsion mode warns that it is not used. | T-M7 |
| Licence lock per record | Records, export, issued sheet | An input pinned to a record is withheld if any revision of the record is licensed (SEC-B-02). | T-REC |
| Sheet wording | Issued sheets | No sheet prints an internal document reference such as "Addendum B §B…" (Y-B-04). S8's assumption A5 now cites "Owner's specification (6° tilt of a horizontal beam); EN 13155." | T-AUTO; T-S8 step C15 |

### E.3 Completeness check

The earlier pack held 935 numbered step rows. A script compared each row with this file after the references were renamed. 853 are present unchanged. The other 82 are listed by old section in the table below. Each one belongs to a section that is replaced, not lost. Those sections are the status tables, the old order table and the old spot-check table.

| Old section | Rows | Why they are not here |
|---|---|---|
| 2.1 Addendum A calculators (12) | 12 | Status table replaced by the facts table of each calculator and the Verification dashboard. |
| 2.2 v0.26.0 base calculators (12 items, 16 templates) | 16 | Status table replaced as above. |
| 2.4 Addendum B calculators (15 items, 15 templates built) | 15 | Status table replaced as above. |
| T-AUTO — Automated proofs for Addendum B | 8 | Kept as rows AU1 to AU8 in section 1.3.1. The counts moved to the runbook. |
| 9. Recommended test order | 31 | The order table is replaced by the order of the parts. The spot checks X1 to X13 are Part 7 steps (table above). |
